# Legislation and regulation: progress report, 2025-09-01 to 2026-09-06

*54 countries. Each row below is carried verbatim from that country's own progress report, which answers a fixed frame of indicators over the period; nothing is written here. A country not listed under an indicator has ***No evidence*** on it in its own report.*

**Progress values.** *Advanced* — a system entered service, a stage was completed or an instrument was made. *Stalled* — a stated target passed without delivery. *Regressed* — an instrument was withdrawn or neutralised, or a reported position worsened. *Mixed* — the indicator's instruments moved in different directions in the period; the clause after the comma names which moved which way. *No change* — the base holds a standing position and nothing in the period touched it. ***No evidence*** — the base holds nothing on this indicator at all. A value may carry a qualifying clause after a comma, as in *Advanced, regulations still pending*.

*The place reports do not share one window; the period above is the range they span.*

## Data protection legislation

| Country | Developments | Progress |
|---|---|---|
| Algeria | [Law 18-07 stands as amended, its officer and breach-notification duties added by the 2025 amendment](https://digitalpolicyalert.org/blog/data-protection-in-africa-roundup). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The law was [enacted in 2018 and in force from August 2023](https://www.trade.gov/country-commercial-guides/algeria-digital-economy), and the [2025 amendment added data-protection-officer and breach-notification duties](https://digitalpolicyalert.org/blog/data-protection-in-africa-roundup).<br><br>Nothing amended it further in the window. What moved is enforcement practice rather than the statute: the authority ran a compliance push on officers, processing registers and audit logs, and issued a deliberation treating images as personal data - both under a law whose own text the base does not hold.</details> | No change |
| Angola | The 2011 law [stands unamended](https://lex.ao/docs/assembleia-nacional/2011/lei-n-o-22-11-de-17-de-junho/); [a revision closed consultation](https://angop.ao/noticias/economia/nova-lei-de-proteccao-de-dados-pode-vigorar-no-proximo-ano-legislativo/) while [an amendment opening electoral data was rejected](https://angop.ao/noticias/politica/parlamento-rejeita-proposta-sobre-proteccao-de-dados-pessoais/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The standing instrument [has been in force since 17 June 2011, with consent and notification duties, sensitive-data limits and transfer approvals](https://lex.ao/docs/assembleia-nacional/2011/lei-n-o-22-11-de-17-de-junho/). Three of its provisions matter more than its fines: article 35(6) substitutes a statute or decree for the regulator's authorisation, article 24 does the same for database interconnection, and article 4(2)(a) puts processing under state-secrecy, state-security and judicial-secrecy rules outside the law altogether.<br><br>2026-06-17 — public consultation [closed on a revision adding a data-protection officer duty for high-volume processors, stronger oversight and sanction mechanisms, and fines redenominated into local currency](https://angop.ao/noticias/economia/nova-lei-de-proteccao-de-dados-pode-vigorar-no-proximo-ano-legislativo/), due at the Council of Ministers and then the National Assembly. No draft text is held.<br><br>2026-06-25 — parliament [rejected in plenary an amendment that would have opened the citizen databases and the electoral registers to parties and citizens](https://angop.ao/noticias/politica/parlamento-rejeita-proposta-sobre-proteccao-de-dados-pessoais/), after it [had cleared committee in April](https://angop.ao/noticias/politica/assembleia-nacional-aprova-na-generalidade-alteracao-a-lei-de-proteccao-de-dados-pessoais/). The registers stay closed.</details> | Mixed, a revision consultation closed while an amendment opening electoral data was rejected |
| Botswana | The 2024 Data Protection Act [has been in force since January 2025](https://www.techhiveadvisory.africa/insights/understanding-botswanas-new-data-protection-act-key-updates-and-changes), repealing a 2018 Act that never commenced; what moved in the window is enforcement. <details><summary>Full record</summary>[The Act came into force on 14 January 2025, repealing a 2018 statute that was never commenced](https://www.techhiveadvisory.africa/insights/understanding-botswanas-new-data-protection-act-key-updates-and-changes). It did not change in the window.<br><br>What changed is that it began to bite. [The commission that had three contract officers, no premises, no published address and no website in July 2025](https://dailynews.gov.bw/news-detail/87795) [issued a determination and enforcement notice against a government directorate in July 2026](https://na.co.bw/data-commission-orders-suspension-of-dpsm-epayslip-portal-over-privacy-law-breaches/), reported under the authority below.<br><br>The one amendment in prospect is the localisation duty reported under policy, which has no draft. No implementing regulations, codes of practice or approved-country list appear on this ledger.</details> | No change |
| Burkina Faso | A new data protection law [was voted on 3 August 2026](https://www.sidwaya.info/surete-aerienne-et-protection-des-donnees-a-caractere-personnel-lassemblee-legislative-du-peuple-adopte-les-projets-de-lois/) awaiting promulgation, after the 2021 statute [ran five years without its enforcement decrees](https://www.sidwaya.info/journee-nationale-de-protection-des-donnees-a-caractere-personnel-la-cil-sollicite-laccompagnement-du-premier-ministre/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-08-03 — [the legislative assembly voted a new law prohibiting and sanctioning processing without consent, indexing administrative fines to turnover and scaling them by gravity, prohibiting transfer of sensitive data abroad, requiring prior consent for commercial prospecting, and leaving private video-surveillance lawful but framed where it faces the public highway](https://www.sidwaya.info/surete-aerienne-et-protection-des-donnees-a-caractere-personnel-lassemblee-legislative-du-peuple-adopte-les-projets-de-lois/), [reported the same day](https://assembleenationale.bf/830). No text, number or gazette reference is held and it must clear conformity review before promulgation.<br><br>Until then [the 2021 law remains the operative text](https://burkina24.com/2025/02/11/droits-numeriques-au-burkina-faso-le-centre-national-de-presse-norbert-zongo-presente-son-rapport-2024/), and it ran its whole life without teeth: [the commission was still pleading with the Prime Minister for the implementing decrees in March 2025](https://www.sidwaya.info/journee-nationale-de-protection-des-donnees-a-caractere-personnel-la-cil-sollicite-laccompagnement-du-premier-ministre/), and they were never issued in the five years the law ran.<br><br>The lesson the new law has to answer is its predecessor's: a statute with sanctions and no implementing machinery is not an enforcement regime, and publication of the decrees, or their express abrogation, would settle it.</details> | Advanced |
| Burundi | A personal data protection law [was unanimously adopted on 15 January 2026](https://techafricanews.com/2026/01/16/burundi-adopts-landmark-personal-data-protection-law-to-safeguard-citizens-in-the-digital-era/): eight chapters, an independent authority and criminal penalties, where no statute existed. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-01-15 — the National Assembly [unanimously adopted a personal data protection law with eight chapters, an independent supervisory authority and criminal penalties, aligned to the African Union convention](https://techafricanews.com/2026/01/16/burundi-adopts-landmark-personal-data-protection-law-to-safeguard-citizens-in-the-digital-era/), [reported the following day as the country's first such statute](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/tech/burundi-lawmakers-approve-data-protection-framework-as-digitalisation-accelerates). No statute existed before it.<br><br>The sequencing is stated by the government itself: legislation came first in order to enable the biometric identity card, which is reported under digital identity and is technically ready but waiting on its own instrument. The authority the law creates is not on record as operating, reported below.</details> | Advanced |
| Cameroon | [Loi n. 2024/017 on personal data protection is in force and unamended](https://prc.cm/fr/multimedia/documents/10258-loi-n-2024-017-du-23-12-2024-web), with no supervisory authority operating. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The law was [promulgated on 23 December 2024 and is in force, with no supervisory authority operating](https://prc.cm/fr/multimedia/documents/10258-loi-n-2024-017-du-23-12-2024-web), a position [restated to the National Assembly in April 2025](https://leconomie.info/minette-libom-li-likeng-cest-en-construisant-un-numerique-au-service-de-tous-que-nous-poserons-les-fondations-dun-developpement-inclusif/).<br><br>Nothing in the window amended it. The promulgated text was acquired in July 2026 and is held as a 24-page page-image scan of which pages 15 to 24 are unread, so what the statute obliges beyond its first half is not established from anything on file.</details> | No change |
| Cape Verde | [Lei 133/V/2001 is unamended in the window](https://expressodasilhas.cv/pais/2025/02/02/a-ia-ou-o-big-data-nao-podem-por-em-causa-os-direitos-das-pessoas/95448), last changed in 2021 to require designation of data-protection officers. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [general regime closely mirrors the European standard, with administrative fines of CVE 50,000 to 3,000,000, cessation, blocking and deletion measures and criminal penalties up to two years, amended in 2013 to create the commission and in 2021 to require designation of data-protection officers](https://www.ibanet.org/document?id=Digital-Regulations-in-the-Metaverse-Era-Cabo-Verde).<br><br>No amendment is recorded in the window. A [named analyst argued in April 2025 for aligning it with artificial intelligence and adding mechanisms for algorithmic supervision](https://expressodasilhas.cv/opiniao/2025/04/07/revolucao-tecnologica/96431); no bill is held. The [commission's president said in February 2025 that several institutions had not complied with the officer obligation](https://expressodasilhas.cv/pais/2025/02/02/a-ia-ou-o-big-data-nao-podem-por-em-causa-os-direitos-das-pessoas/95448).</details> | No change |
| Central African Republic | The data protection law [has been in force since January 2024 with no implementing decree](https://dataprotection.africa/central-african-republic), and none appeared in the window. <details><summary>Full record</summary>[The law was promulgated in January 2024 as a comprehensive regime with no supervisory authority and no implementing decree](https://blog.africadataprotection.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/07/Loi_24_001_portant_protection_des_donnes_a_caractere__personnel.pdf), and [that remains the position](https://dataprotection.africa/central-african-republic). The held text is a scan with a poor character layer, so its wording is legible and individual characters are not reliable.<br><br>Nothing moved it in the twelve months to 2026-08-26: no decree, no amendment and no first application. The authority the law creates is reported below and does not exist either, so a comprehensive statute has been in force for nineteen months with neither the rules nor the body needed to apply it.</details> | No change |
| Chad | No [general data-protection law or supervisory authority](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news-digital/1603-53795-chad-approves-reform-strengthening-national-cybersecurity-agency) exists, the function having been folded into the security agency, while a [law on biometric data in judicial procedures](https://tchadinfos.com/2026/04/11/tchad-les-deputes-adoptent-a-une-large-majorite-la-loi-encadrant-les-donnees-biometriques-judiciaires/) was adopted by 138 votes to 1. <details><summary>Full record</summary>There is no enacted text, number or gazette reference for a general data-protection law, and the function was folded into the security agency in March 2026 ([vacuum](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news-digital/1603-53795-chad-approves-reform-strengthening-national-cybersecurity-agency), [finalisation](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news-digital/1012-51293-chad-finalizes-digital-law-to-align-national-framework-with-global-standards)).<br><br>The biometric-data law was adopted at a plenary in April 2026. Its gazetted text is not held, so retention periods, destruction procedure, oversight body and any requirement of judicial authorisation are unknown, and deputies raised concerns in debate about inappropriate use ([law](https://tchadinfos.com/2026/04/11/tchad-les-deputes-adoptent-a-une-large-majorite-la-loi-encadrant-les-donnees-biometriques-judiciaires/)).<br><br>Legislating the collection of biometric data for judicial use before legislating data protection at all is the sequence, and it is the reverse of the order every other unit in this frame attempts.</details> | Mixed, a biometric-data law adopted by 138 votes to 1 while no general data-protection statute or authority exists at all |
| Comoros | The [2021 data protection law is in force, aligned to the European regulation, with the national authority still not constituted](https://www.techhiveadvisory.africa/insights/review-of-the-comoros-data-protection-law). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The law was [enacted on 29 June 2021, is extraterritorial, requires seventy-two-hour breach notification and a mandatory data-protection officer and carries full data-subject rights, with the national authority not constituted](https://www.techhiveadvisory.africa/insights/review-of-the-comoros-data-protection-law).<br><br>Nothing moved. The authority's non-existence was [restated in a procurement notice of 12 March 2026](https://www.isdb.org/project-procurement/fr/appels-doffres/2026/gpn/projet-de-modernisation-de-ladministration-publique-comorienne-par-le-bais). The statute is held only through an analyst review and is cited as analysis; its own text is not held, so what a Comorian controller must actually do is not checkable from anything on file.</details> | No change |
| Congo | [Loi n. 29-2019 remains in force, its institutional framework completed by a decree of 31 December 2025](https://techafricanews.com/2026/01/22/congo-launches-commission-to-strengthen-personal-data-protection-and-digital-governance/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The law was [promulgated on 10 October 2019 and stood in force at the window's opening with an incomplete institutional framework and no supervisory authority constituted; a decree of 31 December 2025 completed that framework](https://techafricanews.com/2026/01/22/congo-launches-commission-to-strengthen-personal-data-protection-and-digital-governance/).<br><br>The statute itself did not move. Its text is not held, and its number and date reach the base only through a secondary outlet's account of the commission's first session, so what it obliges a controller to do is not checkable from anything on file - including whether registration or notification is required at all.</details> | No change |
| Cote d'Ivoire | [Loi n 2013-450 remains in force and unamended](https://www.autoritedeprotection.ci/artci/), with the telecommunications regulator confirmed as the data-protection authority. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The law has stood [since 19 June 2013, designating the telecommunications regulator as the data-protection authority](https://www.fratmat.info/article/2642003/economie/protection-des-donnees-personnelles-djibril-ouattara-presente-les-progres-realises-par-la-cote-divoire), and was [confirmed in that role after the cyber agency's creation](https://www.autoritedeprotection.ci/artci/).<br><br>Nothing in the window amended it. The base holds no text of the law and no implementing decree, so what it obliges a data controller to do is not checkable from anything on file, and the simplified norms the minister names as an advance rest on the same unheld basis.</details> | No change |
| Djibouti | The [data-protection regime sits inside the Digital Code at 156 articles](https://www.techhiveadvisory.africa/insights/review-of-djiboutis-data-protection-law), with none of its obligations evidenced in operation. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The regime is [in force within the Digital Code across 156 articles, providing for seventy-two-hour breach notification, mandatory data-protection officers and a public register](https://www.techhiveadvisory.africa/insights/review-of-djiboutis-data-protection-law), and [none is evidenced in operation](https://www.jonesday.com/en/insights/2025/07/national-assembly-of-the-republic-of-djibouti-approves-digital-code).<br><br>Alongside it a [tiered prior-authorisation regime for national-identifier and biometric processing is in force, with no authorisation on record for the police body-camera fleet](https://www.techhiveadvisory.africa/insights/review-of-djiboutis-data-protection-law). Nothing amended either in the window: what changed is what is being processed under them, not the law.</details> | No change |
| DR Congo | The Digital Code is [now enforced on the state's own initiative, prosecutors instructed to open cases without a complaint](https://acp.cd/anglais/drc-judicial-police-officers-advised-to-prosecute-perpetrators-of-digital-offences/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The 2023 framework law was in force at the window's opening, its third book governing personal data, with [no data-protection authority operational](https://actualite.cd/2026/04/21/rdc-un-nouvel-ouvrage-sur-la-protection-des-donnees-personnelles-porte-sur-les-fonts).<br><br>2026-08-17 - the attorney general at the Court of Cassation [instructed judicial police officers to open Digital Code cases on their own initiative and forward them for summary hearing](https://acp.cd/anglais/drc-judicial-police-officers-advised-to-prosecute-perpetrators-of-digital-offences/), and the audiovisual regulator's president is [contemplating closing a video platform outright after it did not act on a request to block more than 2,000 accounts](https://www.radiookapi.net/2026/08/19/emissions/okapi-service/faut-il-fermer-ou-restreindre-lacces-tiktok-en-rdc).<br><br>The enforcement now running is criminal rather than regulatory: the law's personal-data book has no supervisor, so what has moved is prosecution under it, not protection through it.</details> | Advanced |
| Egypt | The [2020 data-protection law was operationalised by Executive Regulations issued on 1 November 2025](https://accesspartnership.com/opinion/egypt-finalises-executive-regulations-to-the-personal-data-protection-law-pdpl), enforceable from 1 November 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The law had been [in force since 2020 and never operationalised, with no executive regulations issued](https://accesspartnership.com/opinion/egypt-finalises-executive-regulations-to-the-personal-data-protection-law-pdpl) - which is why the [Alexandria Economic Court decided a mobile-operator case in February 2025 on tort grounds](https://shehatalaw.com/law-update/legal-alert-egyptian-court-judgment-tightens-enforcement-of-privacy-and-data-protection-laws), more than four years after the law passed.<br><br>The Regulations were [issued by ministerial decree on 1 November 2025 and circulated in December, establishing the approvals model, a separate licence for each cross-border transfer specifying destination, purpose, data categories and safeguards, extra approvals for electronic marketing and certain public-space surveillance, registered data-protection officers, and breach notification within seventy-two hours to the regulator and three business days to affected individuals](https://accesspartnership.com/opinion/egypt-finalises-executive-regulations-to-the-personal-data-protection-law-pdpl). The obligations become enforceable from 1 November 2026 at the end of a one-year grace period. The Regulations' own text is not held.</details> | Advanced |
| Equatorial Guinea | [Ley 1/2016](https://blog.africadataprotection.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/10/Guinee-equatoriale-ley-1-2.016-de-proteccion-de-datos.pdf) has been in force since August 2016, giving rights of access, rectification, cancellation and opposition and a sanctions regime to CFA 15,000,000. <details><summary>Full record</summary>It was gazetted on 22 July 2016 and took effect twenty days later ([the law](https://blog.africadataprotection.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/10/Guinee-equatoriale-ley-1-2.016-de-proteccion-de-datos.pdf)).<br><br>Ten years on, no implementing decree, guidance or enforcement action under it is held, and the country signed the continental convention on the same subject only in July 2026.</details> | No change |
| Eritrea | A verification pass dated August 2026 confirms no comprehensive data-protection law in force ([redetermination](https://research.lawlab.africa/eritrea/)). <details><summary>Full record</summary>A dated redetermination as of 1 August 2026 confirms the position is unchanged: no comprehensive data-protection law in force, no supervisory authority established and no enforcement decisions published ([redetermination](https://research.lawlab.africa/eritrea/)).<br><br>A regional ministerial assessment places Eritrea's data-protection policy area at a nascent stage without legislation or regulatory oversight, grouped with Djibouti, South Sudan and Sudan against neighbours that have enacted frameworks with supervisory authorities ([policy matrix](https://hoainitiative.org/sites/default/files/2025-09/25th%20HoAI%20Ministerial%20-%20Background%20Paper%20on%20Digital%20Integration%20Update.pdf)).<br><br>A trade regulatory profile records that Eritrea lacks a comprehensive legal framework for data protection, with no data-protection officer, impact-assessment or retention requirements ([profile](https://dtri.uneca.org/v1/uploads/country-profile/eri-country-profile-en.pdf)).<br><br>This is a searched absence rather than an unexamined one: three independent assessments across four years agree.</details> | No change |
| Eswatini | The [Data Protection Act 2022](https://www.esccom.org.sz/legislation/DATA%20PROTECTION%20ACT.pdf) has been effective since March 2022, and its gazetted text names a Commission while the regulator describes itself as the designated Agency. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Act was gazetted and took effect on 4 March 2022. The mismatch between the body the statute names and the body describing itself as designated is recorded rather than resolved ([Act](https://www.esccom.org.sz/legislation/DATA%20PROTECTION%20ACT.pdf)).<br><br>The Act is being read in press accounts as exposing online naming and shaming to a substantial penalty; that is an account of legal exposure rather than an enforcement action, and no complaint, ruling or authority statement is held ([exposure](https://www.eswatiniobserver.com/baby-daddy-shaming-could-attract-e100-000-fine/)).<br><br>A statute whose enforcing body is ambiguous on the face of the record is a limit on everything the protection cells below can say.</details> | No change |
| Ethiopia | [Proclamation 1321/2024](https://www.aln.africa/ethiopia-new-data-protection-law) has been in force since July 2024, a comprehensive regime with extraterritorial reach and no secondary directives behind it. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The proclamation took effect on 24 July 2024 as a comprehensive personal data regime reaching beyond the country's borders ([Proclamation 1321/2024](https://www.aln.africa/ethiopia-new-data-protection-law)).<br><br>What the base does not hold is any instrument under it: the directives that would give the proclamation operative content were still unissued a year after commencement ([a year on](https://shega.co/news/one-year-on-ethiopia-s-personal-data-protection-law-faces-a-slow-path-to-enforcement)).</details> | No change |
| Gabon | [Loi n 025/2023](https://www.apdpvp.ga/wp-content/uploads/2025/01/Gabon-Loi-025-2023-du-12-juillet-2023-portant-modification-de-la-loi-001-2011-du-25-septembre-relative-a-la-protection-des-donnees-a-caractere-personnel.pdf) has bound from publication on 15 July 2023, with article 219 setting no grace period and no transitional window. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The statute was published in the gazette of 15 July 2023 and took effect at once, article 219 allowing no transition ([Loi n 025/2023](https://www.apdpvp.ga/wp-content/uploads/2025/01/Gabon-Loi-025-2023-du-12-juillet-2023-portant-modification-de-la-loi-001-2011-du-25-septembre-relative-a-la-protection-des-donnees-a-caractere-personnel.pdf)).<br><br>It repeals contrary prior provisions of the 2020 ordinance, and the base does not establish whether the funding levy that ordinance created was carried forward ([the 2020 ordinance](https://journal-officiel.ga/4803-00002-pr-)).<br><br>The gazetted text of the founding 2011 statute is not held, so the earlier baseline is read only through the amending instruments.</details> | No change |
| Gambia | The [Personal Data Protection and Privacy Act 2025](https://www.techhiveadvisory.africa/insights/review-of-gambias-personal-data-protection-and-privacy-act-2025) was enacted after several stalled years, with extraterritorial scope and seven lawful bases. <details><summary>Full record</summary>It carries a sensitive-data regime covering genetic, biometric and health data, and rights of access, rectification, erasure and protection from solely automated decisions ([review](https://www.techhiveadvisory.africa/insights/review-of-gambias-personal-data-protection-and-privacy-act-2025)).<br><br>Passage was in October 2025 ([passage](https://babl.ai/gambia-passes-landmark-data-protection-and-privacy-law/)).<br><br>No implementing regulation, commencement notice or first enforcement action under it is held, and the commission that would supervise it is not separately constituted on the record.</details> | Advanced |
| Ghana | The [Data Protection Act 2012](https://techreviewafrica.com/news/4137/minister-for-communication-stresses-data-protection-as-foundation-of-ghanas-digital-transformation) is in force and unamended, with a draft replacement published during 2025 and not enacted. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The 2012 statute remains the sole general data-protection law, reaching controllers and processors rather than the data-broker layer an operator names as the source of leaks ([the Act](https://techreviewafrica.com/news/4137/minister-for-communication-stresses-data-protection-as-foundation-of-ghanas-digital-transformation)).<br><br>A replacement was drafted and published during 2025 and has not been enacted ([draft replacement](https://digitalpolicyalert.org/blog/data-protection-in-africa-roundup)).<br><br>Fourteen years after commencement the base holds no amendment, no commencement instrument for a successor and no parliamentary timetable.</details> | No change |
| Guinea | The [2016 cybersecurity and data-protection statute](https://anssi.gov.gn/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/loi_l2016037an_relative_a_la_cybersecurite_et_protection_des_do.pdf) covers processing by individuals, firms and public institutions, and is held only as an image scan. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The agency publishes it as a 57-page image-only scan with no text layer, so its provisions are the agency's summary rather than the statute's own words, and no promulgation date is published ([the statute](https://anssi.gov.gn/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/loi_l2016037an_relative_a_la_cybersecurite_et_protection_des_do.pdf)).<br><br>It reached the base through a summit profile rather than through the gazette ([profile](https://smartafrica.org/fr/le-transform-africa-summit-2025-confirme-la-dynamique-continentale-pour-une-afrique-digitale-unie-et-souveraine-depuis-la-guinee)).<br><br>No implementing decree, supervisory authority, registration duty or enforcement action under its data-protection half is held.</details> | No change |
| Guinea-Bissau | The transitional council [ratified the African Union, Budapest and United Nations conventions](https://ang.gw/cnt-aprova-com-alteracoes-lei-contra-divulgacao-de-falsas-informacoes-nas-redes-sociais/) in May 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Domestic protection has rested on the [regional supplementary act on personal data protection](https://www.statewatch.org/media/documents/news/2013/mar/ecowas-dp-act.pdf) since 2010, with the national policy still in development.<br><br>The window's movement is treaty ratification rather than a statute: the [transitional national council ratified the African Union, Budapest and United Nations conventions in May 2026](https://ang.gw/cnt-aprova-com-alteracoes-lei-contra-divulgacao-de-falsas-informacoes-nas-redes-sociais/), having [signed the United Nations convention at Hanoi in October 2025](https://www.pjguinebissau.com/artigos/guine-bissau-adere-a-convencao-das-nacoes-unidas-contra-a-cibercriminalidade/). No domestic data protection act has been passed.</details> | Advanced |
| Kenya | The [2019 Act](https://www.businessdailyafrica.com/bd/corporate/technology/regulator-plans-tighter-data-shields-on-offshore-ai-platforms-5546966) is in force, and a court awarded [eleven petitioners KES 900,000 each](https://www.the-star.co.ke/news/2026-08-03-gra-probes-licensed-betting-firms-over-alleged-data-breach-claims) for a sustained compromise of subscriber data. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The regulator is now stretching the Act's cross-border-transfer duties to reach offshore artificial-intelligence processing ([the Act](https://www.businessdailyafrica.com/bd/corporate/technology/regulator-plans-tighter-data-shields-on-offshore-ai-platforms-5546966), [quality standards](https://www.standardmedia.co.ke/sci-tech/article/2001553485/data-protection-office-bets-on-global-quality-standards-amid-rising-cyber-threats)).<br><br>The judgment of 13 May 2026 found a sustained and systematic compromise; the telco is not named in the account held and the judgment text is not on file ([judgment](https://www.the-star.co.ke/news/2026-08-03-gra-probes-licensed-betting-firms-over-alleged-data-breach-claims)).<br><br>On the receiving side, 15 of 224 betting and gaming firms were fully registered as both controller and processor, with 185 unregistered and given 40 days or licence cancellation, and no follow-up is on file after the deadline expired ([registration ultimatum](https://nation.africa/kenya/news/register-or-face-shutdown-kabogo-to-betting-firms-with-children-s-data-5458126)).<br><br>The gambling regulator has an open investigation into three licensees, with the criminal investigations directorate requesting its entire record back to 2018 — the first move against recipients of leaked subscriber data rather than the leakers ([investigation](https://www.the-star.co.ke/news/2026-08-03-gra-probes-licensed-betting-firms-over-alleged-data-breach-claims)).</details> | Advanced |
| Lesotho | The [Data Protection Act 2011](https://cenfri.org/articles/african-data-governance-reform-in-an-era-of-reform/) remains in force and unamended, on the most recent account held, dated March 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Act has been in force since 2011 and was recorded as unamended as at 23 March 2026 ([review](https://cenfri.org/articles/african-data-governance-reform-in-an-era-of-reform/)).<br><br>No amendment, regulation or commencement instrument has been published since, and the base holds no enforcement action, complaint count or guidance issued under it.</details> | No change |
| Liberia | The [Personal Information Act was signed into force](https://www.wearetech.africa/fr/fils/actualites/gestion-publique/le-liberia-adopte-les-lois-sur-la-cybercriminalite-et-la-protection-des-donnees) on 19 August 2026, with no authority yet existing to administer it. <details><summary>Full record</summary>It had been referred to a House committee in June 2025 and left unpassed, drafted with a media-development organisation under a European programme and validated in December 2024 ([signature](https://www.wearetech.africa/fr/fils/actualites/gestion-publique/le-liberia-adopte-les-lois-sur-la-cybercriminalite-et-la-protection-des-donnees), [committee stage](https://www.liberianobserver.com/politics/house-reviews-privacy-data-protection-bill/article_6675211a-e8bc-43c6-8e04-e73cbf26278a.html)).<br><br>The base holds no authority, commencement order, registration regime or enforcement route under it, so what a data subject can actually do with it is unestablished.<br><br>The country is a regional data-protection signatory and had until this month struggled to enforce privacy law at all ([enforcement position](https://africachinareporting.com/liberia-struggles-to-enforce-privacy-and-data-protection-laws/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Libya | No data-protection statute is in force; the [National Artificial Intelligence Strategy](https://libyaherald.com/2025/10/general-information-authority-makes-its-national-strategy-for-artificial-intelligence-2025-2030-publicly-available) sets 2026 as the target for one, with nothing enacted by August. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The strategy published in October 2025 names a data-protection law among its 2026 deliverables ([strategy](https://libyaherald.com/2025/10/general-information-authority-makes-its-national-strategy-for-artificial-intelligence-2025-2030-publicly-available)). Nothing had been enacted as at 10 August 2026, and the base holds no draft text, no sponsoring ministry and no parliamentary stage for it.<br><br>Personal-data systems have meanwhile continued to be built and launched without one.</details> | No change |
| Madagascar | The [data-protection law of 2014](https://www.lexpress.mg/2025/02/gouvernance-numerique-les-donnees.html) has been in force since January 2015 and is recorded as unenforced. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The law is in force and unenforced, and excludes part of journalistic practice from its scope ([law](https://www.lexpress.mg/2025/02/gouvernance-numerique-les-donnees.html)).<br><br>Eleven years on, no amendment, implementing decree, enforcement action or guidance under it is held, and the supervisory body it provides for has never been constituted.</details> | No change |
| Malawi | The [Data Protection Act 2024](https://www.mca.ac.mw/mca-siteup/uploads/Data%20Protection%20Act%202024.pdf) exempted most controllers for twenty-four months from commencement, and that exemption elapsed in early June 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Act commenced in June 2024 with the regulator designated data protection authority, and exempted controllers and processors not of significant importance from compliance for twenty-four months, so most of the economy sat outside its obligations ([Act](https://www.mca.ac.mw/mca-siteup/uploads/Data%20Protection%20Act%202024.pdf)).<br><br>The general exemption elapsed in early June 2026 so that all controllers and processors are now within scope, while the subsidiary instruments to operate registration and enforcement were still in draft ([compliance](https://malawi24.com/2026/07/16/macra-targets-stronger-data-protection-compliance/), [terms of reference](https://ppda.mw/storage/documents/opentenders/ToRs%20OPERATIONALIZATION_OF_THE_DATA_PROTECTION_Act.pdf)). The commencement day cannot be settled from this base: the circulating expiry date implies a 2 June 2024 commencement and the commencement notice is not held.</details> | Advanced, grace period elapsed |
| Mali | The [personal data protection law of 2013](https://orbiteinfo.com/mali-lapdp-ouvre-sa-2e-session-2026-avec-1-000-dossiers-au-programme/) has been in force since May 2013 as amended, and is the stated basis for the authority's sanctions. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The statute is in force and the data-protection authority cites it as the basis on which it fines and issues formal notices ([law](https://orbiteinfo.com/mali-lapdp-ouvre-sa-2e-session-2026-avec-1-000-dossiers-au-programme/)).<br><br>No amendment, implementing decree or consolidated text has entered the base since, so what can be established here is that the law is used rather than what it now says.</details> | No change |
| Mauritania | The [data protection law](https://www.apd.mr/fr/wp-content/uploads/2023/10/Loi-2017-020-sur-la-protection-des-donnees-a-caractere-personnel.pdf) has been in force since July 2017, requiring approved codes of conduct and free, unambiguous consent, with no enforcement decision published. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The statute covers both manual and electronic processing and requires codes of conduct approved by the authority and free, unambiguous consent ([law](https://www.apd.mr/fr/wp-content/uploads/2023/10/Loi-2017-020-sur-la-protection-des-donnees-a-caractere-personnel.pdf)).<br><br>The authority runs monthly ordinary meetings and sensitisation days for ministries, telecom operators and financial institutions, and no enforcement decision, register entry, sanction or annual report is published ([authority](https://www.apd.mr/fr/)). The held extraction of the law cuts off mid-article, so the chapters covering the authority's powers and sanctions are not captured.</details> | No change |
| Mauritius | [Data Protection Officer Regulations](https://lexpress.mu/s/protection-des-donnees-encadrement-renforce-du-role-des-data-protection-officers-559111) were promulgated in June 2026 for January 2027, while the [Data Protection Act 2017](https://maurice-info.mu/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/The-Economic-and-Financial-Measures-Miscellaneous-Provisions-Bill.pdf) is not among the fifty-eight Acts the omnibus amends. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The regulations formalise designation, qualifications, independence and reporting duties for data protection officers across public and private sectors, with a six-month compliance window ([regulations](https://lexpress.mu/s/protection-des-donnees-encadrement-renforce-du-role-des-data-protection-officers-559111)).<br><br>Amendments to the Act itself were committed in July 2026 with no text, timetable or introduction date ([commitment](https://mitci.govmu.org/mitci/mauritius-to-join-america-india-connect-subsea-cable-programme-announces-pm-ramgoolam/)), and the Act is not among the fifty-eight statutes the omnibus bill amends ([bill](https://maurice-info.mu/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/The-Economic-and-Financial-Measures-Miscellaneous-Provisions-Bill.pdf)). The year's expanded inter-agency data sharing therefore arrives with no matching safeguards amendment ([assessment](https://www.biometricupdate.com/202605/theres-no-digital-id-without-data-protection-mauritius-privacy-chief)).</details> | Mixed, officer regulations promulgated while the Act itself was left out of an omnibus amending fifty-eight statutes |
| Morocco | [Law 09-08](https://fnh.ma/article/actualite-economique/protection-donnes-confiance-moteur-economique-numerique) has been in force since 2009, and the regulator states on the record that a recast is now in the legislative circuit. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The commission's president stated in August 2026 that a recast developed with the justice ministry has been sent to the government's general secretariat, describing it as clarification rather than replacement ([statement](https://fnh.ma/article/actualite-economique/protection-donnes-confiance-moteur-economique-numerique), [account](https://theconversation.com/le-maroc-futur-leader-continental-africain-des-data-centers-et-de-lia-288658)).<br><br>No transmission date or text is held ([framework bill](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/tech/morocco-drafts-landmark-law-to-govern-ai-data-and-digital-identity)). The statute's own text and gazette reference are not held either, nor any amending instrument, and a 2023 amendment survives only as an analyst's attributed statement ([guide](https://www.trade.gov/country-commercial-guides/morocco-digital-economy)).</details> | Advanced |
| Mozambique | The [personal data protection bill](https://clubofmozambique.com/news/mozambique-calls-to-strengthen-data-protection-digital-space-legislation/) was being prepared for submission to parliament in August 2026, with no text, timetable or tabling date published. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Civil society argued at a round table on the draft data protection law that the country needs greater national capacity to store and process strategic, sensitive and personal data in infrastructure under its own jurisdiction ([round table](https://integritymagazine.co.mz/arquivos/68263)).<br><br>The agency's president stated in August 2026 that the draft is being prepared for submission; the Cabinet submission planned for June 2026 is not on record ([statement](https://clubofmozambique.com/news/mozambique-calls-to-strengthen-data-protection-digital-space-legislation/)).<br><br>An independent review of the proposed law was published in September 2025 ([review](https://www.techhiveadvisory.africa/insights/review-of-mozambiques-proposed-personal-data-protection-law)), and the government was recorded as preparing it in July 2026 ([preparation](https://news.isocmedias.co.mz/2026/07/23/governo-prepara-lei-de-protecao-de-dados-pessoais/)).<br><br>Until it passes, the country's data rules are scattered across a constitutional article, the electronic transactions law, banking and labour statutes and a 2003 central-bank notice, with a ratified regional convention and no domestic statute giving effect to it.</details> | Advanced |
| Namibia | The [Data Protection Bill](https://www.namibian.com.na/govt-to-introduce-data-protection-bill-in-september/) has not been tabled about thirteen years after drafting began, and the [March 2022 draft](https://action-namibia.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/10/Data-Protection-Draft-Bill-March-2022....pdf) remains the last version published for comment. <details><summary>Full record</summary>A tabling was announced for September 2025 ([announcement](https://www.namibian.com.na/govt-to-introduce-data-protection-bill-in-september/), [return](https://www.namibiansun.com/local-news/data-protection-bill-returns-to-parliament2025-08-18167195)) and the bill was still in finalisation in August 2026, with no data-protection statute in force ([finalisation](https://itweb.africa/article/namibia-pushes-cyber-security-reforms/mQwkoq6YJWlM3r9A), [concerns](https://observer24.com.na/mp-raises-concerns-about-data-control-of-smart-city-grant-from-china/)).<br><br>The March 2022 revised draft runs to fifty-nine pages and eight Parts, with the supervisory authority at Part 2 ([draft](https://action-namibia.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/10/Data-Protection-Draft-Bill-March-2022....pdf)).<br><br>The 2020 version carries a Part on rights of the data subject, together with Parts on recourse to the judicial authority and on offences and penalties, none of which appears in the 2022 version, where access and correction survive only as controller obligations ([2020 version](https://dataprotection.africa/wp-content/uploads/2022/09/Namibia_DPA-Bill.pdf), [removal](https://www.namibiansun.com/local-news/data-protection-bill-returns-to-parliament2025-08-18167195)).</details> | Stalled |
| Niger | [Loi 2022-59 on personal data protection](https://www.lesahel.org/entretien-avec-le-president-de-la-haute-autorite-de-protection-des-donnees-a-caractere-personnel-hapdp/) has been in force since December 2022, replacing the 2017 statute and supplemented by a 2023 law. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The statute replaced the 2017 law and is supplemented by a further 2023 law ([law](https://www.lesahel.org/entretien-avec-le-president-de-la-haute-autorite-de-protection-des-donnees-a-caractere-personnel-hapdp/), [predecessor](https://www.dataguidance.com/sites/default/files/loi-2019-71_modifloirelativealaprotectiondesdonneesacaracterepersonnel_.pdf)).<br><br>No amendment, implementing decree or enforcement record has entered the base since. It is the privacy backstop for a biometric identity programme now in mass enrolment, which is what makes the absence of a caseload figure material.</details> | No change |
| Nigeria | The [Data Protection Act 2023](https://thesun.ng/fg-ngf-to-states-implement-data-protection-act-or-risk-trust-investment/) is in force with an [amendment bill](https://saharareporters.com/2026/07/19/rulaac-warns-data-protection-bill-could-give-nigerian-govt-sweeping-powers-over-social) before the assembly, and the [first damages award](https://saharareporters.com/2026/08/01/court-slams-stanbicibtc-over-data-privacy-breach-awards-ex-customers-n15million-orders) under it was made in July 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Act is criticised as structurally weak in placing the commission under ministerial control, and its section 63 gives it primacy over any inconsistent law on personal-data processing; the federal government and the governors' forum have been urging states to implement it, which is an admission that the tier handling civil registration, health, education and land records is not yet compliant ([Act](https://thesun.ng/fg-ngf-to-states-implement-data-protection-act-or-risk-trust-investment/), [accountability](https://www.dataphyte.com/topic/technology/who-protects-nigerias-digital-citizens/)).<br><br>An amendment bill was introduced in July 2026 ([bill](https://saharareporters.com/2026/07/19/rulaac-warns-data-protection-bill-could-give-nigerian-govt-sweeping-powers-over-social)).<br><br>A court awarded ₦15m general damages, costs and post-judgment interest for marketing after consent was withdrawn, grounded on the Act, the constitutional privacy provision and the consumer-protection Act together; it held personal data must be erased on termination but refused wholesale deletion where statutory banking retention applies ([judgment](https://saharareporters.com/2026/08/01/court-slams-stanbicibtc-over-data-privacy-breach-awards-ex-customers-n15million-orders)).</details> | Advanced |
| Rwanda | The [data protection and privacy law](https://www.newtimes.co.rw/article/32861/opinions/why-2026-is-the-year-of-accountability-for-data-controllers-and-processors) has been fully in force since a two-year grace period ended in October 2023, and the Act's own text and law number are not held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The grace period ended on 15 October 2023, and the authority's framing of the period since is a year of accountability for controllers ([law](https://www.newtimes.co.rw/article/32861/opinions/why-2026-is-the-year-of-accountability-for-data-controllers-and-processors), [expansion](https://www.unwantedwitness.org/africas-digital-economy-is-expanding-faster-than-its-privacy-protections/)).<br><br>Neither the Act's text nor its law number is held. What the base can say about the regime is therefore drawn from secondary accounts of it and from the appropriation lines that fund the supervisory authority.</details> | No change |
| Sao Tome and Principe | The [2016 personal data protection law](https://www.dataguidance.com/sites/default/files/STP%20-%20Lei%20n.%C2%BA%2003_2016%20-%20Lei%20de%20Garantia%20e%20Protec%C3%A7%C3%A3o%20dos%20Dados%20Pessoais%20d....pdf) is in force and establishes the national authority; it is modelled on a European directive Europe itself replaced in 2018. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The gazette text is now held: the law guarantees and protects the personal data of natural persons and establishes the national personal data protection agency as supervisory authority ([law](https://www.dataguidance.com/sites/default/files/STP%20-%20Lei%20n.%C2%BA%2003_2016%20-%20Lei%20de%20Garantia%20e%20Protec%C3%A7%C3%A3o%20dos%20Dados%20Pessoais%20d....pdf)). The capture is truncated inside Artigo 45, so the later articles are not readable here.<br><br>A secondary legal survey records the modelling on the superseded European directive and the penalties, and remains the only source describing the regime's operation ([survey](https://www.ibanet.org/document?id=Digital-Regulations-in-the-Metaverse-Era-Sao-Tome-Principe)).<br><br>Modelling on a directive replaced in 2018 is the substantive point: the regime the authority enforces is a generation behind the standard its cooperation partners work to.<br><br>The country is a signatory to the continental cyber and data convention, with ratification status not stated ([convention](https://www.ibanet.org/document?id=Digital-Regulations-in-the-Metaverse-Era-Sao-Tome-Principe)).</details> | No change |
| Senegal | The [personal data law of 2008](https://dataprotection.africa/wp-content/uploads/2019/10/Senegal-Factsheet.pdf) has stood eighteen years unamended, and a [revision bill](https://lequotidien.sn/mbour-gouvernance-des-donnees-et-intelligence-artificielle-vers-un-nouveau-dispositif-normatif/) was taken to a two-day workshop to finalise it with its implementing decree. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The law was promulgated in January 2008 and came into force in 2014, and no amending law is found. It did not move through the February 2026 breach of the national identity directorate ([law](https://dataprotection.africa/wp-content/uploads/2019/10/Senegal-Factsheet.pdf), [modernisation](https://aps.sn/donnees-personnelles-la-legislation-senegalaise-doit-etre-modernisee-et-adaptee-aux-enjeux-de-lia-expert/)).<br><br>A workshop was convened to finalise the revision bill and its implementing decree; the source is proxy-dated, so the workshop's own date is unknown and must not be rendered as July 2026 ([bill](https://lequotidien.sn/mbour-gouvernance-des-donnees-et-intelligence-artificielle-vers-un-nouveau-dispositif-normatif/)).<br><br>The national data strategy is the frame requiring the bill to be re-levelled, so the revision is being written to a strategy rather than to the breach.</details> | Mixed, a revision bill in finalisation while the law in force has stood eighteen years unamended through a breach of the identity directorate |
| Seychelles | The [Data Protection Act 2023](https://www.gazette.sc/sites/default/files/2023-12/Act%2024%20-%202023%20-%20Data%20Protection%20Act%202023.pdf) is in force in full, its eighteen-month transition having elapsed, and it expressly recognises participation in a global cross-border privacy system. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Act commenced in December 2023 with an eighteen-month transition; that period has elapsed and controllers and processors are now subject to it in full ([Act](https://www.gazette.sc/sites/default/files/2023-12/Act%2024%20-%202023%20-%20Data%20Protection%20Act%202023.pdf), [force](https://www.applebyglobal.com/publications/data-protection-law-now-in-force-in-seychelles), [review](https://www.techhiveadvisory.africa/insights/review-of-seychelles-dpa)).<br><br>A continental review adds that the Act expressly recognises participation in a global cross-border privacy rules system ([reforms](https://fpf.org/blog/africas-data-protection-reforms-a-continental-perspective-on-the-drivers-of-change-in-legal-frameworks/)).<br><br>A statute fully in force with an explicit cross-border interoperability provision puts this unit ahead of most in the frame on the law. What it lacks is recorded in the next cell.</details> | No change |
| Sierra Leone | Cabinet [authorised a Data Protection and Right to Access Information Bill](https://moice.gov.sl/cabinet-approves-first-ever-data-protection-policy-paving-way-for-new-data-law/) in April 2026, and the country has had no data-protection law in force throughout the period. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Bill was finalised in November 2025 and Cabinet approved the policy and authorised the Bill on 21 April 2026 ([bill](https://moice.gov.sl/cabinet-approves-first-ever-data-protection-policy-paving-way-for-new-data-law/), [finalisation](https://sierraloaded.sl/news/government-data-protection-information-bill)).<br><br>No data-protection law has been in force at any point in the period. That is the frame for everything else in this unit: a biometric register covering most of the population, subscriber data disclosed to police, an identity migration in pilot and two competing identity platforms, all operating with no statutory data-protection regime behind them.</details> | Advanced |
| Somalia | A [Data Protection Act](https://www.techhiveadvisory.africa/insights/review-of-somalias-data-protection-act-and-implementing-regulations) has been in effect since March 2023, and a [regulatory framework under it](https://techafricanews.com/2026/01/08/somalia-advances-digital-governance-with-approval-of-public-data-protection-regulation/) was approved by the Council of Ministers in January 2026 and adopted the following month. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Act has been in effect since 23 March 2023 and its implementing regulations were reviewed ([Act](https://www.techhiveadvisory.africa/insights/review-of-somalias-data-protection-act-and-implementing-regulations)).<br><br>The regulatory framework was approved on 8 January 2026 and adopted in February ([approval](https://techafricanews.com/2026/01/08/somalia-advances-digital-governance-with-approval-of-public-data-protection-regulation/), [adoption](https://itweb.africa/article/somalia-adopts-data-regulatory-framework/)).<br><br>A statute in force since 2023 with regulations adopted in 2026 and an authority taking its first nationwide enforcement step in June puts this country ahead of several larger units in this frame on the sequence, if not on capacity.</details> | Advanced |
| South Africa | The Act is unamended, but binding [regulations on the processing of health information](https://www.gov.za/sites/default/files/gcis_document/202603/54268gon7198.pdf) were gazetted in March 2026 and the regulator [states it is working to amend the Act for AI and privacy by design](https://www.timeslive.co.za/news/south-africa/2026-08-18-sahrc-sounds-alarm-over-misuse-of-personal-information-in-digital-age/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-03-06 - the regulator [gazetted final regulations on the processing of data subjects' health information by certain responsible parties, effective on publication and binding on confidentiality, integrity, availability and cross-border transfer](https://www.gov.za/sites/default/files/gcis_document/202603/54268gon7198.pdf). They are subordinate legislation under the Act rather than an amendment to it, and they are the one binding instrument the window produced.<br><br>The statute itself did not move. What is on the record beyond the regulations is an intention: the regulator [stated on 18 August 2026 that it is working with the justice department to amend the Act to cover artificial intelligence and privacy by design](https://www.timeslive.co.za/news/south-africa/2026-08-18-sahrc-sounds-alarm-over-misuse-of-personal-information-in-digital-age/), and in the same session that it has no capability to test external systems for privacy compliance before launch.<br><br>The Act's own terms are unchanged and its ceiling is low against the harm it addresses: [five years on, the maximum fine remains R10m](https://transformpublicrelations.com/2026/07/06/popia-five-years-later-why-is-nobody-being-held-accountable/) against an average measured breach cost several times that. No amendment Bill, gazette notice or draft text is held, so that half is a stated programme of work rather than a legislative step.</details> | Mixed, binding health-information regulations gazetted while the Act itself is unamended |
| South Sudan | No [data-protection statute or commission](https://www.radiotamazuj.org/en/news/article/feature-digital-rights-ignorance-puts-south-sudanese-at-risk) exists, only a constitutional privacy right, and the minister [states a bill will come](https://www.radiotamazuj.org/en/news/article/south-sudan-to-get-data-protection-bill-in-2026-says-minister) after a policy bill drafted in October 2025. <details><summary>Full record</summary>What stands is the Transitional Constitution's privacy article and nothing else: no statute and no commission as at August 2026 ([absence](https://www.radiotamazuj.org/en/news/article/feature-digital-rights-ignorance-puts-south-sudanese-at-risk)).<br><br>The minister stated in December 2025 that a bill will come in 2026, and a policy bill was drafted in October 2025 by a civil-society partnership ([statement](https://www.radiotamazuj.org/en/news/article/south-sudan-to-get-data-protection-bill-in-2026-says-minister), [feature](https://www.radiotamazuj.org/en/news/article/feature-digital-rights-ignorance-puts-south-sudanese-at-risk), [drafting](https://paradigmhq.org/press-release-paradigm-initiative-partners-with-koneta-hub-to-drive-dialogue-on-data-protection-law-in-south-sudan)).<br><br>Meanwhile a biometric payroll register, a humanitarian biometric registration and a planned universal digital identity are all being built with no statute governing any of them.</details> | Advanced |
| Sudan | A [draft Data and Artificial Intelligence Law](https://sudantribune.net/article/317108) is in redrafting with the digital transformation ministry, legislating data protection and artificial intelligence in one instrument. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The draft went to the Council of Ministers with two others and was reported back in redrafting in August 2026 ([draft](https://sudantribune.net/article/317108), [redrafting](https://www.sudanindependent.com/news/politics/2026/08/11/%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B9%D8%AF%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A9-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B3%D9%88%D8%AF%D8%A7%D9%86%D9%8A%D8%A9-%D8%B9%D9%84%D9%89-%D8%A3%D8%B9%D8%AA%D8%A7%D8%A8-%D9%85%D8%B1)).<br><br>Legislating data protection and artificial intelligence in a single instrument is a design few African states have attempted. Neither text is published, so what it would provide on lawful basis, consent, transfer or supervision cannot be stated.<br><br>The authority that would enforce it already exists, created by decree ahead of the statute.</details> | Advanced |
| Tanzania | The [Personal Data Protection Act, 2022](https://www.clydeco.com/en/insights/2026/02/notification-obligations-arising-from-personal-dat) commenced in May 2023 with regulations in force, and practitioners record that key terms remain undefined and there is no adequacy mechanism for cross-border transfers. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Practitioners record that security breach and without undue delay remain undefined, that there is no adequacy mechanism for cross-border transfers, and that there is no duty to notify data subjects ([Act](https://www.clydeco.com/en/insights/2026/02/notification-obligations-arising-from-personal-dat)).<br><br>Those three gaps bear directly on the rest of this unit: the identity register connects to more than 138 institutions, the social-media tax-credential rule publishes trader identifiers, and the financial consumer-protection regulations cross-reference this Act as their data basis.</details> | No change |
| Togo | A [personal data protection law of ninety-seven articles](https://jo.gouv.tg/sites/default/files/JO/JOS_29_10_2019-64E%20ANNEE-N%C2%B026%20TER.pdf) was gazetted in October 2019, and what moved in the period is the base's holding of it rather than the instrument. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The law was gazetted on 29 October 2019 and the gazette's own signature block is dated a day after the title date ([law](https://jo.gouv.tg/sites/default/files/JO/JOS_29_10_2019-64E%20ANNEE-N%C2%B026%20TER.pdf), [implementation](https://documents.worldbank.org/curated/en/099122123072524831)).<br><br>The instrument did not move in the window. It is the statute that created the protection authority by an article rather than by a separate constitutive act, and it is the basis for the corporate correspondents programme recorded separately.</details> | No change |
| Tunisia | A [draft organic law on personal data protection](https://www.arp.tn/loi/project/4237) was deposited in July 2025 and reached committee hearings in February 2026, to replace the [2004 organic law](https://en.africanmanager.com/personal-data-protection-tunisian-parliament-moves-to-curb-ai-and-algorithm-risks/) still governing. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The bill was deposited on 14 July 2025, referred to the rights and liberties committee two days later, and heard in committee in February 2026 ([deposit](https://www.arp.tn/loi/project/4237), [hearings](https://en.africanmanager.com/personal-data-protection-tunisian-parliament-moves-to-curb-ai-and-algorithm-risks/), [update](https://www.techhiveadvisory.africa/insights/bimonthly-update-on-privacy-in-africa-january-february-2026)).<br><br>The tabled Arabic text is held from the Assembly's own dossier and its article count is settled from it at 132: a committee account giving 123 reads as a transposition whose six-part structure matches the tabled text section for section, and a third account's 32 is a leading-digit loss in optical transcription. Whether the committee has since shortened the bill is not established.<br><br>The 2004 law was still the governing instrument when the replacement entered committee ([law](https://en.africanmanager.com/personal-data-protection-tunisian-parliament-moves-to-curb-ai-and-algorithm-risks/), [rights](https://paradigmhq.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/06/Tunisia-Country-Report.pdf)).</details> | Advanced |
| Uganda | The [Data Protection and Privacy Act](https://www.monitor.co.ug/uganda/news/national/over-35-000-entities-unregistered-as-regulator-cracks-down-on-data-privacy-violations-5563326) is in force and the office's director stated a shortfall of some 35,000 unregistered controllers against a 50,000 estimate. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The statute did not change; the estimate is the office's own and no prosecution outcome is held ([shortfall](https://www.monitor.co.ug/uganda/news/national/over-35-000-entities-unregistered-as-regulator-cracks-down-on-data-privacy-violations-5563326), [enforcement](https://businesstimesug.com/whatsapp-updates-privacy-policies-after-uganda-data-protection-enforcement/), [decision](https://adlegalug.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/PDPO-Decision-in-Ad-legal-International-Ltd-v-WhatsApp-LLC-Meta-Platforms-Inc.pdf), [order](https://pdpo.go.ug/media/2025/07/Ssekamwa-Frank-3-Ors-vs-Google-LLC-PDPO-Decision-18th-July-2024.pdf)).<br><br>Registration at roughly three parts in ten of the office's own estimate of the controller population, three years into the regime, is what the enforcement record below has to be read against.</details> | Advanced |
| Zambia | The [Data Protection Act 2021](https://africabusinessinsight.com/zambia-begins-enforcing-controversial-cyber-crimes-act-amid-digital-economy-growth/) is named as part of the operative framework, and a reported Cabinet repeal intent has produced nothing in sixteen months. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Act's number and year of enactment are not established beyond 2021, and the reported repeal intent has produced nothing on the record in sixteen months ([framework](https://africabusinessinsight.com/zambia-begins-enforcing-controversial-cyber-crimes-act-amid-digital-economy-growth/), [safeguards](https://www.undp.org/digital/blog/dpi-safeguards-come-online-zambias-next-phase-digital-integration), [freedom](https://bloggersofzambia.org/6th-state-of-digital-rights-report/)).<br><br>A statute whose own enactment reference cannot be established, under a repeal intent that has produced nothing, is the legal basis under which the identity register, the health record and the refugee database all operate.</details> | No change |
| Zimbabwe | The [Cyber and Data Protection Act](https://www.veritaszim.net/sites/veritas_d/files/Cyber%20%26%20Data%20Protection%20Act%20Cap1207%20No%205%20of%202021%20gaz%202022-03-11.pdf) was consolidated in 2022, and [Statutory Instrument 155 of 2024](https://www.veritaszim.net/sites/veritas_d/files/SI%202024-155%20Cyber%20and%20Data%20Protection%20%28Licensing%20of%20Data%20Controllers%20and%20Appointment%20of%20Data%20Protection%20Officers%29%20Regulations,%202024.pdf) created a data-controller licensing regime a legal expert calls new and strange. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The same Act establishes a cyber security centre and inserts a false-data-message offence into the criminal code ([Act](https://www.veritaszim.net/sites/veritas_d/files/Cyber%20%26%20Data%20Protection%20Act%20Cap1207%20No%205%20of%202021%20gaz%202022-03-11.pdf), [overview](https://www.michalsons.com/blog/zimbabwes-cyber-and-data-protection-act-overview/78795)).<br><br>The statutory instrument was gazetted in September 2024 with licence applications due six months on; two outlets date the deadline to 12 March 2025 and the gazetted text's own reckoning gives 13 March, and an analyst expects the cost to reach consumers as dearer airtime and data ([instrument](https://www.veritaszim.net/sites/veritas_d/files/SI%202024-155%20Cyber%20and%20Data%20Protection%20%28Licensing%20of%20Data%20Controllers%20and%20Appointment%20of%20Data%20Protection%20Officers%29%20Regulations,%202024.pdf), [warning](https://technomag.co.zw/potraz-issues-warning-to-unlicensed-data-controllers-as-compliance-deadline-lapses/), [fees](https://cite.org.zw/potraz-data-licence-fees-slammed-as-costly-burden-on-businesses-and-consumers/)).<br><br>Two regulatory notices followed in 2026, neither published by the regulator itself: the first is reported with no signature date held ([notice one](https://technomag.co.zw/potraz-issues-warning-to-unlicensed-data-controllers-as-compliance-deadline-lapses/)), and the second sets inspections beginning 1 September 2026 ([notice two](https://www.techzim.co.zw/2026/07/potraz-starts-data-protection-inspections-on-1-september-here-is-what-it-means-for-you/)).</details> | Advanced |

## Cybersecurity legislation

| Country | Developments | Progress |
|---|---|---|
| Algeria | [Decree 20-05](https://www.joradp.dz/FTP/jo-francais/2020/F2020004.pdf) founds the national information systems security apparatus and its agency. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [presidential decree signed on 20 January 2020](https://www.joradp.dz/FTP/jo-francais/2020/F2020004.pdf) is the founding instrument of the national apparatus: it creates the national council for information systems security and the national agency that carries the work.<br><br>The security referential the base already held names this decree without carrying its text, so the instrument behind the framework is now on the record rather than cited at one remove.</details> | No change |
| Angola | The National Assembly [adopted the cybersecurity bill on 12 August 2026 by 104 votes to 56](https://angop.ao/fr/noticias/economia/angola-conta-com-lei-sobre-ciberseguranca/), with promulgation and entry into force not on file. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-08-12 — the National Assembly [adopted the bill in plenary by 104 votes to 56 on a presidential initiative, the governing party for and the main opposition against with no abstentions](https://angop.ao/fr/noticias/economia/angola-conta-com-lei-sobre-ciberseguranca/), after [committee approval on 4 August](https://pti.ao/proposta-de-lei-da-ciberseguranca-entra-na-fase-de-analise-detalhada/) and [passage in generality in January 2026](https://www.novojornal.co.ao/politica/detalhe/proposta-de-lei-de-ciberseguranca-aprovada-mas-com-a-abstencao-da-unita-que-receia-que-governo-recorra-ao-corte-de-internet-nos-dias-das-eleicoes-e-das-manifestacoes-69381.html).<br><br>The text [sets graduated administrative fines as multiples of the national minimum wage, from 7 to 70 for individuals on minor infractions and up to 500 for individuals and 4,000 for legal persons on the most serious](https://www.rfi.fr/fr/afrique/20260817-angola-la-loi-sur-la-cybersécurité-adoptée-par-le-parlement-fait-craindre-un-tour-de-vis-sécuritaire). It also [requires telecommunications operators to transmit any communication whose content is criminal or prejudicial to state security](https://pti.ao/lei-da-ciberseguranca-aprovada-na-globalidade/), which the opposition and part of civil society object to as leaving unclear which content qualifies and on what terms subscribers' personal data reaches the authorities, a year before the presidential election.<br><br>No promulgation, gazette number or entry into force is on file. The vote followed a cyberattack on the incumbent operator and came days after the online-falsehoods law entered into force.</details> | Advanced |
| Botswana | The Cybersecurity Act [was assented in November 2025 and gazetted, and was still not in force at the window's close](https://www.bocra.org.bw/sites/default/files/sites/default/files/documents/Cybersecurity_ACT_-_5%20Nov_2025.pdf), its stated April 2026 commencement having passed. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2025-11-05 — [the Act was assented and gazetted on 11 November 2025, with commencement on notice](https://www.bocra.org.bw/sites/default/files/sites/default/files/documents/Cybersecurity_ACT_-_5%20Nov_2025.pdf), against a Bill only at the start of the window. [Commencement was stated for April 2026](https://dailynews.gov.bw/news-detail/90260) and that date passed with no notice on file. Section 3(2) excludes classified information held by eight security and revenue bodies.<br><br>So [the 2018 cybercrime statute remains the operative law, its repeal enacted and not commenced](https://www.bocra.org.bw/sites/default/files/sites/default/files/documents/Cybersecurity_ACT_-_5%20Nov_2025.pdf).<br><br>The authority the Act creates is in the same position. [It exists in law with a board, a chief executive, a licensing power over cybersecurity providers, a power to approve software before market placement and a critical-infrastructure mandate, and it is not established, with no organisation or vote in the year's estimates](https://itweb.africa/article/botswana-advances-robust-digital-ecosystems/rxP3jMBEb527A2ye) — an authority in law with no money at both the legal and the fiscal end.</details> | Advanced, made and gazetted but not in force |
| Burkina Faso | Two application decrees [were adopted on 30 July 2026](https://burkina24.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/CONSEIL-DES-MINISTRES-N%C2%B025-DU-30-JUILLET-2026.pdf), classifying critical-infrastructure bodies and fixing periodic audits and auditor accreditation under the 2024 security law. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-07-30 — the Council of Ministers [adopted a decree identifying, classifying and setting obligations for critical-infrastructure bodies, defined as any entity housing installations indispensable to society's vital functions and expressly spanning energy, water, health, telecommunications and finance, and a second decree fixing conditions for periodic audits and vesting auditor accreditation in the national cyberspace control body](https://burkina24.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/CONSEIL-DES-MINISTRES-N%C2%B025-DU-30-JUILLET-2026.pdf). Before them [the 2024 law asserted an auditor-accreditation duty with no procedure attached](https://www.an.bf/storage/libelrapportautres/rXFCav065KRZXiB2PB65xip11Qg8fPpCZ3sjGpsf.pdf).<br><br>Two things the decrees do not close. The accrediting body is named by function rather than by institution. And [the technical approval and homologation regime for security products, provided by articles 7 and 9 of the law and carrying fines of FCFA 2m to 100m for importing or selling without approval, is still left to a regulation that has not been made](https://www.an.bf/storage/libelrapportautres/rXFCav065KRZXiB2PB65xip11Qg8fPpCZ3sjGpsf.pdf).<br><br>The mapping instrument lands just before the mass biometric enrolment it will have to cover, reported under registration of the population.</details> | Advanced |
| Burundi | A stand-alone cybercrime statute [has been in force since March 2022](https://amategeko.gov.bi/wp-content/uploads/2022/08/BOB_No3ter-2022.pdf), with cybersecurity obligations carried in the [2024 communications code](https://arct.gov.bi/wp-content/uploads/2024/09/LOI_COMMUNICATIONS-ELECTRONIQUES.pdf). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[Loi n 1/10 du 16 mars 2022 portant prevention et repression de la cybercriminalite is in force and recorded as such on the official legal database](https://amategeko.gov.bi/wp-content/uploads/2022/08/BOB_No3ter-2022.pdf), and the base holds its complete promulgated text.<br><br>[The 2024 code des communications electroniques et postales carries cybersecurity obligations at articles 160 to 167 alongside it](https://arct.gov.bi/wp-content/uploads/2024/09/LOI_COMMUNICATIONS-ELECTRONIQUES.pdf). Nothing moved on either in the window. What the base does not hold is any implementing regulation, any national cybersecurity strategy, or any published enforcement action under the 2022 statute.</details> | No change |
| Cameroon | [Loi n. 2010/012 remains in force and is invoked to justify network restrictions](https://lejour.cm/cameroun-quand-les-lois-sur-la-cybercriminalite-menacent-les-libertes/); no reform is adopted on record. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The law has stood since 21 December 2010 and is [in force and invoked to justify network restrictions, with civil-society reform recommendations pending and none adopted on record](https://lejour.cm/cameroun-quand-les-lois-sur-la-cybercriminalite-menacent-les-libertes/).<br><br>The recommendations were addressed to government after a workshop in June 2026. Nothing since reports a government response, a drafting instruction or a bill. The statute is the legal basis under which the restrictions recorded elsewhere in this report were imposed, which is why its non-reform is the material fact rather than its age.</details> | No change |
| Central African Republic | The cybersecurity and cybercrime law [has been in force since early 2024 carrying six to ten year sentences](https://www.africatopsuccess.com/un-projet-de-loi-pour-lutter-contre-la-cybercriminalite-en-centrafrique/), and is [named as the instrument now used against online content](https://corbeaunews-centrafrique.org/des-reseaux-sociaux-a-lintelligence-artificielle-comment-la-vitesse-de-la-technologie-panique-le-regime-obsolete-de-bangui/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[The law was voted by acclamation in January 2024 and has been in force since, carrying six to ten year sentences and fines of FCFA 5m to 10m](https://www.africatopsuccess.com/un-projet-de-loi-pour-lutter-contre-la-cybercriminalite-en-centrafrique/). Its text is unchanged and the statute itself is not held, which is a standing acquisition target.<br><br>2026-07-29 — [a commentator names it as the instrument now used against online content](https://corbeaunews-centrafrique.org/des-reseaux-sociaux-a-lintelligence-artificielle-comment-la-vitesse-de-la-technologie-panique-le-regime-obsolete-de-bangui/). That is an opinion column rather than a record of prosecutions, so what the base can state is the claim and not a case.<br><br>The agency the same law creates has never been established, reported under cybersecurity readiness, so the enforcement side of the statute runs through the ordinary criminal process.</details> | No change |
| Chad | [Loi 009/PR/2015](https://arcep.td/lois.html) is the cybersecurity statute, and the agency under it was [reformed in March 2026](https://www.alwihdainfo.com/Tchad-promulgation-de-la-reforme-de-l-ANSICE_a148767.html) with a cybercrime unit created in June. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The regulator's own register of the laws it works under names Loi n. 009/PR/2015 on cybersecurity and the fight against cybercrime, alongside Loi n. 008/PR/2015 on electronic transactions and Loi n. 007/PR/2015 on the protection of personal data ([register](https://arcep.td/lois.html)).<br><br>Decret n. 148/PR/2026 of 23 March 2026 promulgated the law ratifying the January ordinance reforming the computer-security and electronic-certification agency, widening its remit to cybersecurity, personal data, electronic transactions, cybercrime, video surveillance, drones, social platforms and artificial-intelligence systems ([promulgation](https://www.alwihdainfo.com/Tchad-promulgation-de-la-reforme-de-l-ANSICE_a148767.html)); the Senate had ratified it 58 votes to 3 ([ratification](https://atpe.td/des-reformes-adoptees-pour-renforcer-lansice/)).<br><br>Decret n. 2010/PR/2026 of 1 June 2026 then created a cybercrime unit inside the agency and appointed its coordinator and deputy ([unit](https://www.alwihdainfo.com/tchad-creation-dne-cellule-de-lutte-contre-la-cybercriminalite-a-lansice/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Comoros | [Loi n 21-012/AU was promulgated on 18 January 2022](https://justice.gouv.km/texte/decret-n22-003-pr-promulgation-de-la-loi-n21-012-au-relative-a-la-cyber-securite-et-a-la-lutte-contre-la-cybercriminalite/); [cybercrime sits separately at articles 449 to 505 of the penal code](https://anaden.org/actualites/anaden-information-les-avancees-sur-le-code-numerique). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The base's position on this indicator was wrong and is corrected here. The cybersecurity law was [adopted by the Assembly on 25 June 2021 and promulgated by decret n 22-003/PR on 18 January 2022; its own text defines cybersecurity, cybercrime, critical infrastructure and the supervisory commission, sets its scope at articles 1 to 4 and carries an offence and penalty regime covering illegal access, data and system integrity and fraud](https://justice.gouv.km/texte/decret-n22-003-pr-promulgation-de-la-loi-n21-012-au-relative-a-la-cyber-securite-et-a-la-lutte-contre-la-cybercriminalite/). It had been recorded as drafted and not enacted, on a [2021 agency account written before promulgation](https://anaden.org/actualites/anaden-information-les-avancees-sur-le-code-numerique).<br><br>The [penal code, promulgated in February 2021, separately covers technology-specific offences, online intellectual-property infringement and service-provider liability at articles 449 to 505](https://anaden.org/actualites/anaden-information-les-avancees-sur-le-code-numerique).<br><br>Nothing moved in the window. No implementing decree, prosecution, caseload or enforcement figure is on file at either end.</details> | No change |
| Cote d'Ivoire | [Ordonnance n 2024-950, ratified by the Senate in June 2025, is unchanged in law](https://techreviewafrica.com/news/2427/ivory-coast-senate-unanimously-approves-cybersecurity-and-e-government-bill) and remains the transfer instrument. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The ordinance [moved network security, audit, certification and electronic-certificate issuance from the telecommunications regulator to the cyber agency, and was ratified on 16 June 2025](https://techreviewafrica.com/news/2427/ivory-coast-senate-unanimously-approves-cybersecurity-and-e-government-bill), a step [carried through the electronic-exchanges ratification law](https://www.fratmat.info/article/2633388/economie/echanges-electroniques-le-projet-de-loi-portant-ratification-de-lordonnance-et-modification-des-articles-3-et-17-de-lordonnance-n2017-du-2-aout-2017-adopte-a-lassemblee-nationale).<br><br>Nothing in the window amended it. What moved is the exercise of the transferred mandate rather than the statute: the certificate power is now used through the national root of trust.
