No consolidated cybersecurity statute exists, and the successor strategy is a policy instrument rather than a law.</details> | No change |
| Djibouti | The [Digital Code was published in September 2025](https://www.journalofficiel.dj/wp-content/uploads/2025/09/code-numerique.pdf) and a [statutory cybersecurity authority followed in December](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news-digital/1211-50395-djibouti-advances-bill-to-create-national-cybersecurity-authority). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Digital Code, [regulating personal data, electronic communications, cybersecurity and cybercrime, was published in the Official Journal on 18 September 2025](https://www.journalofficiel.dj/wp-content/uploads/2025/09/code-numerique.pdf) after adoption on 30 June.<br><br>2025-11-04 - the Council of Ministers [approved the bill creating a National Cybersecurity Authority attached to the Presidency, tasked with the national strategy, protection of critical infrastructure and state information systems](https://www.presidence.dj/conseil-des-ministres/2025-11-04); the [National Assembly passed it on 12 November](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news-digital/1211-50395-djibouti-advances-bill-to-create-national-cybersecurity-authority).<br><br>A World Bank report records [operationalising the Code's telecommunications and cybersecurity books as a priority](https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/099021026071059349/pdf/P174461-c8bd0e28-2653-456e-845a-f8242ec8facc.pdf); no implementing decree under either is held.</details> | Advanced |
| Egypt | A [regulatory framework for cybersecurity services](https://www.tra.gov.eg/wp-content/uploads/2024/10/CERT-FW.pdf) rests on the 2018 crimes law, which [two bills would now amend](https://www.elbalad.news/6945377). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The regulator's [operative framework for providing cybersecurity services rests on the 2018 information technology crimes law and the 2003 telecommunications regulation law](https://www.tra.gov.eg/wp-content/uploads/2024/10/CERT-FW.pdf).<br><br>2026-04-22 - two separately sponsored private members' bills would amend the 2018 law: one [to strengthen protection of children against newly emerging electronic crimes](https://www.elwatannews.com/news/details/8270615), the other [to address artificial-intelligence-generated deepfakes and tie fines to the benefit obtained](https://www.elbalad.news/6945377). Neither is recorded as passed.<br><br>The regulator also [signed a cross-border cyber security cooperation agreement with a Gulf council in October 2025](https://mcit.gov.eg/en/Media_Center/Latest_News/News/97759).</details> | Advanced |
| Equatorial Guinea | [Ley 7/2024 on social media and computer crime](https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/noticias/ley_por_la_que_se_regula_el_uso_de_las_redes_sociales_los_delitos_informaticos_y_la_ciber_delincuencia) has been in force since December 2024, its full text unheld and its provisions known from reporting. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Its provisions were reported in rollout from July 2026 ([the law](https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/noticias/ley_por_la_que_se_regula_el_uso_de_las_redes_sociales_los_delitos_informaticos_y_la_ciber_delincuencia)).<br><br>The same statute creates the national cybersecurity and critical infrastructure agency under the Presidency, and that body has no premises, staffing, budget or activity on record, so the law's institutional half exists only on paper.</details> | No change |
| Eritrea | Computer offences sit in two articles of the general Penal Code; no dedicated cybersecurity statute exists ([Penal Code](https://www.unodc.org/cld/uploads/res/document/penal-code_html/PENAL_CODE_ERITREA.pdf)). <details><summary>Full record</summary>Eritrea's only identifiable computer-offence provisions are Articles 374 and 375 of the 2015 Penal Code, covering unauthorised use of a computer — unauthorised access, interception, alteration or destruction of data, interference with lawful data use — and an aggravated form ([Penal Code](https://www.unodc.org/cld/uploads/res/document/penal-code_html/PENAL_CODE_ERITREA.pdf)).<br><br>A country cybersecurity assessment records, item by item, no cybercrime legislation, no cybersecurity regulation, no officially recognised national computer incident response team, no national cybersecurity strategy, no governance roadmap and no responsible agency ([assessment](https://cyrilla.org/api/files/1598632563560c3v0eydmfzh.pdf)).<br><br>Nothing in the window changed either position. Criminal provisions exist; the institutional apparatus a cybersecurity regime needs does not.</details> | No change |
| Eswatini | [Cybersecurity, critical infrastructure, e-commerce and artificial-intelligence legislation](https://techafricanews.com/2025/08/04/eswatini-ramps-up-tech-reform-with-new-legislation-5g-rollout-and-coding-training-for-all/) was named as forthcoming alongside the capital's smart-city deployment. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The communications regulator publicly stated that cyberbullying is a criminal offence under the computer crime statute ([statement](https://eswatinipositivenews.online/esccom-warns-emaswati-cyberbullying-is-a-crime/)). No prosecution or complaint figure is on the base.<br><br>Four instruments were named together in August 2025 ([legislation](https://techafricanews.com/2025/08/04/eswatini-ramps-up-tech-reform-with-new-legislation-5g-rollout-and-coding-training-for-all/)).<br><br>None has entered the base since as a draft, a tabling or an enactment. Naming a legislative programme alongside a deployment of 59 camera systems and a fifth-generation network is the sequence worth recording: the deployment is in progress and the four laws that would govern it are a list.</details> | Advanced |
| Ethiopia | [Proclamation 1426/2026](https://www.insa.gov.et/pdf/download/critical-infrastructure-cybersecurity-proclamation-no-14262026-1786083598) designates twelve critical sectors and places eighteen obligations on their owners and operators, commencing about July 2027. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The proclamation was signed at Addis Ababa on 21 July 2026, and article 28 sets commencement one year after gazettement ([Proclamation 1426/2026](https://www.insa.gov.et/pdf/download/critical-infrastructure-cybersecurity-proclamation-no-14262026-1786083598)).<br><br>The designated sectors run from information and communications technology, financial services and transport to agriculture, trade and industry; a Critical Infrastructure Cybersecurity Fund is established, and the security agency is to write sector standards, give technical assistance and enforce through the transition ([briefing on the proclamation](https://www.ena.et/web/eng/w/eng_9359494)).<br><br>Ratification, assent and commencement dates are not established in the base, and the regulations the Council of Ministers and the agency may make under articles 26 and 27 do not yet exist.</details> | Advanced |
| Gabon | [Loi n 027/2023](https://www.gabonreview.com/laninf-renforce-la-cybersecurite-nationale-avec-secbom/) on cybersecurity and cybercrime is in force from July 2023, its text not held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The statute appeared in the same gazette issue as the data-protection law, of 15 July 2023, and is named as present in the captured issue without being reproduced in it ([gazette issue](https://www.apdpvp.ga/wp-content/uploads/2025/01/Gabon-Loi-025-2023-du-12-juillet-2023-portant-modification-de-la-loi-001-2011-du-25-septembre-relative-a-la-protection-des-donnees-a-caractere-personnel.pdf)).<br><br>It reaches the base through the security agency's own account of its work rather than through the instrument ([agency account](https://www.gabonreview.com/laninf-renforce-la-cybersecurite-nationale-avec-secbom/)).<br><br>No article text, implementing decree, prosecution or sanction under it is held.</details> | No change |
| Gambia | The [2009 Act](https://www.wipo.int/wipolex/en/legislation/details/10478) still carries the computer-misuse and cybercrime provisions; its replacement is stuck in committee. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [Information and Communications Act](https://www.wipo.int/wipolex/en/legislation/details/10478), assented on 29 May 2009, holds the computer-misuse, cybercrime and data-processing provisions and remains the operative instrument.<br><br>The Communications Bill 2025 that would replace it [went to a second reading on 3 March 2026 and was referred to committee](https://thepoint.gm/africa/gambia/headlines/communication-bill-2025-ignites-security-privacy-concern-in-parliament), with concerns over surveillance oversight unresolved. The [regional convention on cybersecurity](https://mocde.gov.gm/3189-2/) was signed in December 2022 and is still unratified.</details> | No change |
| Ghana | The [Cybersecurity Act 2020](https://csa.gov.gh/resources/cybersecurity_Act_2020%28Act_1038%29.pdf) produced its first published penalties in August 2026, three totalling GH¢720,000, of which two are already unsettled. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Act establishes the cyber security authority and a fund, provides for designation, registration and compliance audit of critical information infrastructure, and licenses cybersecurity service providers, with no licence register, designation list or enforcement record held before this month ([the Act](https://csa.gov.gh/resources/cybersecurity_Act_2020%28Act_1038%29.pdf), [in application](https://www.bog.gov.gh/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/DIGITAL-CREDIT-SERVICES-FAQs.pdf)).<br><br>The authority fined the companies registrar GH¢240,000 for engaging an unlicensed provider and that provider GH¢120,000 for operating without a licence ([first penalties](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/13/ghana-csa-fines-orc-gh%c2%a2240000-over-unlicensed-cybersecurity-provider/)).<br><br>The registrar contests its sanction as premature and procedurally unfair, dating its procurement to a commitment authorisation of 28 November 2025 and a tender advertised on 4 December and evaluated on 22 December, before the directive it is said to have breached, and has asked the Attorney-General to intervene ([challenge](https://www.citinewsroom.com/2026/08/orc-csa-sanction-was-premature-seeks-ags-intervention/)).<br><br>The largest penalty, GH¢360,000 against an accounting firm's Ghana practice, was announced and declared satisfactorily resolved in a joint statement the same day, with no statement of whether it was paid, reduced or withdrawn ([penalty](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/18/ghana-cyber-security-authority-fines-ey-ghana-gh360000-for-unlicensed-services/), [resolution](https://www.citinewsroom.com/2026/08/ey-ghana-csa-resolve-licensing-issues-after-gh%C2%A2360000-fine/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Guinea | The [first conviction](https://mediaguinee.com/2026/08/tpi-dixinn-bella-bah-condamne-a-6-mois-de-prison-dont-5-avec-sursis-40-millions-gnf-damende-et-a-la-suppression-de-son-compte-facebook/) under the 2016 statute came in August 2026, and it ordered the deletion of the defendant's social-media account. <details><summary>Full record</summary>A court of first instance convicted an activist and entrepreneur of insult and defamation by means of a computer system on 19 August 2026, sentencing him to six months of which five were suspended and a 40 million GNF fine, and ordering the deletion of his account; the conviction rests on article 363 of the penal code and article 29 of the cybersecurity law ([conviction](https://mediaguinee.com/2026/08/tpi-dixinn-bella-bah-condamne-a-6-mois-de-prison-dont-5-avec-sursis-40-millions-gnf-damende-et-a-la-suppression-de-son-compte-facebook/)).<br><br>The prosecution had sought a year and argued a conviction would set an example to other social-media users; the defence, which asked for acquittal, said it would consider an appeal. Deletion of an account as a criminal penalty is something the base holds no other instance of.<br><br>The statute's cybercrime half reaches offences on national territory and those requiring collection of electronic evidence, again on the agency's summary rather than the text ([the statute](https://anssi.gov.gn/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/loi_l2016037an_relative_a_la_cybersecurite_et_protection_des_do.pdf), [in application](https://letravail224.com/appel-a-manifestation-dinteret-pour-le-recrutement-dun-consultant-informaticien-specialiste-en-informatique-ingenieur-systeme-expert-en-cybersecurite-dans-le-cadre-du-projet-d/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Guinea-Bissau | Three cybercrime conventions were [ratified in May 2026](https://ang.gw/cnt-aprova-com-alteracoes-lei-contra-divulgacao-de-falsas-informacoes-nas-redes-sociais/) alongside a law against false online information. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Guinea-Bissau [signed the United Nations Convention against Cybercrime at Hanoi in October 2025](https://www.pjguinebissau.com/artigos/guine-bissau-adere-a-convencao-das-nacoes-unidas-contra-a-cibercriminalidade/), and the [transitional council ratified it with the African Union and Budapest conventions in May 2026](https://ang.gw/cnt-aprova-com-alteracoes-lei-contra-divulgacao-de-falsas-informacoes-nas-redes-sociais/).<br><br>The same sitting passed a law against the dissemination of false information online, which the ledger carries as its own row. The institutional side is still scoped rather than built: the [feasibility study proposes a security operations centre and a national incident response team](https://cybilportal.org/projects/feasibility-study-of-guinea-bissaus-cybersecurity-landscape/).</details> | Advanced |
| Kenya | The Court of Appeal [struck the false-publication sections](https://www.kictanet.or.ke/kensafespace-project-impact-digital-rights-kenya/) and a shutdown power, the National Assembly has [appealed to the Supreme Court](https://www.wearetech.africa/fr/fils/actualites/tech/kenya-le-parlement-veut-retablir-les-sanctions-contre-les-fausses-informations-en-ligne), and [cyber cafe logging rules](https://itweb.africa/article/kenya-tightens-cyber-cafe-rules-to-close-identity-gap/6GxRKqYQ1aAqb3Wj) take effect in September 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The 2018 statute is narrowed by litigation, with the false-publication offences and the non-judicial website-shutdown power struck and a conservatory order blocking a national internet shutdown; the base does not date the judgments, so whether the narrowing falls inside the twelve months is unestablished ([litigation](https://www.kictanet.or.ke/kensafespace-project-impact-digital-rights-kenya/)).<br><br>Operators of public access centres must capture each customer's name and identity number, the terminal used and session times, and retain the records at least three years, on pain of fines of up to 0.2 per cent of annual turnover with a minimum of about US$3,860 ([rules](https://itweb.africa/article/kenya-tightens-cyber-cafe-rules-to-close-identity-gap/6GxRKqYQ1aAqb3Wj)).<br><br>The regulator clarified on 13 August 2026 that the conditions do not require operators to track browsing history; the conditions were gazetted on 7 August and take effect on 7 September 2026 ([clarification](https://techcabal.com/2026/08/13/kenya-says-cyber-cafes-dont-have-to-track-browsing-history/)).<br><br>2026-08-31 — the National Assembly [asked the Supreme Court to restore sections 22 and 23, which carried fines of up to KSh 5m and up to ten years' imprisonment](https://www.wearetech.africa/fr/fils/actualites/tech/kenya-le-parlement-veut-retablir-les-sanctions-contre-les-fausses-informations-en-ligne). [The Court of Appeal held the wording too broad, Parliament argues it reaches only knowing publication presented as authentic](https://www.wearetech.africa/fr/fils/actualites/tech/kenya-le-parlement-veut-retablir-les-sanctions-contre-les-fausses-informations-en-ligne). The statute's reach is contested, not settled.<br><br>Accession to the Council of Europe cybercrime convention is planned, with public views invited by 11 September 2026 ([accession](https://posts.kictanet.or.ke/accession-to-the-budapest-convention/)).</details> | Mixed, the cybercrime statute was narrowed by litigation while new logging duties were imposed on public access centres |
| Lesotho | The Computer Crime and Cybersecurity Bill was split into a [Cybercrime Bill](https://www.gov.ls/ict/ministry-of-information-communications-science-technology-and-innovation-launched-lescomcsirt-and-inaugural-national-cybersecurity-forum/) and a [Cybersecurity Bill](https://itweb.africa/article/lesotho-steps-up-national-cyber-security/Pero3qZ3J4jvQb6m), both awaiting National Assembly approval. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Both bills were reported on 29 July 2026 as awaiting National Assembly approval, on no published timetable ([forum](https://www.gov.ls/ict/ministry-of-information-communications-science-technology-and-innovation-launched-lescomcsirt-and-inaugural-national-cybersecurity-forum/), [account](https://itweb.africa/article/lesotho-steps-up-national-cyber-security/Pero3qZ3J4jvQb6m)).<br><br>The Cybersecurity Bill is intended to build the institutions protecting critical digital infrastructure, which is the statute the interim response team now operating was set up ahead of ([interim body](https://lesothotribune.co.ls/interim-csirt-lesotho-cybersecurity-launch/)). No text of either bill is held.</details> | Advanced |
| Liberia | The [Cybercrime Act was signed](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/public-management/liberia-enacts-cybercrime-and-data-protection-laws) on 19 August 2026, seven months after passage, alongside [sector cybersecurity regulations](https://lta.gov.lr/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/Cybersecurity-Regulations.pdf) already in force. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The signature ends a delay carried since January 2026, when Senate concurrence was communicated to the House; no commencement date, implementing regulation or enforcement body is published with it, and an international index still places the country in its second-lowest maturity tier ([enactment](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/public-management/liberia-enacts-cybercrime-and-data-protection-laws)).<br><br>The regulator's own 2026 regulations require telecom providers to store personal data primarily inside the country, establish an in-house cybersecurity unit within a year, notify the regulator of breaches within 24 hours and customers within 72, run annual penetration testing and keep security-event logs for at least twelve months; penalties are revenue-linked but apply only from a year after the effective date, and the penalty escalation ladder is unreadable in both held copies ([regulations](https://lta.gov.lr/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/Cybersecurity-Regulations.pdf)).<br><br>A National Cybersecurity Act scheduled for the third quarter of 2025 by the strategy's own action plan is not held as a draft, bill or enactment ([action plan](https://mopt.gov.lr/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/Liberia_National-Cyber-Security-Strategy-2025-2029.docx.pdf)).</details> | Advanced |
| Libya | A comprehensive cybersecurity law is in drafting on the eastern legislative track, discussed with the [National Security Council](https://alwasat.ly/news/libya/524932) in July 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The House of Representatives established a committee for the draft under the Speaker's decision 115 of 2025, and drafting was discussed with the National Security Council on 16 July 2026 ([drafting](https://alwasat.ly/news/libya/524932)).<br><br>No text has been published, no reading has been recorded, and the base holds nothing on how a law passed on that track would apply in the west of the country.</details> | Advanced |
| Madagascar | A [complete recasting of the 2014 cybercrime law](https://www.agenceecofin.com/actualites-numerique/0907-140040-madagascar-adopte-un-projet-de-loi-pour-moderniser-son-arsenal-contre-la-cybercriminalite) reached a plenary vote on 4 September 2026 and [the deputies deferred it, with the electronic communications bill, to a later session](https://www.lexpress.mg/2026/09/tsimbazaza-les-deputes-ajournent-le.html). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The deputies voted to defer five bills, the cybercrime recast and the electronic communications and digital infrastructure bill first among them, saying they needed longer on texts turning on both security imperatives and fundamental freedoms; no resumed date is set ([deferral](https://www.lexpress.mg/2026/09/tsimbazaza-les-deputes-ajournent-le.html)). Commentary reads the contested articles as written to suit the state's own control interests ([comment](https://newsmada.com/2026/09/05/cybersurveillance/)).<br><br>The cybercrime bill reached the National Assembly carrying prison terms of two to ten years and providing for a new digital protection and investigation unit ([report](https://www.tiatanindrazana.mg/fandriampahalemana/volavolan-dalana-momba-ny-heloka-anhabaka-67905.php)), presented as an update of the state's arsenal against rising digital risk ([account](https://www.wearetech.africa/fr/fils/actualites/tech/madagascar-actualise-son-arsenal-face-a-la-montee-des-risques-numeriques)).<br><br>Revision was reported again in August 2026 alongside signature of the United Nations cybercrime convention, with findings that artificial-intelligence-enabled fraud is outpacing African states' response capacity given as the reason ([revision](https://2424.mg/cybercriminalite-madagascar-veut-renforcer-son-arsenal-juridique-face-a-la-montee-des-menaces-numeriques-boostees-par-lia/)).<br><br>Three of the seven bills on the session's order paper are digital: one on electronic communication and digital infrastructure, one recasting the cybercrime law and one authorising ratification of the convention ([order paper](https://midi-madagasikara.mg/assemblee-nationale-sept-textes-au-menu-dune-session-extraordinaire-a-partir-du-25-aout/)). No bill texts are on file.</details> | Stalled |
| Malawi | The [2016 Act is in force since 1 June 2017](https://malawilii.org/akn/mw/act/2016/33/eng@2017-12-31); a [policy and strategy went to consultation in December 2025](https://www.linkedin.com/posts/jolly-chibwana-486620156_moderated-regional-stakeholder-consultations-activity-7402990349041872896-kMyt). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The statute's own text is now held. The [Electronic Transactions and Cyber Security Act, chapter 74:02, has been in force since 1 June 2017 and establishes the computer emergency response team](https://malawilii.org/akn/mw/act/2016/33/eng@2017-12-31).<br><br>2025-12 - [regional stakeholder consultations ran from 1 to 5 December 2025 to support a new cyber security policy and strategy](https://www.linkedin.com/posts/jolly-chibwana-486620156_moderated-regional-stakeholder-consultations-activity-7402990349041872896-kMyt); no draft or timetable is on record, and the account is an officer's personal post rather than an institutional one.<br><br>2026-02 - the regulator [reported that the parliamentary committee on media and communications pledged to support review of the legislation governing mobile-money fraud](https://macra.mw/2026/02/16/task-force-on-mobile-fraud-prevention-call-for-collaboration/). A pledge to review is the whole of the parliamentary record here.</details> | Advanced |
| Mali | The [cybercrime repression law of 2019](https://sgg-mali.ml/JO/2019/mali-jo-2019-43.pdf) is in force, and a [national cybersecurity strategy was approved by decree](https://sgg-mali.ml/JO/2026/mali-jo-2026-04.pdf) in March 2026 with a 2026-2030 action plan. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Law n. 2019-056 is the statute the specialised cybercrime pole and its criminal chamber work under, and its text is held from the official journal ([law](https://sgg-mali.ml/JO/2019/mali-jo-2019-43.pdf)).<br><br>A [decree of 6 March 2026 approves the strategy and its action plan](https://sgg-mali.ml/JO/2026/mali-jo-2026-04.pdf); the strategy text itself is not held, so what is established is that it exists and its period.<br><br>The Council of Ministers approved the creation of a national information systems security agency in June 2026 ([agency](https://koulouba.ml/communique-du-conseil-des-ministres-du-mercredi-10-juin-2026-cm-n2026-23-sgg/)). No prosecution count under the law is published.</details> | Advanced |
| Mauritania | The [cybercrime law of 2016](https://mtnima.gov.mr/sites/default/files/loi_2016_-_007_relative_la_cybercriminalite.pdf) is the instrument in force, its text now held; no amendment is on the record. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Law n. 2016-007 defines computer data, subscriber data and traffic data and sets the offences and procedure for cybercrime ([law](https://mtnima.gov.mr/sites/default/files/loi_2016_-_007_relative_la_cybercriminalite.pdf)).<br><br>Against it, a [national emergency response team and security operations centre were put out to international tender in September 2025](https://wardip.mr/wp-content/uploads/2025/09/ADDITIF-N%C2%B02-au-DAO-N%C2%B0-5-CSIRT-SOC.pdf) and no award is on the record.<br><br>The base holds no prosecution count, case record or amendment, so the statute is dated and its use is not.</details> | No change |
| Mauritius | The [Cybersecurity and Cybercrime Act 2021](https://mauritiusassembly.govmu.org/mauritiusassembly/wp-content/uploads/2023/03/act1621.pdf) has been in force since December 2021 and carries the fake-profile, cyberbullying and undesirable-content offences. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The enacted text as gazetted is held, carrying the offences of misuse of a fake profile, cyberbullying and failure to moderate undesirable content with their penalty scales ([Act](https://mauritiusassembly.govmu.org/mauritiusassembly/wp-content/uploads/2023/03/act1621.pdf)).<br><br>It is the statute the ministry's own online-content warnings rest on, and no charge, prosecution or takedown count under any of those sections is published in this base.</details> | No change |
| Morocco | [Law 05-20 on cybersecurity](https://www.anrt.ma/sites/default/files/2022-02/loi_05-20_cybersecurite_fr.pdf) has been in force since July 2020, with its own article 53 deferring effect to implementing texts the base does not hold. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The law sets outsourcing conditions in article 12 and penalties in article 49, and defers its force to implementing texts in article 53 ([law](https://www.anrt.ma/sites/default/files/2022-02/loi_05-20_cybersecurite_fr.pdf)).<br><br>The decree defining the sensitive data the law turns on is not held, so the statute is on the books and the base cannot establish which processing it reaches. No prosecution, enforcement action or compliance count under it is recorded.</details> | No change |
| Mozambique | A [Cyber Security Law](https://www.incm.gov.mz/?sdm_process_download=1&download_id=5244) and a [Cybercrime Law](https://intic.gov.mz/mocambique-publica-no-boletim-da-republica-as-leis-da-seguranca-cibernetica-e-dos-crimes-ciberneticos/) were published on 1 July 2026 and come into force on 29 September, with [regulations](https://intic.gov.mz/mocambique-publica-no-boletim-da-republica-as-leis-da-seguranca-cibernetica-e-dos-crimes-ciberneticos/) due within 180 days. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Cyber Security Law was approved by the assembly in April 2026, promulgated in June and published on 1 July, creating a national incident response team seated in the ICT institute and, at articles 67 to 71, a Cyber Security Fund financed by a flat 1 per cent of licensed providers' prior-year gross revenue ([law](https://www.incm.gov.mz/?sdm_process_download=1&download_id=5244), [approval](https://clubofmozambique.com/news/mozambique-parliament-unanimously-approves-cyber-security-law/)).<br><br>The Cybercrime Law was published alongside it, providing for preservation and collection of digital evidence and for international cooperation, and repealing six penal-code articles in favour of its own offences ([cybercrime law](https://intic.gov.mz/mocambique-publica-no-boletim-da-republica-as-leis-da-seguranca-cibernetica-e-dos-crimes-ciberneticos/)).<br><br>Neither set of implementing regulations had been made by August 2026, and the Cyber Security Fund's own operating rules are among what they are left to settle ([regulations](https://intic.gov.mz/mocambique-publica-no-boletim-da-republica-as-leis-da-seguranca-cibernetica-e-dos-crimes-ciberneticos/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Namibia | A [Cybercrime Bill](https://itweb.africa/article/namibia-pushes-cyber-security-reforms/mQwkoq6YJWlM3r9A) was named as being finalised in July 2026, with no draft published and no tabling date given. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Enforcement has relied on the electronic transactions statute, the communications statute and the penal code; cabinet approved cybercrime proposals in November 2025 ([proposals](https://itweb.africa/article/namibia-cabinet-approves-fresh-proposals-to-combat-cyber-crime/5yONPvEraz37XWrb)) and the minister named the bill as being finalised alongside the data protection bill in July 2026 ([finalisation](https://itweb.africa/article/namibia-pushes-cyber-security-reforms/mQwkoq6YJWlM3r9A)).<br><br>The base first names this bill in July 2026, and the national incident response team states in its own report that it cannot fully execute its mandate absent a cybersecurity law.</details> | Advanced, marginal |
| Niger | The [cybersecurity centre and the certification agency were created by decree in October 2025](https://www.gouv.ne/index.php/les-communiques-du-gouvernement/919-communique-du-conseil-des-ministres-du-samedi-11-octobre-2025), and the new penal code adds cybercrime provisions. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [Council of Ministers of 11 October 2025](https://www.gouv.ne/index.php/les-communiques-du-gouvernement/919-communique-du-conseil-des-ministres-du-samedi-11-octobre-2025) adopted the decree organising the electronic certification agency under articles 17 and 18 of the 2019 electronic transactions law, and the decrees creating the national cybersecurity centre and approving its statutes as the body coordinating implementation of the 2022 national cybersecurity strategy.<br><br>The justice ministry's [criminal-chain workshop of 17 April 2026](https://justice.gouv.ne/index.php/les-actualites-1/350-vendredi-17-avril-2026-ministere-de-la-justice-idlo-atelier-de-la-chaine-penale-pour-echanger-sur-les-defis-en-matiere-de-lutte-contre-les-infractions-a-la-loi-penale) reports that the new penal code introduces provisions on cybercrime and related offences, with the 2020 national criminal policy to be aligned with the current framework.<br><br>The [2019 cybercrime statute](https://mcnti.gouv.ne/ova_doc/loi_2019-33_portant-repression-de-la-cybercriminalite-au-niger_-vf/) remains the dedicated law. Neither the centre's operating capability nor a national incident-response team's activity is evidenced.</details> | Advanced |
| Nigeria | The [Cybercrimes (Amendment) Act 2024](https://www.vanguardngr.com/2025/08/how-misuse-of-cybercrime-act-cost-nigeria-n1-1trn-in-7-yrs/) is in force, and lawyers argue enforcement agencies misapply it against dissent rather than cyber threats. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Civil-society lawyers argue the Act is misapplied against dissent, with most such cases collapsing because complainants never testify; the financial sector alone lost ₦53.4bn to cybercrime in 2024 ([Act](https://www.vanguardngr.com/2025/08/how-misuse-of-cybercrime-act-cost-nigeria-n1-1trn-in-7-yrs/)).<br><br>No prosecution, conviction or dismissal series under the Act is published, so the claim of misuse and the claim of necessity rest on the same absence of data.</details> | No change |
| Rwanda | The [2018 cybercrimes law](https://cyber.gov.rw/index.php?eID=dumpFile&f=70&t=f&token=8a1cd43626bfcc2b99fba277aca01e6f2aaa1788) remains the core statute, and [software security directives](https://cyber.gov.rw/updates/article/ncsa-publishes-application-software-security-directives/) were issued in April 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The statute in force since 2018 covers offences, service-provider duties, investigation powers and critical-infrastructure protection, and is named as operative by the current national cybersecurity strategy ([law](https://cyber.gov.rw/index.php?eID=dumpFile&f=70&t=f&token=8a1cd43626bfcc2b99fba277aca01e6f2aaa1788)).<br><br>The cyber authority issued application software security directives in April 2026, operationalising security by design across the development lifecycle for internet and intranet-accessible applications in public and private institutions ([announcement](https://cyber.gov.rw/updates/article/ncsa-publishes-application-software-security-directives/), [directives](https://cyber.gov.rw/index.php?eID=dumpFile&t=f&f=505&token=1eb7198ee3d2b957e6a1d79096a0bc60b8118728)).<br><br>Directives are subordinate instruments. The statute beneath them is eight years old and unamended.</details> | Advanced |
| Sao Tome and Principe | A [2017 cybercrime statute](https://natlex.ilo.org/dyn/natlex2/natlex2/files/download/110002/152017.pdf) creates the computer-crime offences, sets the procedural powers for electronic evidence and requires a 24-hour police contact point. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Artigo 1 states that the law establishes substantive and procedural criminal provisions and provisions on international cooperation relating to cybercrime and the collection of electronic evidence, adapting domestic law to the international conventions ([statute](https://natlex.ilo.org/dyn/natlex2/natlex2/files/download/110002/152017.pdf)).<br><br>It creates offences of computer forgery, damage to programs or data, computer sabotage, illegal access, illegal interception and illegitimate reproduction of a protected program, and requires the judicial police to keep a permanent international cooperation contact point ([statute](https://natlex.ilo.org/dyn/natlex2/natlex2/files/download/110002/152017.pdf)).<br><br>A national cybersecurity strategy for 2024 to 2028 exists in draft and went to public consultation in September 2025 ([consultation](https://www.ager.st/index.php/pt/actualidades/19-annonces/106-estrategia-nacional-de-ciberseguranca-de-sao-tome-e-principe)); the statute is in force and the strategy is not.</details> | No change |
| Senegal | A [critical-infrastructure protection law was adopted unanimously](https://www.seneweb.com/fr/news/Politique/securite-numerique-lassemblee-nationale-adopte-a-lunanimite-le-projet-de-loi-sur-les-infrastructures-critiques_n_501981.html) on 20 August 2026, and both the [bill text and the inter-commission report](https://www.vie-publique.sn/docs/28c7e9ff-d2e3-4dd9-9bd0-577ace23d448/projet-loi-25-2026-protection-infrastructures-critiques-securite-numerique-assemblee-nationale-du-senegal.pdf) on it are now held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The National Assembly adopted the bill on the protection of critical information infrastructure and digital security ([adoption](https://atlanticactu.com/cyberattaques-recurrentes-le-senegal-se-dote-dune-autorite-nationale-de-cybersecurite/)). Adopted is not promulgated; the base holds no gazette text.<br><br>The Assembly adopted the law unanimously ([adoption](https://www.seneweb.com/fr/news/Politique/securite-numerique-lassemblee-nationale-adopte-a-lunanimite-le-projet-de-loi-sur-les-infrastructures-critiques_n_501981.html), [detail](https://news.broadcastmediaafrica.com/2026/08/17/senegal-advances-new-cybersecurity-bill-to-protect-critical-infrastructure-and-strengthen-digital-sovereignty/), [safeguard](https://itweb.africa/article/senegal-moves-to-safeguard-digital-infrastructure/KzQenMjyxD47Zd2r)). Every provision reaching the base through the news accounts is the ministry's own description and the enacted text is not held; the base held nothing on this workstream at the window's start ([cooperation](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/senegal-pushes-stronger-cybersecurity-cooperation-to-protect-critical-infrastructure), [security](https://www.wearetech.africa/fr/fils/actualites/tech/cyberattaques-le-senegal-prepare-une-loi-pour-proteger-ses-infrastructures-critiques), [digital security](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/14/senegal-moves-to-strengthen-critical-infrastructure-and-digital-security/)).<br><br>The bill's own text and the parliamentary inter-commission report on it are both held, so the base can read the provisions and the committee's reading of them rather than the tabling alone ([bill](https://www.vie-publique.sn/docs/28c7e9ff-d2e3-4dd9-9bd0-577ace23d448/projet-loi-25-2026-protection-infrastructures-critiques-securite-numerique-assemblee-nationale-du-senegal.pdf), [text](https://www.vie-publique.sn/docs/63b20e46-aaa7-4936-8fdb-7132ac4447b9/projet-loi-25-2026-assemblee-nationale-protection-infrastructures-information-critiques-securite-numerique.pdf), [hosting](https://aps.sn/lhebergement-des-donnees-personnelles-au-coeur-de-la-pleniere-sur-la-securite-numerique-au-senegal/), [continuity](https://aps.sn/cybersecurite-khady-sarr-alerte-sur-les-risques-pour-la-continuite-des-soins/)).<br><br>During the debate the Assembly's first vice-president argued cybersecurity must become a standing administrative culture rather than a compliance exercise, calling for mandatory staff training and minimum device-security standards — an account of where the state's own practice stands rather than of what the law requires ([culture](https://lesoleil.sn/actualites/technologie/el-malick-ndiaye-nous-devons-faire-de-la-cybersecurite-une-veritable-culture-administrative/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Seychelles | The [Cybercrimes Act 2021](https://www.gazette.sc/sites/default/files/2021-12/Act%2059%20-%20Cybercrimes%20and%20other%20Related%20Crimes%20Act%202021.pdf) was [amended in December 2025](https://www.gazette.sc/sites/default/files/2025-12/Act%2023%202025%20-%20Cybercrimes%20and%20other%20Related%20Crimes%20%28Amendment%29%20Act%202025.pdf), widening offences and adding search and interception powers. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The 2021 Act created the first standalone cybercrime offences regime, repealing the Computer Misuse Act, and commenced on 1 February 2022 ([Act](https://www.gazette.sc/sites/default/files/2021-12/Act%2059%20-%20Cybercrimes%20and%20other%20Related%20Crimes%20Act%202021.pdf)).<br><br>The amendment passed the National Assembly on 15 December and was assented on 22 December 2025; it widens the offence definitions, defines subscriber information as identity, address and billing or service details, and adds search and interception powers ([amendment](https://www.gazette.sc/sites/default/files/2025-12/Act%2023%202025%20-%20Cybercrimes%20and%20other%20Related%20Crimes%20%28Amendment%29%20Act%202025.pdf)).<br><br>Cabinet separately approved a judiciary digitalisation programme in May 2026 expressly requiring stronger cybersecurity and data-management systems around the courts ([judiciary](https://statehouse.gov.sc/index.php/cabinet-decisions/6974/cabinet-business-wednesday-13th-may-2026)). No prosecution, caseload or enforcement figure under the Act is published.</details> | Advanced |
| Sierra Leone | The [Cyber Security and Crime Act 2021](https://sierralii.gov.sl/akn/sl/act/2021/7/eng@2021-11-25/source.pdf) is in force and unamended, with no prosecution under it reported. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Act was gazetted on 25 November 2021 and the text is on the national legal information institute ([Act](https://sierralii.gov.sl/akn/sl/act/2021/7/eng@2021-11-25/source.pdf)).<br><br>It remains in force and unamended, and no prosecution under it appears in the base ([status](https://owlpress-sl.com/dont-call-meeting-inside-whatsapp-scam-emptying-orange-money/)).<br><br>That absence is the finding worth carrying: a statute reaching the conduct exists, and the mobile-money fraud wave recorded elsewhere has produced no reported case under it.</details> | No change |
| Somalia | The lower house [approved a Cybersecurity Law](https://nca.gov.so/somalias-parliament-approves-the-cybersecurity-law/) in January 2026, and the upper house [took only a first reading](https://www.dawan.africa/news/somalia-senate-holds-first-reading-of-cybersecurity-meteorological-agency-bills) on 29 August 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The House of the People approved the law on 26 January 2026, assigning policy to the communications ministry, technical oversight to the regulator and obligations to critical-infrastructure operators ([approval](https://nca.gov.so/somalias-parliament-approves-the-cybersecurity-law/), [coverage](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/tech/somali-parliament-approves-cybersecurity-law-amid-rising-threats)).<br><br>The Upper House gave a six-chapter, 33-article cybersecurity bill its first reading on 29 August 2026, at the first sitting of its eighth term ([first reading](https://www.dawan.africa/news/somalia-senate-holds-first-reading-of-cybersecurity-meteorological-agency-bills)). The chamber's own published procedure requires three readings before a bill passes it ([procedure](https://senate.gov.so/laws/?lang=en)).<br><br>So the instrument is short of final approval, and nothing on file states whether the bill now before the upper house is the one the lower house approved in January. The country ranked in the international index's fifth tier at 37.39 of 100 in 2024 and is a signatory to the continental cyber convention ([index](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/tech/somali-parliament-approves-cybersecurity-law-amid-rising-threats)).<br><br>The law's text is not held, and no implementing regulation, commencement date or enforcement action is on record.</details> | Advanced, and the law is not yet through both chambers |
| South Africa | The Cybercrimes Act [is in force](https://www.gov.za/sites/default/files/gcis_document/202106/44651gon324.pdf), the state [signed the United Nations cybercrime convention in October 2025](https://www.justice.gov.za/m_speeches/2025/20251030-ACWG-Min.html), and [a revised strategy awaits adoption](https://www.gov.za/news/speeches/minister-khumbudzo-ntshavheni-state-security-agency-dept-budget-vote-202627-22-may). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[The Cybercrimes Act is the standing substantive statute](https://www.gov.za/sites/default/files/gcis_document/202106/44651gon324.pdf), held as an excerpt.<br><br>2025-10-25 — [the state signed the United Nations Convention against Cybercrime](https://www.justice.gov.za/m_speeches/2025/20251030-ACWG-Min.html), told to a G20 ministerial by the justice minister. No ratification step has followed.<br><br>2026-05-22 — [consultation on a revised National Cybersecurity Strategy is substantially complete with adoption expected in the following quarter](https://www.gov.za/news/speeches/minister-khumbudzo-ntshavheni-state-security-agency-dept-budget-vote-202627-22-may). No adopted text is on file, so the strategy is an expectation stated in a budget vote.<br><br>Signature, a statute and a strategy in consultation is a fuller legislative position than most countries in this base hold, and none of the three is a report of enforcement: no prosecution count under the Act appears anywhere on this ledger.</details> | Advanced |
| South Sudan | A [Cyber Crimes and Computer Abuse Act](https://www.radiotamazuj.org/en/news/article/minister-ateng-defends-cybercrime-law-amid-concerns) was signed in February 2026, criminalising false information, online impersonation and hate speech alongside hacking, and the text is withheld. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The bill passed in November 2025 and was signed on 18 February 2026, with the ministry setting out its operation the following day ([signature](https://www.radiotamazuj.org/en/news/article/minister-ateng-defends-cybercrime-law-amid-concerns), [passage](https://techafricanews.com/2025/11/26/south-sudan-takes-major-step-against-cybercrime-with-new-bill/), [operation](https://www.onecitizendaily.com/index.php/2026/02/20/ict-ministry-vows-to-ensure-effective-operation-of-cyber-crime-act/), [effect](https://www.eyeradio.org/controversial-cybercrime-act-takes-effect-amid-free-expression-concerns/)).<br><br>The speech offences and the withheld text are the objections on record. The country has no data-protection statute, no privacy commission and no incident response team, so the first computer-crime statute is also the first digital statute of any kind.<br><br>Its text cannot be read because there is no operating gazette, which is recorded separately.</details> | Advanced |
| Sudan | A [draft Cybersecurity Law](https://sudantribune.net/article/317108) is in redrafting, and its relationship to the cybercrime provisions the country described to a regional experts' meeting is not established. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The draft is the second of the three-instrument package and was reported in redrafting in August 2026 ([draft](https://sudantribune.net/article/317108), [redrafting](https://www.sudanindependent.com/news/politics/2026/08/11/%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B9%D8%AF%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A9-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B3%D9%88%D8%AF%D8%A7%D9%86%D9%8A%D8%A9-%D8%B9%D9%84%D9%89-%D8%A3%D8%B9%D8%AA%D8%A7%D8%A8-%D9%85%D8%B1)).<br><br>Its relationship to the cybercrime provisions the country separately described to an Arab experts' meeting in July 2026 is not established ([meeting](https://www.suna.sd/posts/okyl-ozar-alaadl-ykod-ofd-alsodan-almshark-fy-alagtmaaa-altasaa-lfryk-alkhbraaa-alaarb-almaany-bmoagh-graym-tkny-almaalomat)).<br><br>That matters because the cybercrime statute in force is being used against speech, and whether the new draft replaces, amends or sits alongside it is exactly what no source says.</details> | Advanced |
| Uganda | The Constitutional Court [nullified five sections of the Computer Misuse Act](https://www.newvision.co.ug/category/news/constitutional-court-nullifies-key-computer-m-NV_230010_042026) on quorum and vagueness grounds, and government [declared telecom-infrastructure attacks economic sabotage](https://nilepost.co.ug/news/360525/government-declares-telecom-infrastructure-vandalism-economic-sabotage). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The nullification covered sections 11, 23 and 26 to 29. The movement stem records an instrument neutralised; the rights reading is the opposite ([nullification](https://www.newvision.co.ug/category/news/constitutional-court-nullifies-key-computer-m-NV_230010_042026)).<br><br>Vandalism against telecommunications infrastructure is now treated as economic sabotage, with a subscriber-card conviction obtained under the 2024 interception regulations rather than the Act ([sabotage](https://nilepost.co.ug/news/360525/government-declares-telecom-infrastructure-vandalism-economic-sabotage), [crackdown](https://itweb.africa/article/uganda-steps-up-crackdown-on-telecoms-vandalism/dgp45MaB8lZqX9l8)).<br><br>Which statute an offence is charged under is worth recording when the principal computer-crime Act has just lost five sections.</details> | Mixed, five Computer Misuse Act sections nullified for vagueness while vandalism enforcement was stepped up as economic sabotage |
| Zambia | The [Cyber Security Act No. 3 of 2025](https://www.parliament.gov.zm/sites/default/files/documents/acts/Act%20No.%203%20of%202025%2C%20The%20Cyber%20Security_0.pdf) and the [Cyber Crimes Act No. 4 of 2025](https://www.parliament.gov.zm/sites/default/files/documents/acts/Acts%20No.%204%20of%202025%2C%20The%20Cyber%20Crimes.pdf) are both in force, and the investigatory and interception powers in them are court-gated. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The gazetted text of the security Act was acquired from the National Assembly's own directory; it commenced on 12 May 2025 by statutory instrument ([Act](https://www.parliament.gov.zm/sites/default/files/documents/acts/Act%20No.%203%20of%202025%2C%20The%20Cyber%20Security_0.pdf), [signature](https://www.parliament.gov.zm/sites/default/files/documents/acts/Act%20No.%203%20of%202025%2C%20The%20Cyber%20Security_0.pdf)).<br><br>Enforcement of the crimes Act was declared fully operational in July 2026. The gazetted texts of both Acts are now held, and the investigatory powers in the crimes Act and the interception power in the security Act are both court-gated; the claim that the 2025 Acts authorise interception without prior judicial review is not borne out, while the presidential-control half of that claim is ([enforcement](https://africabusinessinsight.com/zambia-begins-enforcing-controversial-cyber-crimes-act-amid-digital-economy-growth/), [warning](https://diggers.news/local/2025/04/18/us-warns-its-citizens-over-new-surveillance-law-in-zambia/), [compliance](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/22/zambia-reinforces-cybersecurity-and-privacy-through-cyber-crimes-act/), [freedom](https://bloggersofzambia.org/6th-state-of-digital-rights-report/)).<br><br>The Law Association was reported set to mount a challenge in April 2025; the single held source is a lede-only capture and the absence of any subsequent record is itself the finding ([challenge](https://www.lusakatimes.com/2025/04/22/laz-set-to-mount-legal-challenge-against-cyber-laws-warns-of-democratic-backslide/)).</details> | Mixed, two 2025 Acts in force with their investigatory powers court-gated while a threatened legal challenge has produced no record |
| Zimbabwe | The [Cyber and Data Protection Act](https://www.veritaszim.net/sites/veritas_d/files/Cyber%20%26%20Data%20Protection%20Act%20Cap1207%20No%205%20of%202021%20gaz%202022-03-11.pdf) is unamended, cybercrime offences sit in the Criminal Law Code, and a national cybersecurity strategy was completed in October 2025. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Act of 2021 remains the principal statute, designating the telecommunications regulator as data protection authority and providing for a cyber security centre; it is unamended and administrative sanctions are still not provided for ([Act](https://www.veritaszim.net/sites/veritas_d/files/Cyber%20%26%20Data%20Protection%20Act%20Cap1207%20No%205%20of%202021%20gaz%202022-03-11.pdf)).<br><br>The regulator's director-general stated on 24 January 2026 that Zimbabwe has no standalone cybercrime Act, and that hacking, unlawful acquisition of data and related offences sit in Part VIII of the Criminal Law (Codification and Reform) Act as amended in 2021, hacking carrying a fine or up to ten years' imprisonment ([clarification](https://news.pindula.co.zw/2026/01/25/there-is-no-separate-cyber-crimes-act-in-zimbabwe-potraz/)).<br><br>The ICT ministry announced on 15 October 2025 that the National Cybersecurity Strategy had been completed, a day after Cabinet approved the artificial-intelligence strategy ([strategy](https://www.heraldonline.co.zw/new-cybersecurity-strategy-to-boost-zimbabwes-digital-resilience/)). The same minister described a 2026-2030 strategy as still being finalised in July 2026, and no text is held at either date ([July 2026](https://gemnation.co.zw/zimbabwe-to-establish-national-cybersecurity-authority/)).</details> | Advanced |

## Legislation covering digital id

| Country | Developments | Progress |
|---|---|---|
| Algeria | A [digital-identity and trust-services law has been before the Assembly since 2 November 2025](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news-digital/0511-50150-algeria-updates-digital-services-and-online-identity-law); no promulgation is held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The law did not exist at the window's opening. It was [approved by the Council of Ministers on 2 November 2025 and is before the National Assembly](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news-digital/0511-50150-algeria-updates-digital-services-and-online-identity-law), [reported in the biometrics press](https://www.biometricupdate.com/202511/algeria-approves-draft-legislation-on-digital-id-trust-services), [in the state daily](https://www.elmoudjahid.dz/fr/actualite/le-projet-de-loi-sur-les-services-de-confiance-numeriques-a-l-apn-la-securite-des-systemes-d-information-nationaux-renforcee-244691) and [as a digital-trust and electronic-transactions measure](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/tech/algeria-approves-law-to-strengthen-digital-trust-and-e-transactions). No promulgation or gazette reference is held as at 2026-08-03.<br><br>Meanwhile a unified national digital identity is already issued and in use as a single credential across the government services portal, so the credential is running nine months ahead of the statute that would govern it.</details> | Advanced |
| Angola | The Council of Ministers [approved a civil and criminal identification bill in April 2026](https://angop.ao/noticias/politica/bi-passa-ser-documento-unico-valido-para-identificacao/), which would set the country's first dedicated identity-data regime. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-04-29 — the Council of Ministers [approved a bill making the identity card the only valid document for banking, telecoms, school enrolment, property registration and contracts, and establishing the country's first dedicated regime for processing identity data](https://angop.ao/noticias/politica/bi-passa-ser-documento-unico-valido-para-identificacao/). Voter cards, driving licences and passports are currently accepted as substitutes.<br><br>No parliamentary stage has been recorded since. Two other instruments touch the same ground and neither is this one: [an electoral registration law replacing in-person enrolment with automatic enrolment from the civil identity database](https://executivedigest.sapo.pt/servicos-de-informacoes-angolanos-acedem-a-dados-biometricos-sem-controlo-judicial-relatorio/), which reaches the base only through a secondary account with no gazette reference, and the data protection law reported above, which is the general regime the identity-specific one would sit beside.</details> | Advanced |
| Benin | The 2017 identification law [is the statutory base for the identifier and the agency](https://sgg.gouv.bj/doc/loi-2017-08/), extended in June 2025 [to foreigners and Afro-descendants](https://www.lanation.bj/actualites/assemblee-nationale-larticle-6-de-la-loi-sur-lidentification-des-personnes-physiques-retouche). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[The 2017 law is the statutory base for the identification of natural persons, creating the identification agency and the unique personal identifier](https://sgg.gouv.bj/doc/loi-2017-08/). It is held as the text layer of a scan, so quotation from it is unsafe.<br><br>2025-06-23 — [law 2025-12 creates a residents file with distinct foreigner and Afro-descendant identifiers, for migration control and civic inclusion](https://www.lanation.bj/actualites/assemblee-nationale-larticle-6-de-la-loi-sur-lidentification-des-personnes-physiques-retouche). Nothing of the kind existed before it, and it is the only amendment to the identity statute in the window.<br><br>The instrument the estate still lacks is a rule on what may be asked of the register: the data protection authority's intervention on the electoral roll, reported under data protection, was a regulator settling by decision a question no statute answers.</details> | Advanced |
| Botswana | [Section 14 of the Digital Services Act provides for online unique identification](http://www.bocra.org.bw/sites/default/files/sites/default/files/documents/digital_services_ACT.pdf), and the [amending registration Act commenced in November 2025](https://www.botswanaspeaks.gov.bw/media/COMMITTEE%20OF%20SUPPPLY%20SPEECHES/MLHA%20-%20Committee%20of%20Supply%20Speech%20FINAL%20%20-%2024-02-2026.pdf). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2025-10-17 — [the Digital Services Act was assented, its section 14 providing for online unique identification](http://www.bocra.org.bw/sites/default/files/sites/default/files/documents/digital_services_ACT.pdf).<br><br>2026-02-24 — [the ministry confirmed the National Registration (Amendment) Act No. 7 of 2025 commenced in November 2025, and reported a digital-ID roadmap facilitated by the African Development Bank due at the end of March 2026](https://www.botswanaspeaks.gov.bw/media/COMMITTEE%20OF%20SUPPPLY%20SPEECHES/MLHA%20-%20Committee%20of%20Supply%20Speech%20FINAL%20%20-%2024-02-2026.pdf).<br><br>No commencement notice for the Digital Services Act itself is held, and no roadmap document.</details> | Advanced |
| Burundi | A ministerial ordinance [made identity-card registration of every SIM compulsory from 25 June 2026](https://www.agenceecofin.com/actualites-numerique/0506-139050-burundi-identification-des-cartes-sim-obligatoire-avant-le-25-juin), while the [identification file itself still awaits instituting texts](https://isoko.gov.bi/?procedures=fichier-national-de-lidentification-carte-nationale-didentite). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-06 — [the regulator required every subscriber to regularise SIM identification against the national identity card, with non-compliant lines deactivated from 25 June 2026](https://www.agenceecofin.com/actualites-numerique/0506-139050-burundi-identification-des-cartes-sim-obligatoire-avant-le-25-juin), under a ministerial ordinance of 13 January 2026. It is the first instrument on the ledger to make the national identity card a condition of holding a service.<br><br>Underneath it the register is not yet constituted: [the official procedure page for the Fichier National d'Identification states that further texts remain to be adopted to institute the file](https://isoko.gov.bi/?procedures=fichier-national-de-lidentification-carte-nationale-didentite). An obligation to identify against a register precedes the instrument establishing the register.</details> | Advanced |
| Cameroon | [Decret n. 2025/059 on identity titles](https://www.prc.cm/files/e7/0f/3b/6ccd45456387ac207b4797c196318209.pdf) and the [2024 civil-registration law](https://www.biometricupdate.com/202501/new-civil-registration-law-refocuses-cameroons-push-for-sdg-16-9-legal-id-target) are both in force and unamended. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The decree was [signed on 28 February 2025 governing five identity-title classes and is in force, unamended](https://www.prc.cm/files/e7/0f/3b/6ccd45456387ac207b4797c196318209.pdf); its refugee and disability chapters are not captured in the base.<br><br>The [civil-registration law was enacted on 23 December 2024 in 130 sections, providing for electronic declaration, a national database, digital certificates and a unique personal identification number](https://www.biometricupdate.com/202501/new-civil-registration-law-refocuses-cameroons-push-for-sdg-16-9-legal-id-target), and is also unamended. Its text is not held, so what the unique personal identification number obliges, and how it relates to the biometric card, is not checkable from anything on file.</details> | No change |
| Central African Republic | Government [terms of reference scope legal reform covering civil status with digital identification](https://www.finances.gouv.cf/sites/default/files/inline-files/TDRS%20Assistance%20Jurique%20Renforcement%20du%20cadre%20l%C3%A9gal.pdf); no such law is held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [terms of reference cover personal data, cybersecurity, electronic communications and civil status with digital identification](https://www.finances.gouv.cf/sites/default/files/inline-files/TDRS%20Assistance%20Jurique%20Renforcement%20du%20cadre%20l%C3%A9gal.pdf). Two of the instruments they scope were made in early 2024 - [the data protection law](https://arcep.cf/fr/images/documents/reglementation/lois/Loi_24_001_portant_protection_des_donnes_a_caractere_personnel.PDF) and [the cybersecurity law](https://arcep.cf/fr/images/documents/reglementation/lois/Loi_24_002_relative_a_la_cyber_securite.pdf) - and nothing records the identification leg being drafted.<br><br>What exists instead is an obligation without a statute behind it on the record: the unique identification number is [recorded as made mandatory in a budget-support completion report](https://www.afdb.org/sites/default/files/documents/projects-and-operations/central_african_republic-economic_and_social_reform_consolidation_support_programme_phase_i_pacres_i-_p-cf-k00-020-pcr-fevrier_2025.pdf), with no instrument named.</details> | No change, a first stated position with no earlier account behind it |
| Chad | The [2016 ordinance](https://www.anats.td/) founds the identity agency, and a [December 2025 decret](https://lendjampost.com/tchad-un-nouveau-decret-resserre-les-regles-dattribution-des-passeports-officiels/) makes it central to official document production. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The agency's own site names Ordonnance n. 001/PR/2016 as its founding instrument and states that its integrated population and secure-documents system, after enrolment and deduplication, assigns every enrolled individual a unique national identification number across 29 centres ([agency](https://www.anats.td/)).<br><br>Decret n. 3004 of 5 December 2025 replaced the 1997 regime for official passports: it abolishes the special passport, resets the beneficiary list, removes the automatic entitlement of spouses, adds explicit refusal grounds and a return obligation at end of function, and makes the agency the central actor for personalisation and physical production ([decret](https://lendjampost.com/tchad-un-nouveau-decret-resserre-les-regles-dattribution-des-passeports-officiels/)).<br><br>The statistics institute's bulletin sets the civil-registration frame the identity system sits on — the 2013 civil registration law, the 2016 ordinance and a 2017 decret ([bulletin](https://www.inseed.td/index.php/component/jdownloads/send/6-documents-et-publications/461-bulletin-trimestriel-d-information-09-inseed)).</details> | Advanced |
| Comoros | The biometric identity card is [instituted by loi n 14-023/AU, promulgated by decret n 14-137/PR](https://justice.gouv.km/texte/decret-n14-137-pr-portant-promulgation-de-la-loi-n14-023-au-instituant-la-carte-didentite-comorienne-numerique/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The justice ministry's legislation index [carries the promulgating decree and a download link to the law instituting the digital, biometric Comorian identity card](https://justice.gouv.km/texte/decret-n14-137-pr-portant-promulgation-de-la-loi-n14-023-au-instituant-la-carte-didentite-comorienne-numerique/). This is the first record in the base of a statute behind the card, which until now stood on procurement and operational accounts alone.<br><br>The index dates the decree to 28 August 2024 while numbering it in the 2014 series and dating the law it promulgates to 24 June 2014. The decree's own text is not held, so the discrepancy is reported here rather than settled.<br><br>What the statute requires - enrolment duties, the data it may hold, who may read it - is not on file, so nothing here says what the card's legal basis actually obliges.</details> | No change, a first record of the promulgating decree with the law's own text still not held |
| Cote d'Ivoire | The only identification instrument on file is [decret n 2017-193 on subscriber identification](https://anssi.gouv.ci/documents/43/DECRET3.PDF); no foundational digital-identity statute is held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The decree was [made on 22 March 2017 and requires identification of subscribers to public telecommunications and information-technology services and of cybercafe users](https://anssi.gouv.ci/documents/43/DECRET3.PDF). Nothing in the window touched it.<br><br>It is not a digital-identity law. The national biometric register, the identification number and the credentials issued against them run throughout this report, and the base holds no statute, ordinance or decree establishing any of them, setting who may query the register, or stating what a holder may refuse. That absence sits under every identity row here.</details> | No change |
| Djibouti | Identification is governed by a [2019 law creating the register of natural persons and the national identification number](https://www.journalofficiel.dj/texte-juridique/loi-n39-an-19-8eme-l-portant-identification-des-personnes-physiques-en-republique-de-djibouti-creation-du-numero-national-didentification-et-etablissement-dun-registre-national/), unchanged in the period. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The founding law [makes the Registre National des Personnes Physiques the unique reference for identifying individuals and for producing every secure national document - identity card, passport, voter card, driving licence - with administrative management at the population directorate and technical and security management at the state information-systems agency](https://www.journalofficiel.dj/texte-juridique/loi-n39-an-19-8eme-l-portant-identification-des-personnes-physiques-en-republique-de-djibouti-creation-du-numero-national-didentification-et-etablissement-dun-registre-national/).<br><br>Its [implementing decree defines the identification number as a digital instrument combining civil-status and personal data, and makes its use compulsory across public, parapublic and private administration for identification and third-party data exchange](https://www.journalofficiel.dj/texte-juridique/decret-n2020-150-pr-mi-portant-composition-caracteristique-et-champ-dapplication-lies-au-numero-national-didentification-nni/).<br><br>Nothing amended either in the period; what moved is what runs on them.</details> | No change |
| DR Congo | A [bill on the identification of natural persons was tabled at the National Assembly bureau on 12 June 2026](https://actualite.cd/2026/06/12/assemblee-nationale-venance-eyanga-et-patrick-namazihana-deposent-une-proposition-de-loi). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-06-12 - the bill was [tabled: it would set one legal frame for identification data and its collection, processing, retention and protection, impose security and interoperability requirements, and connect civil-registry, migration and health databases](https://actualite.cd/2026/06/12/assemblee-nationale-venance-eyanga-et-patrick-namazihana-deposent-une-proposition-de-loi). No committee report or plenary date is held.<br><br>It runs alongside rather than through the twenty-year identity concession already signed, and nothing on file connects the two. A statutory frame arriving after the platform it would govern is the sequence here, and no source states which would prevail on retention, access or foreign processing.</details> | Advanced |
| Egypt | A [member's proposal for a biometric national identity card for children from age five was discussed at two workshops](https://www.youm7.com/story/2026/4/19/%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AA%D9%86%D8%B3%D9%8A%D9%82%D9%8A%D8%A9-%D8%AA%D8%B9%D9%82%D8%AF-%D9%88%D8%B1%D8%B4%D8%AA%D9%8A-%D8%B9%D9%85%D9%84-%D9%84%D9%85%D9%86%D8%A7%D9%82%D8%B4%D8%A9-%D9%85%D9%82%D8%AA%D8%B1%D8%AD-%D8%A5%D8%B5%D8%AF%D8%A7%D8%B1-%D8%A8%D8%B7%D8%A7%D9%82%D8%A9-%D8%B1%D9%82%D9%85-%D9%82%D9%88%D9%85%D9%8A/7382985); no bill is tabled. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Nothing of the kind was on record at the window's opening. A [member's proposal for a national identity card for children carrying a photograph from age five, requiring amendment of the Child Law, was discussed at two workshops, with recommendations including a unified national child-data platform, a full biometric identification system for children, and linkage between health, education and other government databases](https://www.youm7.com/story/2026/4/19/%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AA%D9%86%D8%B3%D9%8A%D9%82%D9%8A%D8%A9-%D8%AA%D8%B9%D9%82%D8%AF-%D9%88%D8%B1%D8%B4%D8%AA%D9%8A-%D8%B9%D9%85%D9%84-%D9%84%D9%85%D9%86%D8%A7%D9%82%D8%B4%D8%A9-%D9%85%D9%82%D8%AA%D8%B1%D8%AD-%D8%A5%D8%B5%D8%AF%D8%A7%D8%B1-%D8%A8%D8%B7%D8%A7%D9%82%D8%A9-%D8%B1%D9%82%D9%85-%D9%82%D9%88%D9%85%D9%8A/7382985). Participants also recommended a strict legal framework for protecting the resulting data.<br><br>No bill has been tabled. The country's identity layer - a digital identity app, a financial identity system, a biometric authentication pilot and facial verification at mobile-line enrolment - all now operates with no statute of its own, and the only identity legislation on the record is a proposal to extend it to children.</details> | Advanced |
| Equatorial Guinea | Identity rests on a [1957 registry law](https://unstats.un.org/unsd/demographic-social/products/dyb/documents/metadata/2025/Equatoria_Guinea-2025-VT-Metadata.pdf) and a [regional passport regulation](https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/noticias/reglamento_de_aplicacion_del_decreto_de_expedicion_del_pasaporte_biometrico_cemac), with reform under review. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The statistical questionnaire records the position exactly: [civil registration still runs on a registry law inherited from 1957](https://unstats.un.org/unsd/demographic-social/products/dyb/documents/metadata/2025/Equatoria_Guinea-2025-VT-Metadata.pdf). The biometric credential above it is regional, governed by the [regulation applying the CEMAC biometric passport decree](https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/noticias/reglamento_de_aplicacion_del_decreto_de_expedicion_del_pasaporte_biometrico_cemac).<br><br>Reform is under consideration rather than enacted: an [interministerial council reviewed reform of the 1957 law in November 2025](https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/noticias/osa_nsue_preside_un_nuevo_consejo_interministerial), and a [draft decree would recode the tax identification number for individuals as well as companies](https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/noticias/el_consejo_interministerial_analiza_nuevas_medidas_para_reforzar_el_empleo_la_fiscalidad_y_la_cooperacion_internacional).</details> | No change |
| Eritrea | No digital-identity statute exists; the closest instrument is the 1992 nationality proclamation ([proclamation](https://www.refworld.org/legal/legislation/natlegbod/1992/en/14094)). <details><summary>Full record</summary>No dedicated digital-identity statute was located. The closest identifiable legal instrument is the Nationality Proclamation 21/1992, still in force, which provides the legal basis for nationality certification through the Department of Internal Affairs and contains no digital-identity, electronic-identity, biometric or electronic-transactions framework ([proclamation](https://www.refworld.org/legal/legislation/natlegbod/1992/en/14094)).<br><br>Identity cards are nonetheless required by other systems: anti-money-laundering law obliges financial institutions to identify every customer ([proclamation](https://erhcb.com/pdf/AML_CFT_PROCLAMATION_%20OF_STATE_OF_ERITREA_2014.pdf)), and a national identity card is required before a passport is issued ([country guidance](https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/eritrea-knowledge-base-profile/eritrea-knowledge-base-profile)).<br><br>So the card carries statutory weight without a statute defining it, and nothing regulates what data it holds or who may read it.</details> | No change |
| Eswatini | Identity rests on the [Identification Order of 1998](https://eswatinilii.org/akn/sz/act/order-in-council/1998/4/eng@1998-12-01); no digital identity Act exists, and three conventions went to senators for ratification in July 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The King's Order in Council of 1998 establishes the population register and the personal identity number, and is the founding legal instrument behind national identity; no dedicated digital-identity Act is held ([Order](https://eswatinilii.org/akn/sz/act/order-in-council/1998/4/eng@1998-12-01)).<br><br>The ICT minister presented the African Union Malabo Convention on cyber security and personal data protection, the Budapest Convention on cybercrime and the Tampere Convention to senators for ratification on 10 July 2026 ([workshop](https://www.linkedin.com/posts/ministry-of-ict-eswatini_the-minister-of-information-communications-activity-7481421459043770369-IcuH)).<br><br>SADC ICT ministers had urged member states to consider ratifying Malabo in September 2019 ([ministerial](https://www.sadc.int/sites/default/files/2021-06/Media_Statement__ICT_Information_Transport_and_Met_meeting__2019.pdf)). Seven years later it is at the presentation stage.<br><br>The digital identity itself remains planned under the Digital Eswatini project, with no legal basis on file for it.</details> | Advanced, on treaty ratification rather than on any identity statute |
| Ethiopia | The 2023 identification proclamation became a binding operational requirement across banking, with migration to the new verification service mandated ([decision](https://capitalethiopia.com/2025/12/10/nbe/)). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The central bank and the national identity programme began harmonising all bank customer account numbers with the national digital identity, mandating migration from the first to the second verification service for all new and existing accounts with a deadline of 30 March 2026 ([decision](https://capitalethiopia.com/2025/12/10/nbe/)).<br><br>The government stated in December 2025 that 30 million citizens had been registered and that the identity had been carried into Digital Ethiopia 2030 with integration planned down to the lowest administrative levels ([statement](https://www.gcs.gov.et/en/2025/12/22/the-fayda-digital-id-has-been-implemented-as-a-key-component-of-the-digital-2025-strategy/)).<br><br>The statute itself was not amended in the window; what moved is the weight placed on it ([Digital Identification Proclamation 1284/2023](https://ethiodata.et/wp-content/uploads/2023/09/Ethiopian-Digital-Identification-Proclamation-No.-1284_2023-1.pdf)).<br><br>The base holds no statutory limit on state access to identity data and no redress route for a registrant, which is carried separately as a not-held position.</details> | Advanced |
| Gabon | The identity framework [rests on two named texts](https://www.gabonreview.com/identite-digitale-le-gabon-franchit-un-cap-vers-une-gouvernance-inclusive-et-securisee/), a law on the personal identification number and one on the civil registry centre, neither with a date. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Both texts are named as the legal basis of the national digital identity system, with no adoption, promulgation or gazette date for either ([framework](https://www.gabonreview.com/identite-digitale-le-gabon-franchit-un-cap-vers-une-gouvernance-inclusive-et-securisee/)).<br><br>They were named again in the stocktake of legal identity projects, which lists three separate systems resting on them ([legal identity stocktake](https://www.gabonreview.com/identification-legale-et-digitale-le-gabon-enclenche-la-mise-en-oeuvre-de-trois-projets-strategiques/)).<br><br>Neither text has a number, date or gazette reference on file, so the identity card, the biometric register and the digital identity system all operate on a basis the base cannot read.</details> | No change |
| Gambia | The [digital identity strategy for 2023 to 2028](https://www.uneca.org/stories/validation-of-the-gambia's-national-digital-id-and-digital-transformation-strategy) is in force and now carries a delivered system, with no statute governing it. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The strategy was validated at a supported workshop in May 2024 and provided for a system that has since been built ([validation](https://www.uneca.org/stories/validation-of-the-gambia's-national-digital-id-and-digital-transformation-strategy), [strategy](https://policies.gov.gm/d/9c7cefe5-80cf-11ef-b086-029254d29bb1)).<br><br>What the base does not hold is the legal basis for issuing identity cards outside the country, which the opposition alleged was unlawful in August 2025 for issuance in Mauritania, Congo and Togo; no official response, regulation or registry statement has followed ([allegation](https://thepoint.gm/africa/gambia/headlines/udp-raises-alarm-over-govt-unlawful-issuance-of-id-cards-abroad)).</details> | No change |
| Ghana | [Amendment regulations in force from June 2026](https://nia.gov.gh/wp-content/uploads/LI-2523-national-identity-register-amendment-regulations-2026.pdf) make biometric-only verification law and bar visual inspection and photocopying of the card. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The regulations were signed on 2 March 2026, gazetted on 27 March and came into force on 9 June, with penalties running to 2,000 penalty units for an organisation ([regulations](https://nia.gov.gh/wp-content/uploads/LI-2523-national-identity-register-amendment-regulations-2026.pdf)); the gazetted artefact is an image scan with no text layer, so the regulation is not machine-readable ([regulations](https://nia.gov.gh/wp-content/uploads/LI-2523-national-identity-register-amendment-regulations-2026.pdf), [agency statement](https://www.facebook.com/officialNIAGH/posts/press-releasegovernment-makes-biometric-verification-mandatory-for-ghana-card-ve/1522136326623174/)).<br><br>A fee schedule made under separate 2025 regulations took effect on 2 February 2026: first registration free below 25 and GH¢30 at 25 and over, replacement GH¢200 and record updates free, across 276 district offices, the agency stating it was approved by Parliament rather than introduced by the agency ([fees](https://www.myjoyonline.com/revised-ghana-card-fees-approved-by-parliament-not-unilaterally-introduced-nia/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Guinea-Bissau | No identity statute is held; enrolment runs on a [contracted production network](https://ang.gw/regioes-ministro-da-justica-inaugura-centro-de-producao-de-bilhete-de-identidade-em-bolama/) and the regional card scheme. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The base holds no Guinean law governing the identity register or the biometric card. What it holds is delivery: an [eighteenth identity card production centre opened in June 2026](https://ang.gw/regioes-ministro-da-justica-inaugura-centro-de-producao-de-bilhete-de-identidade-em-bolama/) and a [third regional enrolment centre in February](https://www.semlex.com/2026/03/09/inauguration-du-centre-denrolement-de-sao-domingos/).<br><br>The governing frame is regional rather than national: [a consultation opened in February 2026 on a roadmap from national identification systems to the regional biometric card](https://www.ecowas.int/ecowas-holds-virtual-consultation-with-member-states-for-the-development-of-a-roadmap-from-foundational-identification-systems-to-the-ecowas-national-biometric-id-enbic/).</details> | No change |
| Kenya | The High Court recognised a [registered phone number as a personal digital identifier](https://www.biometricupdate.com/202603/kenyan-high-court-ruling-recognizes-phone-numbers-as-digital-id) and gave the Attorney-General six months to legislate. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The judgment also bars recycling of dormant numbers without consent; the window was still open as at 12 August 2026 with nothing tabled and closes about September ([judgment](https://www.biometricupdate.com/202603/kenyan-high-court-ruling-recognizes-phone-numbers-as-digital-id)).<br><br>Analysis of it argues the judgment should produce a coordinated number-recycling policy binding banks, savings societies and platforms that keep relying on numbers after reassignment ([analysis](https://www.kictanet.or.ke/https-www-kictanet-or-ke-phone-number-recycling-digital-identity-kenya/)).<br><br>It is the only instrument in the base that defines a digital identifier in law, and it arrived through litigation rather than through Parliament.</details> | Advanced |
