| Lesotho | The [National Identity Cards Act 2011 is the founding statute](https://lesotholii.org/akn/ls/act/2011/9/eng@2011-03-21); an electoral Bill now ties voter registration to the identity number. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2011-03-21 — [Act 9 of 2011 establishes the National Identity Register and the identity-card regime](https://lesotholii.org/akn/ls/act/2011/9/eng@2011-03-21), commenced in 2013.<br><br>2026-06-04 — [the Senate adopted its legislation committee’s report on the electoral amendment Bill, which ties voter registration and verification to the national identity number](https://senate.parliament.ls/2026/07/13/hansard-4-june-2026/).<br><br>2026-06-29 — [the home affairs ministry opened a workshop on a systems architecture and modernisation framework, setting out the legal and institutional implications of the reforms proposed](https://www.gov.ls/development/ministry-of-home-affairs-advances-digital-transformation-of-national-identity-system/).<br><br>No amendment to the 2011 Act is before Parliament.</details> | Advanced |
| Liberia | The [2011 Act](https://citizenshiprightsafrica.org/wp-content/uploads/2021/11/Liberia-NIR-Act-2011.pdf) is the enacting statute for the biometric register and remains unamended. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [National Identification Registry Act](https://citizenshiprightsafrica.org/wp-content/uploads/2021/11/Liberia-NIR-Act-2011.pdf), approved 1 August 2011, repeals the earlier decree, creates the autonomous registry, and makes the National Biometric Identification System, the biometric card and the Social Security Number the primary government identity number.<br><br>What has moved since is executive rather than legislative: [Executive Order 147](https://emansion.gov.lr/sites/default/files/documents/Executive%20Order%20147%20%20-%20Mandatory%20Enrollment%20of%20all%20Citizens%20and%20the%20use%20of%20NBIC_0.pdf) of April 2025 compels enrolment and verification, with data handling bound back to Part IX of the same Act. No amending statute is on the record.</details> | No change |
| Libya | [Law 8 of 2014](https://www.gia.gov.ly/wp-content/uploads/2023/02/%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%82%D8%A7%D9%86%D9%88%D9%86-%D8%B1%D9%82%D9%85-8-%D9%84%D8%B3%D9%86%D8%A9-2014%D9%85-%D8%A8%D8%B4%D8%A3%D9%86-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B1%D9%82%D9%85-%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%88%D8%B7%D9%86%D9%8A..pdf) creates the twelve-digit national number, mandatory from birth for every state service. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [law](https://www.gia.gov.ly/wp-content/uploads/2023/02/%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%82%D8%A7%D9%86%D9%88%D9%86-%D8%B1%D9%82%D9%85-8-%D9%84%D8%B3%D9%86%D8%A9-2014%D9%85-%D8%A8%D8%B4%D8%A3%D9%86-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B1%D9%82%D9%85-%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%88%D8%B7%D9%86%D9%8A..pdf) is the statutory basis for everything built since: a twelve-digit number assigned from birth and required to obtain any state service.<br><br>The institution beneath it is older — the [civil registry authority created by decree in 1998](https://security-legislation.ly/ar/latest-laws/%D9%82%D8%B1%D8%A7%D8%B1-%D8%B1%D9%82%D9%85-115-%D9%84%D8%B3%D9%86%D8%A9-1998-%D8%A8%D8%B4%D8%A3%D9%86-%D8%A5%D9%86%D8%B4%D8%A7%D8%A1-%D9%85%D8%B5%D9%84%D8%AD%D8%A9-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A3%D8%AD%D9%88/) — and the competences were redistributed by a [2018 presidential council decision](https://lawsociety.ly/legislation/%d9%82%d8%b1%d8%a7%d8%b1-%d8%b1%d9%82%d9%85-130-%d9%84%d8%b3%d9%86%d8%a9-2018-%d9%85-%d8%a8%d8%b4%d8%a3%d9%86-%d8%a5%d8%b9%d8%a7%d8%af%d8%a9-%d8%aa%d9%88%d8%b2%d9%8a%d8%b9-%d8%a7%d8%ae%d8%aa%d8%b5/) that gave the information authority the national database. No amending statute has been passed.</details> | No change |
| Madagascar | Two identification laws cleared constitutional review inside the window: the [founding 2025 law](https://www.hcc.gov.mg/?p=9621) and a 2026 special birth-registration law. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Constitutional Court declared the law on identification of natural persons constitutional in August 2025, after adoption by the National Assembly on 3 July and the Senate on 4 July ([decision](https://www.hcc.gov.mg/?p=9621)).<br><br>A second law, for derogatory registration of adults without birth certificates, was adopted on 21 April 2026 ([adoption](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news/2304-54942-madagascar-passes-law-to-expand-birth-registration-via-biometric-census)) and cleared for promulgation on 13 May 2026 ([decision](https://www.hcc.gov.mg/?p=9952)).<br><br>Enrolment under the framework opened in January 2026 ([launch](https://prea.gov.mg/presidence/lancement-officiel-de-lenrolement-biometrique-izaho-tokana/)). Neither promulgated text is held; the base carries the Court's decisions on them.</details> | Advanced |
| Malawi | The [National Registration Act is under review](https://cajnewsafrica.com/2026/07/13/malawi-outlines-plans-to-transition-to-digital-ids/) and [registration service fees were raised](https://malawi24.com/2026/08/17/nrb-hikes-registration-fees/) by regulations gazetted in July 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>A consultative meeting under the acceleration project reviewed the registration Act and the marriage and family relations statute, the bureau stating the aim is a modern and secure civil registration and identification system; no bill, draft or timetable is on record ([review](https://cajnewsafrica.com/2026/07/13/malawi-outlines-plans-to-transition-to-digital-ids/)).<br><br>Amendment regulations gazetted on 31 July 2026 raised fees for identity card replacement, electronic know-your-customer verification, civil registration and marriage services; the individual levels and their previous values are not carried in the record held, so the size of the increase is not established, and the electronic verification charge is a charge on third parties for using the identity system ([fees](https://malawi24.com/2026/08/17/nrb-hikes-registration-fees/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Mali | The [national identification number was instituted by law 06-040 of 2006](https://sgg-mali.ml/JO/2006/mali-jo-2006-28.pdf), with [decree 06-442 fixing how it applies](https://sgg-mali.ml/JO/2006/mali-jo-2006-33.pdf). <details><summary>Full record</summary>Both founding texts are now held ([law](https://sgg-mali.ml/JO/2006/mali-jo-2006-28.pdf), [decree](https://sgg-mali.ml/JO/2006/mali-jo-2006-33.pdf)).<br><br>They are twenty years old and the base holds no amendment and no enrolment, coverage or issuance figure against them, so the identifier's legal footing is settled and its reach is not. The agency for securing civil status documents was created by ordinance in February 2025 ([agency](https://sgg-mali.ml/JO/2025/mali-jo-2025-05.pdf)).</details> | No change |
| Mauritania | The [civil status code of 2011](https://www.coursupreme.mr/docs/loi0032011.pdf) created the national population register, and [law 2025-011 replaced certain of its provisions](https://natlex.ilo.org/dyn/natlex2/natlex2/files/download/117696/MRT-117696.pdf) in February 2025. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The 2011 law repealed and replaced the 1996 code and is the instrument behind the national population register ([code](https://www.coursupreme.mr/docs/loi0032011.pdf)); law n. 2025-011 of 24 February 2025 repeals and replaces certain of its provisions ([amendment](https://natlex.ilo.org/dyn/natlex2/natlex2/files/download/117696/MRT-117696.pdf)).<br><br>Both texts are held in Arabic and the base does not hold a consolidated version, so which provisions the 2025 law changed is not established from the record.</details> | Advanced |
| Mauritius | The [1985 identity card Act](https://lawsofmauritius.govmu.org/portal/viewlegislationdocument/web/?docnumber=&doctitle=TmF0aW9uYWwgSWRlbnRpdHkgQ2FyZCBBY3Q%3D&doctype=act) carries the digital card through 2024 regulations, and a [Road Traffic (Amendment) Bill](https://lexpress.mu/s/avinash-ramtohul-batir-un-avenir-ou-les-services-publics-suivent-levolution-technologique-562186) would give the digital driving licence legal recognition. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Act has been in force since April 1986 and is the statutory basis for the card ([Act](https://lawsofmauritius.govmu.org/portal/viewlegislationdocument/web/?docnumber=&doctitle=TmF0aW9uYWwgSWRlbnRpdHkgQ2FyZCBBY3Q%3D&doctype=act)); the regulations of February 2024 set the terms on which the card and the mobile identity are issued and read ([regulations](https://paps.mnis.govmu.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/05/29_The-National-IC-Reg-2024-.pdf)).<br><br>The Road Traffic (Amendment) Bill would [make the digital driving licence an official electronic version equivalent to the traditional one and let holders settle fixed penalties online, across more than 800,000 licence holders](https://lexpress.mu/s/avinash-ramtohul-batir-un-avenir-ou-les-services-publics-suivent-levolution-technologique-562186); the Bill text is not held and no enactment date is stated.<br><br>The Finance Act 2026, assented in August, carries the statutory changes behind the budget's identity and registry measures ([Act](https://mauritiusassembly.govmu.org/mauritiusassembly/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/Act1426.pdf)).<br><br>The Electronic Transactions (Amendment) Act 2026 was enacted in May ([Act](https://mauritiusassembly.govmu.org/mauritiusassembly/wp-content/uploads/2026/05/Act0526.pdf)). A forty-year-old card statute is doing the work, extended by regulation rather than replaced.</details> | Advanced |
| Morocco | [Law 72-18](https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/mor204689.pdf) creates the population and social registries, the civil and social digital identifier and the register agency, deferring force to implementing texts. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The statute was promulgated by dahir in August 2020 and creates the national population register, the unified social register, the civil and social digital identifier and the national register agency ([law](https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/mor204689.pdf)).<br><br>Articles 19 and 22 carve out defence and security processing, and article 45 defers force to implementing texts. The base holds no implementing decree, so the identifier's legal operation cannot be established from the statute alone.</details> | Advanced |
| Mozambique | The digital certification framework is unamended; the ICT institute [signed a memorandum with Brazil on digital certification on 13 February 2026](https://intic.gov.mz/intic-e-iti-brasil-assinam-memorando-de-entendimento-para-cooperar-na-area-de-certificacao-digital-em-mocambique/) covering the root certification authority behind it. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-02-13 — the national ICT institute [signed a memorandum with Brazil's information technology institute to cooperate on digital certification, digital-identity technologies and the root certification authority](https://intic.gov.mz/intic-e-iti-brasil-assinam-memorando-de-entendimento-para-cooperar-na-area-de-certificacao-digital-em-mocambique/) underpinning the national digital certification system.<br><br>The law behind it did not move: that system rests on a 2019 decree under a 2017 statute, and neither was amended in the window. A memorandum is a cooperation instrument, not a legal one, and no term, budget or deliverable is published for it.</details> | No change, the 2017 and 2019 instruments stand and the movement was cooperation rather than law |
| Namibia | The [Civil Registration and Identification Act 13 of 2024](https://namiblii.org/akn/na/act/2024/13/eng@2024-12-30) is the statutory basis for a single civil register and a unique identity number, and a [gazette notice withdrawing older documents](https://informante.web.na/?p=398234) has not issued. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Act was assented in December 2024 as the basis for a single civil register, a unique identity number and identity-data confidentiality, and was read by officials in July 2026 as permitting institutions to accept either the physical card or its digital version, with disclosure conditional on the holder's consent; the commencement date is not established in this base ([Act](https://namiblii.org/akn/na/act/2024/13/eng@2024-12-30), [reading](https://informante.web.na/?p=398234)).<br><br>The notice that will end the validity of pre-independence cards, older documents and current machine-readable cards has not issued, so until it does the September 2026 card is additive rather than replacing anything ([notice](https://informante.web.na/?p=398234)).</details> | Advanced |
| Niger | An [ordonnance of April 2026 with its application decree](https://www.lesahel.org/wuri-niger-restructure-une-opportunite-pour-une-identification-universelle-de-la-population-au-service-de-linclusion-et-du-un-puissant-facteur-de-developpement-inclusif-et-une-dimension/) replaced the civil-status regime, and a [National Nationality Register](https://www.lesahel.org/wuri-niger-restructure-une-opportunite-pour-une-identification-universelle-de-la-population-au-service-de-linclusion-et-du-un-puissant-facteur-de-developpement-inclusif-et-une-dimension/) was instituted under the justice ministry. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The ordonnance of 22 April 2026 and its application decree of 18 May are in force, and no gazetted text is held ([ordonnance](https://www.lesahel.org/wuri-niger-restructure-une-opportunite-pour-une-identification-universelle-de-la-population-au-service-de-linclusion-et-du-un-puissant-facteur-de-developpement-inclusif-et-une-dimension/)).<br><br>The same ordonnance institutes a national nationality register under the justice ministry ([register](https://www.lesahel.org/wuri-niger-restructure-une-opportunite-pour-une-identification-universelle-de-la-population-au-service-de-linclusion-et-du-un-puissant-facteur-de-developpement-inclusif-et-une-dimension/)). Between them the civil, nationality and population registers are set up by one instrument, which is a cleaner legal basis than most units in this base have and is not yet matched by an operating system.</details> | Advanced |
| Nigeria | The [NIMC Act 2026](https://www.vanguardngr.com/2026/08/nimc-act-2026-experts-demand-transparency-stronger-data-protection/) makes the identity number the foundational credential, and a [legal reading](https://www.mondaq.com/nigeria/constitutional-administrative-law/1829270/the-national-identity-management-commission-establishment-act-2026-key-new-provisions) puts mandatory use across passports, banking, telecommunications and land dealings. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Act replaces the 2007 statute, recognises the identity number as the foundational credential under a one-person-one-identity policy, incorporates the data-protection regime and lets the commission prosecute identity fraud; the gazetted text was still not publicly available about six weeks after assent, so neither the expansion reading nor the counter-reading can be checked against it ([Act](https://www.vanguardngr.com/2026/08/nimc-act-2026-experts-demand-transparency-stronger-data-protection/), [transformation](https://www.thisdaylive.com/2026/07/22/shettima-nigerias-digital-transformation-under-tinubu-unrivalled-hails-ongoing-reforms-by-nimc-management/), [analysis](https://punchng.com/pros-cons-of-amended-nimc-act/)).<br><br>On a law firm's reading the commission becomes the root certification authority for a national public key infrastructure and the national biometric-data repository, use of the number is made mandatory across four named sectors, the Act states that proof of identity is not proof of eligibility or citizenship, and unauthorised database access carries penalties of at least NGN10m or five years for an individual ([reading](https://www.mondaq.com/nigeria/constitutional-administrative-law/1829270/the-national-identity-management-commission-establishment-act-2026-key-new-provisions)).</details> | Advanced |
| Rwanda | A [single digital identification law](https://www.newtimes.co.rw/article/34398/news/technology/govt-sets-2027-deadline-for-full-shift-to-digital-id) writes a June 2027 sunset of all legacy documents into statute, and the law's number and enactment date are not held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The sunset is written into the law rather than set by administrative decision, which is what makes the enrolment count a legal deadline rather than a target ([deadline](https://www.newtimes.co.rw/article/34398/news/technology/govt-sets-2027-deadline-for-full-shift-to-digital-id), [enrolment](https://www.biometricupdate.com/202508/rwanda-national-identification-agency-begins-digital-id-enrollment)).<br><br>The law's number and enactment date are not held, so the base cannot say what it provides on correction, appeal or exclusion for anyone unenrolled when legacy documents cease to be valid.</details> | Advanced |
| Sao Tome and Principe | An [aggregated identification document law](https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/099102025185177863/pdf/P177158-d0ebd79d-6646-4e92-b9ae-1183b632c15a.pdf) was flagged as urgent by a lender mission in 2025, its absence stated as a potential constraint on data collection, and no later source confirms passage. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The mission flagged review, public consultation, finalisation and approval as urgent ([law](https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/099102025185177863/pdf/P177158-d0ebd79d-6646-4e92-b9ae-1183b632c15a.pdf)).<br><br>A year later nothing records movement. The foundational digital identity it would govern has live prototypes and a system integrator engaged, so the build is running ahead of the statute — the same order this frame finds in most units.</details> | No change |
| Senegal | A [bill on computerising civil-status management](https://www.child-identity.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/07/PRESENTATIONETATSENEGAL-Aliou-Sall.pdf) remains listed among outstanding steps, validated but not enacted, and the register-reconstitution decree is likewise unsigned. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The bill appears among outstanding steps in the agency's own August 2025 presentation and again in the June 2026 programme closing report ([bill](https://www.child-identity.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/07/PRESENTATIONETATSENEGAL-Aliou-Sall.pdf), [closure](https://aps.sn/le-programme-nekkal-a-permis-dimportantes-avancees-dans-la-modernisation-de-letat-civil-selon-un-responsable/)).<br><br>Validated is not enacted. The digitisation programme it would govern has meanwhile closed its external financing, deployed software across most centres and loaded millions of records, so the legal basis trails the system by a wide margin.</details> | No change |
| Seychelles | The identity-card statute was [amended in 2023](https://www.gazette.sc/sites/default/files/2023-11/Act%2022%20-%202023%20-%20Miscellaneous%20Amendments%20Act%202023.pdf) on identity numbers and disclosure; no statute names or governs the SeyID credential itself. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Part 13 of the 2023 omnibus Act amends the National Identity Cards Act on the national identity number requirement, cards for non-resident citizens and the rules on disclosing information, and does not create the digital credential ([Act](https://www.gazette.sc/sites/default/files/2023-11/Act%2022%20-%202023%20-%20Miscellaneous%20Amendments%20Act%202023.pdf)).<br><br>The December 2025 cybercrimes amendment defines subscriber information — identity, address and billing or service details — for evidential purposes, the strongest in-window legal movement touching identity data ([amendment](https://www.gazette.sc/sites/default/files/2025-12/Act%2023%202025%20-%20Cybercrimes%20and%20other%20Related%20Crimes%20%28Amendment%29%20Act%202025.pdf)).<br><br>So the physical card has a statute and the digital credential has none: SeyID rests on administrative practice, which is a different kind of guarantee.</details> | Advanced |
| Sierra Leone | The [National Civil Registration Act 2016](https://www.parliament.gov.sl/uploads/acts/The%20Nationa%20Civil%20Registration%20Act,%202016.pdf) is the governing statute, with a replacement Bill [validated in November 2025](https://thecalabashnewspaper.com/ncra-concludes-final-national-validation-of-draft-civil-registration-and-identification-bill/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The 2016 Act establishes the National Civil Registration Authority as an independent body with its own board and a confidentiality duty ([Act](https://www.parliament.gov.sl/uploads/acts/The%20Nationa%20Civil%20Registration%20Act,%202016.pdf)); a draft Civil Registration and Identification Bill completed final national validation on 19 November 2025 and is not yet before Parliament ([Bill](https://thecalabashnewspaper.com/ncra-concludes-final-national-validation-of-draft-civil-registration-and-identification-bill/)).<br><br>The subscriber regulations phase-out of unregistered cards was announced on 30 July 2026, linking registration to the national identification number, and the instrument's own text is not held ([regulations](https://sierraloaded.sl/local/natca-phase-out-unregistered-national-fraud/)). Government conceded on 4 August 2026 that the linkage is incomplete ([linkage](https://sierraleoneconcordtimes.com/government-unveils-multi-agency-strategy-to-tackle-rising-cyber-fraud/)).<br><br>That concession matters because the fraud response recorded elsewhere rests on registration and identity verification as its principal control, and the control is admitted to be partial.</details> | Advanced |
| Somalia | The [2023 identity law](https://osagsomalia.com/media/documents/bulletins-contents/legislation/2023/SHarciga_Aqoonsiga_iyo_Diiwaangalinta_dadwaynaha.pdf) gained its [implementing regulation](https://www.osagsomalia.com/media/documents/bulletins-contents/cabinet-decisions/2024/Xeer-nidaamiyaha_Sharciga_Aqoonsiga_Diiwaan-galinta_Dadwaynaha.pdf) in June 2024, and a [civil status law](https://www.osagsomalia.com/media/documents/bulletins-contents/legislation/2026/sharciga_Dad-dhigista_iyo_Tirakoobka_Xaaladaha_Madaniga.pdf) followed in April 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Law No. 009 of March 2023 is the founding statute for the identity authority and the national digital identity system ([law](https://osagsomalia.com/media/documents/bulletins-contents/legislation/2023/SHarciga_Aqoonsiga_iyo_Diiwaangalinta_dadwaynaha.pdf)). Its implementing regulation was made by the Prime Minister on 8 June 2024 and gazetted that October ([regulation](https://www.osagsomalia.com/media/documents/bulletins-contents/cabinet-decisions/2024/Xeer-nidaamiyaha_Sharciga_Aqoonsiga_Diiwaan-galinta_Dadwaynaha.pdf)).<br><br>Law No. 46, published 5 April 2026, adds the civil-registration and vital-statistics layer beside the identity infrastructure ([law](https://www.osagsomalia.com/media/documents/bulletins-contents/legislation/2026/sharciga_Dad-dhigista_iyo_Tirakoobka_Xaaladaha_Madaniga.pdf)).<br><br>The base now holds all three primary texts. What it does not hold is any provision setting out how the identity system and the civil register are to be joined.</details> | Advanced |
| South Africa | Draft identification regulations were [gazetted on 4 May 2026](https://www.biometricupdate.com/202605/south-africa-gazettes-digital-id-draft-regulation-seeks-comments) and consulted to 6 June, while the [National Identification and Registration Bill is still before Parliament](https://www.polity.org.za/article/opinion-south-africas-digital-identity-regulations-a-governance-crossroads-2026-05-29). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-05-04 — draft amendments to the Identification Regulations were [gazetted](https://www.biometricupdate.com/202605/south-africa-gazettes-digital-id-draft-regulation-seeks-comments), with a 33-day comment period closing on 6 June 2026. They would create [voluntary digital-ID credentials with legal parity to the physical document](https://www.citizen.co.za/news/south-africa/smart-ids-go-digital-home-affairs-publishes-draft-rules/), grant accredited trusted entities API access to the population register under data-sharing agreements, and set three assurance levels, seven-year audit logs and private-sector enrolment points, with finalisation dated September 2026.<br><br>The primary legislation has not kept pace. The [National Identification and Registration Bill remains before Parliament](https://www.polity.org.za/article/opinion-south-africas-digital-identity-regulations-a-governance-crossroads-2026-05-29), named as the statutory foundation the regulations should rest on, and the record does not say whether its passage is now a precondition. A submission on the closing day asked the department to suspend the regulations pending that Bill, a published digital identity policy and demonstrated compliance with the data protection Act, on the ground that a biometric enrolment and real-time verification system is being built with no formal role for the Information Regulator. That submission is held as a supplied document with no published address, so it cannot be linked here.</details> | Advanced, the regulations moved ahead of the Bill they are said to rest on |
| South Sudan | The [Nationality Act 2011](https://mojca.gov.ss/wp-content/uploads/2023/03/Nationality-Act-2011.pdf) is the operative statute, creating the register of nationals and the certificate issued from it. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Act establishes the Directorate of Nationality, Passports and Immigration and requires it to maintain a Register of South Sudanese Nationals; the Nationality Certificate is the credential it creates ([Act](https://mojca.gov.ss/wp-content/uploads/2023/03/Nationality-Act-2011.pdf)).<br><br>No statute governs the national identity card, biometric capture or identity verification as such. The card programme recorded separately runs under an administrative mandate, not a digital identity law, and the base holds no bill that would supply one.</details> | No change |
| Sudan | The [Electronic Transactions Act 2007](https://cbos.gov.sd/sites/default/files/Electronic_Transactions_act_2007.pdf) and the [Civil Registry Act 2011](https://citizenshiprightsafrica.org/wp-content/uploads/2011/01/Sudan-Civil-Registration-Act-2011-EN.pdf) carry the digital identity; two later instruments it cites are not held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The 2007 Act covers digital signatures, electronic authentication and certification, and establishes an electronic licensing committee ([Act](https://cbos.gov.sd/sites/default/files/Electronic_Transactions_act_2007.pdf)). The 2011 Act founds the civil registry and its unique national number, and requires the registry to supply data to competent state organs ([Act](https://citizenshiprightsafrica.org/wp-content/uploads/2011/01/Sudan-Civil-Registration-Act-2011-EN.pdf)).<br><br>The national digital identity's terms, published 21 May 2026, cite both, plus a digital identity regulation of 2025 and a cybersecurity act of 2025 ([terms](https://sudapass.gov.sd/terms?lang=en)).<br><br>Neither 2025 instrument could be located as an independently accessible text, so the legal basis a reader can check stops at 2011.</details> | Advanced |
| Togo | A [review of the 2009 civil registration law](https://www.togofirst.com/en/justice/2408-19853-togo-reviews-civil-registration-law-to-support-digital-transformation) held its scoping meeting, with proposals due before the end of 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The scoping meeting was held in August 2026 ([review](https://www.togofirst.com/en/justice/2408-19853-togo-reviews-civil-registration-law-to-support-digital-transformation)).<br><br>It is the statute under which the birth-registration catch-up campaigns and the online certificate pilot operate, and the review is the first movement on it the base holds. No draft or proposal is on record.</details> | Advanced |
| Tunisia | [Organic laws on biometric identity](https://www.accessnow.org/press-release/tunisia-biometric-id-passport-bills-passed) were adopted in March 2024, and civil society records that committee deliberations lacked public consultation. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The laws were adopted by parliament on 6 March 2024, with use of the biometric card and passport targeted for the first half of 2025 ([laws](https://www.accessnow.org/press-release/tunisia-biometric-id-passport-bills-passed), [usage](https://www.zawya.com/en/economy/north-africa/tunisia-biometric-id-card-and-passport-usage-to-begin-in-h1-2025-bn9vuol5), [rollout](https://idtechwire.com/tunisia-to-get-biometric-ids-in-h1-of-2025)).<br><br>The consultation criticism is an attributed position rather than a finding of this report.<br><br>Two and a half years on, whether issuance has begun is recorded separately as not established — so the enabling statutes are in force and their effect is unmeasured.</details> | No change |
| Zambia | A [statutory instrument amended the citizenship regulations](https://zambialii.org/akn/zm/act/si/2025/98/eng@2025-12-31/source) while the registration department [began computerising document issuance](https://www.zambiamonitor.com/govt-embarks-on-digitalisation-of-identity-services/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2025-12-31 — [Statutory Instrument No. 98 of 2025 amended the citizenship regulations](https://zambialii.org/akn/zm/act/si/2025/98/eng@2025-12-31/source), the clearest in-window regulatory movement touching national identity documents.<br><br>2025-08 — [the home affairs permanent secretary announced that the registration department has begun computerising national registration card, passport and citizenship-document issuance with the state technology institute, targeting online applications including from the diaspora](https://www.zambiamonitor.com/govt-embarks-on-digitalisation-of-identity-services/).<br><br>The instruments governing identity are being amended at the edges while the system beneath them is rebuilt; no consolidated digital identity statute is held, and the new register's legal basis remains the National Registration Act.</details> | Advanced |
| Zimbabwe | No digital-identity Act is held. Identity rests on the [National Registration Act](https://zimlii.org/akn/zw/act/1976/36), and a 2026 constitutional amendment moved the voters' roll to the registrar-general. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The National Registration Act, commenced in 1976 and consolidated to 31 December 2016, is the statute under which the register of residents and the issue of identity documents sit ([Act](https://zimlii.org/akn/zw/act/1976/36)).<br><br>Constitution of Zimbabwe Amendment (No. 3) Act, Act No. 6 of 2026, amends the Constitution to assign the registrar-general responsibility for voter registration and custody of the voters' roll, consolidating electoral identity under the national identity authority ([amendment](https://t792ae.c2.acecdn.net/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/Act-No.-6-of-2026-Constitution-of-Zimbabwe-Amendment-Act-No.-6-of-2026.pdf)).<br><br>The commissioning of the Mutare provincial registry office for biometric passport and identity services was described by the registrar-general as foundational to a digital identity system ([registry office](https://www.heraldonline.co.zw/classy-e-passport-office-opens-in-mutare/)).<br><br>There is no electronic-identity or digital-identity Act, and no legal basis on file for the national digital identity that remains announced and unlaunched.</details> | Advanced, on a constitutional amendment rather than on any digital-identity statute |

## Digital payments legislation

| Country | Developments | Progress |
|---|---|---|
| Algeria | A [first regulation dedicated to payment service providers](https://www.joradp.dz/FTP/JO-FRANCAIS/2025/F2025028.pdf) was followed by one [bringing them inside the anti-money-laundering regime](https://www.joradp.dz/FTP/jo-francais/2025/F2025068.pdf). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The umbrella statute is [the monetary and banking law of June 2023, whose article 166 repealed the prior ordinance](https://www.joradp.dz/FTP/JO-FRANCAIS/2023/F2023043.pdf).<br><br>2025-04-14 - [the first Algerian regulatory instrument specifically dedicated to payment service providers was adopted by the central bank](https://www.joradp.dz/FTP/JO-FRANCAIS/2025/F2025028.pdf).<br><br>2025-09-24 - [a second regulation explicitly brings payment service providers inside the anti-money-laundering regime](https://www.joradp.dz/FTP/jo-francais/2025/F2025068.pdf), and in November the banking commission [issued guidelines on virtual assets](https://www.bank-of-algeria.dz/stoodroa/2025/11/lignes-directrices-06-2025-FR-1.pdf). No licence granted under the provider regime is recorded.</details> | Advanced |
| Botswana | Electronic payment regulations [were amended in April 2026, extending scope to value transfer services and reaching beneficial owners](https://www.bankofbotswana.bw/sites/default/files/Electronic%20Payment%20Services%20%28Amendment%29%20Regulations%2C%202026.pdf), with unlicensed operation punishable by P1,000,000 and five years. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-04-02 — [amended regulations came into effect extending scope to money or value transfer services, taking fit-and-proper tests down to beneficial owners and 10% shareholders, and making unlicensed operation punishable by P1,000,000 and five years](https://www.bankofbotswana.bw/sites/default/files/Electronic%20Payment%20Services%20%28Amendment%29%20Regulations%2C%202026.pdf), [with the central bank's implementation release in July](https://www.bankofbotswana.bw/sites/default/files/press-release-files/Implementation%20of%20the%20Electronic%20Payment%20Services%20%28Amendment%29%20Regulations%2C%202026.pdf). Float interest is ring-fenced and directed by the central bank, and financial-inclusion promotion was dropped from the permitted uses without being replaced by any customer entitlement — which is the change a wallet holder would notice if anyone told them.<br><br>The virtual-asset regime is unchanged and still criticised: [the Act is in force with crypto not legal tender](https://www.mmegi.bw/business/crypto-finds-footing-but-remains-far-from-legal-tender/news), and [a regional follow-up assessment in February 2026 still records provider-oversight shortcomings](https://www.mmegi.bw/business/crypto-oversight-gaps-persist-in-botswana/news).</details> | Advanced |
| Burundi | The 2018 payment-systems statute [is the framework law](https://www.brb.bi/sites/default/files/2024-09/LOI%20N%201-07%20DU%2011%20MAI%202018%20PORTANT%20SYSTEME%20NATIONAL%20DE%20PAIMENT.pdf); the central bank has since published [an oversight framework](https://www.brb.bi/sites/default/files/2025-11/CADRE%20DE%20SURVEILLANCE%20DE%20SYSTEME%20DE%20PAIEMENT%20%2020%20NOV%202025.pdf) and [launched a national instant payment system](https://www.brb.bi/node/3293). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[Loi n 1/07 du 11 mai 2018 portant systeme national de paiement is the framework statute, and the law the central bank cites for its power to regulate the issue and use of payment instruments; its articles 22 to 24 carry the move of payment instruments off paper and the admissibility of electronic and optical evidence](https://www.brb.bi/sites/default/files/2024-09/LOI%20N%201-07%20DU%2011%20MAI%202018%20PORTANT%20SYSTEME%20NATIONAL%20DE%20PAIMENT.pdf). Every official copy located is an image-only scan.<br><br>[The central bank has published an oversight framework covering the real-time gross settlement system, the clearing house, the central securities depository, the national switch, instant payment systems and electronic money platforms, with a licensing, monitoring, inspection and sanction regime for payment service providers](https://www.brb.bi/sites/default/files/2025-11/CADRE%20DE%20SURVEILLANCE%20DE%20SYSTEME%20DE%20PAIEMENT%20%2020%20NOV%202025.pdf), and in April 2026 [launched the national instant payment system under a regulation of its own](https://www.brb.bi/node/3293).</details> | Advanced |
| Chad | There is no national payments statute; the operative law is the [regional regulation](https://www.beac.int/systemes-paiement/instructions-circulaires-reglements/) of December 2016, with national practice set by convention. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The central bank's own register names Reglement n. 03/CEMAC/UMAC/CM of 21 December 2016 on payment systems among the constitutive instruments in force, directly applicable to Chad as a member state ([register](https://www.beac.int/systemes-paiement/instructions-circulaires-reglements/)).<br><br>The national layer is contractual. The finance minister chaired a meeting on 17 February 2026 with the four payment establishments operating in Chad to build an operational framework for moving state financial flows onto mobile money and pressed for a framework convention ([meeting](https://atpe.td/flux-financiers-de-letat-vers-lintegration-du-paiement-electronique/)), and on 18 February signed partnership conventions with all four allowing public revenue to be paid through their platforms ([conventions](https://tchadinfos.com/2026/02/18/digitalisation-des-paiements-publics-le-ministere-des-finances-signe-des-partenariats-avec-des-operateurs-de-mobile-money/)).<br><br>A statute a citizen could be pointed to does not exist.</details> | Advanced |
| Comoros | The [2026 finance law puts electronic filing and payment of taxes on a statutory footing](https://finances.gouv.km/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/Decret-portant-promulgation-du-loi-des-finances-2026.pdf), above a [2017 electronic-money regulation](https://banque-comores.km/uploads/Loi%20et%20textes%20reglementaire/REGLEMENTS/2017/R%C3%A8glement%20n%C2%B001_2017_BCC_DSBR%20sur%20la%20monnaie%20%C3%A9lectronique.pdf). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The standing instrument is the central bank's [regulation on electronic-money activity, setting the conditions and modalities for carrying it on, on the authority of the 2013 banking law, the 2012 anti-money-laundering law and the 2015 payment-systems decree](https://banque-comores.km/uploads/Loi%20et%20textes%20reglementaire/REGLEMENTS/2017/R%C3%A8glement%20n%C2%B001_2017_BCC_DSBR%20sur%20la%20monnaie%20%C3%A9lectronique.pdf).<br><br>2025-12 - the finance law [inserted a new section into the fiscal-procedures code establishing teledeclaration and telepayment, with a phased obligation on large and medium taxpayers, the legal value of an electronic filing, authentication, archiving and penalties for not using it](https://finances.gouv.km/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/Decret-portant-promulgation-du-loi-des-finances-2026.pdf), implementation dates left to regulation.<br><br>2026-04 - the tax directorate [opened a three-day workshop to validate that legal framework](https://finances.gouv.km/actualite_post/atelier-dgi-au-retaj-sur-la-validation-du-cadre-juridique-des-teleprocedures-fiscales-2026/), which places the implementing texts still in draft four months after the statute.</details> | Advanced |
| Congo | An [order of 22 May 2026 makes all public revenue payable exclusively through one national platform](https://sgg.cg/JO/2026/congo-jo-2026-24-2.pdf), with payment outside it void. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The order was [signed on 22 May 2026 and gazetted on 11 June with a 60-day conformity window: all public revenue is payable exclusively through one national platform, defined in the text as a national and sovereign payments switch, payment outside it is void and non-discharging, and administrations must suppress every parallel circuit](https://sgg.cg/JO/2026/congo-jo-2026-24-2.pdf).<br><br>Derogations are limited to technical unavailability, absence of access to electronic means, or duly established exceptional circumstances. It is the most categorical digital-payments instrument on this ledger, and it obliges the state rather than the market.</details> | Advanced |
| Cote d'Ivoire | A [banking regulation bill](https://web.sgg.gouv.ci/publications/conseils-des-ministres/details/3265) adopted in April 2026 would bring financial technology firms and electronic money into the national banking perimeter. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-04-29 - the Council of Ministers [adopted a bill aligning national law with the monetary union's uniform banking law, bringing financial technology firms and new operations such as electronic money - which the communique states are not covered by current legislation - into the national banking perimeter; a second bill reforming microfinance regulation was adopted at the same sitting](https://web.sgg.gouv.ci/publications/conseils-des-ministres/details/3265).<br><br>Both are government-adopted bills. No parliamentary passage, promulgation or entry into force is held for either, and the communique states no date by which either is expected. The regional instruments the bill aligns to are already directly applicable, so what the bill adds is national enforcement rather than the rule itself.</details> | Advanced, a bill adopted in council and not yet enacted |
| Djibouti | Payments are governed by a [2016 law placing every payment service under central-bank licensing and supervision](https://www.journalofficiel.dj/texte-juridique/loi-n118-an-15-7eme-l-portant-creation-dun-systeme-de-paiement-national-sa-reglementation-et-sa-surveillance/), unchanged in the period. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The law [applies to every payment service and system operated in the country, empowers the central bank to license, regulate and supervise payment-system providers, and defines electronic money and electronic funds transfer](https://www.journalofficiel.dj/texte-juridique/loi-n118-an-15-7eme-l-portant-creation-dun-systeme-de-paiement-national-sa-reglementation-et-sa-surveillance/).<br><br>Beneath it the central bank acts by instruction rather than by statute: from [31 August 2025 all cheques had to meet new format, security and encoding standards, with non-conforming cheques refused by the settlement platform and legacy stock withdrawn](https://banque-centrale.dj/wp-content/uploads/2025/09/BCD-Communique-SYRAD.pdf), a measure that took effect a day before this period opened.<br><br>No amendment, and no implementing text on electronic money issued in the period, is held.</details> | No change |
| Egypt | The central bank approved [regulations for a digital financial identity platform](https://www.cbe.org.eg/en/news-publications/news/2026/08/24/09/40/ekyc-2026) and the financial regulator [admitted two projects to its sandbox](https://techafricanews.com/2026/09/01/egypt-fra-approves-two-new-fintech-projects-regulatory-sandbox/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2025-09 - the central bank issued [regulations on governance, internal control and fit-and-proper criteria for key officials of payment system operators and payment service providers](https://www.cbe.org.eg/en/news-publications/news/2025/09/01/13/10/payment-system-operators-and-payment-service-providers) - the conduct rules beneath the payments market.<br><br>2026-08-23 - its board [approved the regulations governing the digital financial identity platform for identifying bank customers and verifying their identities electronically](https://www.cbe.org.eg/en/news-publications/news/2026/08/24/09/40/ekyc-2026), which is the rule that lets an account be opened without a counter.<br><br>2026-09-01 - the financial regulator [granted preliminary approval for two further technology projects to enter its regulatory sandbox](https://techafricanews.com/2026/09/01/egypt-fra-approves-two-new-fintech-projects-regulatory-sandbox/).<br><br>No licence issued under the payment-institution rules, and no count of customers identified through the platform, appears on this ledger.</details> | Advanced |
| Equatorial Guinea | A [presidential order banned cash collection of taxes and fees](https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/noticias/orden_de_la_presidencia_del_gobierno_relativa_a_la_recaudacion_o_cobro_en_efectivo_de_impuestos_tasas_y_demas_ingresos_de_la_administracion), routing public revenue to bank accounts and electronic platforms. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [order of September 2025](https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/noticias/orden_de_la_presidencia_del_gobierno_relativa_a_la_recaudacion_o_cobro_en_efectivo_de_impuestos_tasas_y_demas_ingresos_de_la_administracion) is the operative instrument: cash collection of taxes, fees and other administrative revenue is prohibited and payment must go to bank accounts or electronic platforms.<br><br>It bites on benefits too — [social benefits are now paid bank-only, and a 2025 census purged 2,282 untraceable pensioners](https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/noticias/el_delegado_nacional_del_inseso_comparece_ante_la_camara_de_los_diputados). The regional layer moved separately: the [deposit insurance ceiling rose to 8m CFA for individuals and 15m for firms from 10 December 2025](https://www.droitmediasfinance.com/index.php/actualites/droit-bancaire/1165-cemac-i-cobac-avec-la-recente-reforme-du-fogadac-le-plafond-dindemnisation-des-deposants-passe-de-5-a-8-millions-de-fcfa).</details> | Advanced |
| Eritrea | A regional peer assessment records a state-owned financial system with no cash machines, cards or internet banking ([evaluation](https://www.esaamlg.org/reports/MER%20of%20Eritrea-July%202025.pdf)). <details><summary>Full record</summary>A regional anti-money-laundering peer assessment, adopted in July 2025 after an on-site visit in 2024, states that Eritrea's financial system is state-owned and offers simple and less sophisticated services, with no automatic teller machines, debit or credit cards, or internet banking ([evaluation](https://www.esaamlg.org/reports/MER%20of%20Eritrea-July%202025.pdf)).<br><br>There is therefore nothing for payments legislation to regulate, and the base holds no payment systems act, no e-money regulation and no licensing regime. The only draft text addressing digital finance is an unofficial consultation draft ([drafts](https://www.undp.org/eritrea/news/eritrea-launches-landmark-digital-customs-system-modernizing-trade)).<br><br>The one instrument on the ledger is a central bank legal notice ordering cash deposits, which is currency management rather than payments law.</details> | No change |
| Eswatini | The [National Payments System Act 2023](https://www.centralbank.org.sz/wp-content/uploads/2021/04/NATIONAL-PAYMENT-SYSTEM-ACT-2023.pdf) is in force, regulations to operationalise it were approved by Cabinet in July 2026, and the fast-payments platform completed onboarding in November 2025. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Act of 2023 provides for the regulation and supervision of payment systems, the safety and efficiency of the national payments system and the central bank's functions in relation to them ([Act](https://www.centralbank.org.sz/wp-content/uploads/2021/04/NATIONAL-PAYMENT-SYSTEM-ACT-2023.pdf)).<br><br>Cabinet approved National Payment Systems Regulations of 2026 in the first quarter of 2026/27 to operationalise it; no gazette text, publication or commencement date is held ([Cabinet](https://eswatinipositivenews.online/cabinet-approves-major-reforms-to-strengthen-eswatinis-development/)).<br><br>The central bank governor told a regional report launch on 13 November 2025 that the eighth and final participant would go live on the fast-payments platform two days later ([remarks](https://www.centralbank.org.sz/wp-content/uploads/2021/04/Governors-Welcoming-Remarks-SIIPS-in-Africa-2025-Report-Launch-November-2025.pdf)).<br><br>The statute is the enablement and the switch is the delivery; the regulations between them are approved and unpublished.</details> | Advanced |
| Ethiopia | The central bank widened its [virtual-asset prohibition](https://www.thereporterethiopia.com/51969/) to the whole category, and a court [struck down a decree](https://www.thereporterethiopia.com/52044/) revoking licences without a court order. <details><summary>Full record</summary>A central-bank public notice of 23 July 2026 widened the prohibition by clarification rather than by new rule, covering use, purchase, sale, exchange, transfer, trading, settlement and facilitation absent express authorisation, with no new regulation and no enforcement mechanism announced ([public notice](https://www.thereporterethiopia.com/51969/)).<br><br>The Federal High Court of Lideta struck down a December 2025 lottery-administration decree that had cut banking services to betting platforms and gateways, holding revocation of a licence without a court order unconstitutional ([the ruling](https://www.thereporterethiopia.com/52044/)).<br><br>No payments proclamation or electronic transactions statute is held, so the operative law reaches the base as directives, notices and one judgment.</details> | Mixed, the central bank widened a prohibition by notice while a court struck down a decree |
| Gambia | The [2011 mobile money regulation](https://www.cbg.gm/downloads-file/b2064e61-f37b-11e9-876c-02e599c15748) remains the rulebook, carrying the interoperability duty the 2025 switch delivers on. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [regulation](https://www.cbg.gm/downloads-file/b2064e61-f37b-11e9-876c-02e599c15748) made in October 2011 sets a GMD10m capital floor, a cash-merchant regime and a duty to interoperate. A separate [pricing policy](https://www.cbg.gm/downloads-file/448bc45a-06e7-11ea-876c-02e599c15748) effective December 2013 sets cost-recovery tariffs for the settlement system, clearing house, securities settlement and the national switch.<br><br>The interoperability duty took fourteen years to deliver: the [instant payment switch launched in December 2025](https://www.gamswitch.com/press-release/the-gambia-launches-bantaba-2-0-its-first-real-time-interoperable-payment-system) links banks, mobile money and fintechs on a phased rollout.</details> | No change |
| Ghana | The [Virtual Asset Service Providers Act 2025](https://www.myjoyonline.com/upsa-law-school-roundtable-charts-path-for-regulating-digital-assets-in-ghana/) passed in December 2025, and a digital banking framework is complete and awaiting consultation. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Before it there was no statute, against more than 3 million virtual-asset users and over 100 service providers recorded in a July 2025 registration exercise, with regulated institutions directed not to facilitate virtual-asset activity at all ([policy position](https://www.bog.gov.gh/wp-content/uploads/2025/11/Ghanas-Policy-Position-on-Virtual-Assets-and-Service-Providers.pdf)).<br><br>On the regulator's account the Act is risk-based and technology-neutral, splits supervision between the central bank and the securities regulator by activity, attaches licensing to the service rather than to holding, excludes virtual assets from deposit protection and from invoicing, pricing and wages, and provides transition arrangements; the licensing requirements are still being built ([the Act](https://www.myjoyonline.com/upsa-law-school-roundtable-charts-path-for-regulating-digital-assets-in-ghana/), [questions answered](https://www.bog.gov.gh/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/VASP-Act-FAQs.pdf)).<br><br>The gazetted text is not held, so every characterisation is the central bank's own. A digital banking framework with comprehensive draft guidelines is complete and awaiting stakeholder consultation, with no date ([framework](https://www.myjoyonline.com/bog-to-roll-out-new-digital-banking-framework-as-financial-sector-goes-more-digital/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Guinea | There is [no payments statute](https://www.bcrg.gn/projets-de-reformes-en-cours-a-travers-la-dsmp/), and the national payment council that would sit under one is at terms-of-reference stage. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Both the payments law and the council are at terms-of-reference stage under the donor programme, the law awaiting the lender's no-objection; the position comes from the central bank's own project register, which is undated and whose latest dated entry is January 2025 ([project register](https://www.bcrg.gn/projets-de-reformes-en-cours-a-travers-la-dsmp/)).<br><br>On virtual assets the central bank issued a warning rather than a regime: it states it has granted no licence or authorisation to any entity for crypto-asset trading or online financial placement, sets out seven warning signs of fraudulent offers, notes that registration with a foreign authority is not a domestic licence, and reserves the right to refer illegal fund collection, money laundering and fraud to the courts ([communique](https://www.africaguinee.com/crypto-actifs-et-placements-financiers-en-ligne-la-banque-centrale-alerte-sur-les-plateformes-non-autorisees-de-trading/)).</details> | No change |
| Guinea-Bissau | Payment law is regional: the [central bank's register](https://www.bceao.int/sites/default/files/inline-files/4%20-%20Liste%20Guin%C3%A9e-Bissau_2026.pdf) licenses six banks, one payment institution and one electronic money issuer. <details><summary>Full record</summary>No domestic payments statute appears in the base. Authorisation runs through the monetary union: the [first licensed payment institution in the country appeared on the register in September 2025](https://www.bceao.int/sites/default/files/2025-09/LISTE_DES_ETABLISSEMENTS_DE_PAIEMENT_AGREES_15_septembre_2025.pdf), and the [June 2026 register carries six banks, one payment institution and one electronic money issuer](https://www.bceao.int/sites/default/files/inline-files/4%20-%20Liste%20Guin%C3%A9e-Bissau_2026.pdf).<br><br>Domestic instruments are administrative rather than legislative: the revenue directorate's [order making the payment platform the sole channel for the tax identification number](https://kontaktu.mef.gw/instructions).</details> | No change |
| Kenya | [Virtual asset regulations were gazetted](https://new.kenyalaw.org/akn/ke/act/ln/2026/134/eng@2026-07-22/source) in July 2026 with joint licensing across ten categories, and existing operators have until November to transition. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The 2025 Act commenced on 4 November 2025, giving pre-existing operators a year to comply ([the Act](https://new.kenyalaw.org/akn/ke/act/2025/20)).<br><br>The regulations set capital by licence class from KES 300m for stablecoin issuance to nil for advisers, with a seizure and freezing framework; the draft ownership cap was dropped between draft and gazette, and the Finance Bill replaces a proposed 3 per cent transfer tax with a 10 per cent excise duty on provider fees ([regulations](https://new.kenyalaw.org/akn/ke/act/ln/2026/134/eng@2026-07-22/source), [compliance cost](https://techcabal.com/2026/08/24/kenya-virtual-asset-marke/)).<br><br>Six named exchanges have said they intend to apply, and the regulators had not announced the application process as at 7 August 2026 ([applicants](https://techcabal.com/2026/08/07/firms-lining-up-for-crypto-licences-in-kenya/)).<br><br>On mobile money the position is different in kind: the central bank's pricing principles operate through voluntary operator alignment, with no gazetted tariff rule or determination to cite, appeal or extend to a competitor, reversible at the operator's discretion ([pricing principles](https://www.standardmedia.co.ke/business/article/2001554316/safaricom-halves-m-pesa-merchant-fees-in-cbk-led-move)).</details> | Advanced |
| Lesotho | The [Payment System Bill reached second reading in May 2026](https://www.lena.gov.ls/finance-minister-presents-payment-system-bill/), to replace the 2014 Act and license fintech providers. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2014-09-12 — [the Payment Systems Act governs payment systems and instruments, electronic money and cards among them](https://centralbank.org.ls/wp-content/uploads/Payment-Systems-Act-2014-1.pdf).<br><br>2025-11-17 — [the financial sector strategy sets strengthening the payments system and access to digital financial services as cross-cutting priorities](https://centralbank.org.ls/wp-content/uploads/2025-2030-Financial-Sector-Development-Strategic-Plan-II-LR-Singles-17-November-2025.pdf).<br><br>2026-05-05 — [the finance minister moved the Payment System Bill to second reading, to repeal and replace the 2014 Act and license fintech payment providers](https://www.lena.gov.ls/finance-minister-presents-payment-system-bill/).<br><br>No enactment date is on the record.</details> | Advanced |
| Liberia | The [mobile money regulations](https://www.cbl.org.lr/sites/default/files/documents/MobileMoneyRegulations.pdf) are in force and unamended, and the interoperable fee schedule of 2026 was approved under them. <details><summary>Full record</summary>They are the central bank's standing mobile-money framework ([regulations](https://www.cbl.org.lr/sites/default/files/documents/MobileMoneyRegulations.pdf)).<br><br>No payments statute, electronic transactions act or switch instrument sits above them, so a fee schedule for a national instant payment system rests on a regulation written before that system existed.</details> | No change |
| Libya | A prime-ministerial decree of [6 March 2026](https://libyaherald.com/2026/03/aldabaiba-government-and-cbl-take-further-actions-to-encourage-increased-e-payments-use-cbl-reduces-new-fx-bureaux-commission) obliges treasuries and collecting agencies to accept electronic payment, the first such instrument on file. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The decree was issued on 6 March 2026 and is reported alongside a cut in the foreign-exchange levy, making electronic acceptance a duty of state collecting bodies rather than an option ([decree](https://libyaherald.com/2026/03/aldabaiba-government-and-cbl-take-further-actions-to-encourage-increased-e-payments-use-cbl-reduces-new-fx-bureaux-commission)).<br><br>The base holds no implementing regulation, no compliance deadline and no penalty for a body that does not comply.</details> | Advanced |
| Madagascar | The [2016 electronic money law](https://textes.lexxika.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/05/Loi-2016-056-02-Fevrier-2016-Sur-la-Monnaie-Electronique-et-les-Etablissements-de-Monnaie-Electronique.pdf) remains the governing instrument; nothing amended it in the window. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The statute runs to 120 articles and licenses electronic money institutions under the banking supervisor ([law](https://textes.lexxika.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/05/Loi-2016-056-02-Fevrier-2016-Sur-la-Monnaie-Electronique-et-les-Etablissements-de-Monnaie-Electronique.pdf)); its constitutionality was confirmed in January 2017 ([decision](https://www.hcc.gov.mg/?p=2730)).<br><br>The 2026 finance law, itself cleared by the Constitutional Court in December 2025, carries mobile transaction tax provisions ([decision](https://www.hcc.gov.mg/?p=9749)).<br><br>The central bank's digital currency project has a published distribution model and a pilot framed for August 2026, with no legislative instrument behind it in the base ([project](https://www.banky-foibe.mg/eariary)).</details> | No change |
| Malawi | A [financial crimes amendment](https://malawi24.com/2026/08/07/mps-hailed-for-fast-tracking-digital-finance-reforms/) removing all references to cheques passed Parliament in August 2026, one of four bills effecting the cheque phase-out. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The bill was passed alongside amendments to the payment systems and deposit insurance statutes, following a bills of exchange amendment that effects the phase-out ([bills](https://malawi24.com/2026/08/07/mps-hailed-for-fast-tracking-digital-finance-reforms/)).<br><br>Assent, gazette date and commencement are not published, and the safeguarding regulations the finance minister promised are not drafted. A member warned that rural users risk being left behind by the switch away from cheques ([warning](https://www.nyasatimes.com/mcp-mp-warns-rural-malawi-risks-being-left-behind-in-switch-away-from-cheques-unless-govt-fixes-patchy-internet/)), and the base holds no primary for the discontinuation itself and no date for it.</details> | Advanced |
| Mauritania | [Law 2021-014 on electronic payment services and means](https://mtnima.gov.mr/sites/default/files/2024-02/%D9%82%D8%A7%D9%86%D9%88%D9%86%20%D8%B1%D9%82%D9%85%202021-014%20%D9%8A%D8%AA%D8%B9%D9%84%D9%82%20%D8%A8%D8%AE%D8%AF%D9%85%D8%A7%D8%AA%20%D9%88%20%D9%88%D8%B3%D8%A7%D8%A1%D9%84%20%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AF%D9%81%D8%B9%20%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A5%D9%84%D9%83%D8%AA%D8%B1%D9%88%D9%86%D9%8A%20%28%D8%AC.%D8%B1%201490%29.pdf) has been in force since July 2021 and is the statute the central bank's payment instructions are issued under. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Official Gazette text of 30 July 2021 covers definitions, electronic payment services and means, payment cards and the contestation of a card payment ([law](https://mtnima.gov.mr/sites/default/files/2024-02/%D9%82%D8%A7%D9%86%D9%88%D9%86%20%D8%B1%D9%82%D9%85%202021-014%20%D9%8A%D8%AA%D8%B9%D9%84%D9%82%20%D8%A8%D8%AE%D8%AF%D9%85%D8%A7%D8%AA%20%D9%88%20%D9%88%D8%B3%D8%A7%D8%A1%D9%84%20%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AF%D9%81%D8%B9%20%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A5%D9%84%D9%83%D8%AA%D8%B1%D9%88%D9%86%D9%8A%20%28%D8%AC.%D8%B1%201490%29.pdf), [text](https://www.droit-afrique.com/uploads/Mauritanie-Loi-2021-14-services-moyens-paiement-electronique.pdf)).<br><br>The held capture is an excerpt cut off partway through Titre III, so the later articles the central bank's own instruction cites are not on file, and no implementing decree, licensing decision or amendment to the statute is held.</details> | No change, a statute in force since 2021 that the base has only now acquired |
| Mauritius | The [2018 payment systems Act](https://mauritiusassembly.govmu.org/mauritiusassembly/wp-content/uploads/2023/03/act1718.pdf) is the governing statute; a payment aggregator guideline was issued under it in July 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Act was assented in November 2018 and is now at its seventh consolidated version ([Act](https://mauritiusassembly.govmu.org/mauritiusassembly/wp-content/uploads/2023/03/act1718.pdf), [consolidation](https://lawsofmauritius.govmu.org/portal/viewlegislationdocument/web/?docnumber=&doctitle=TmF0aW9uYWwgUGF5bWVudCBTeXN0ZW1zIEFjdCAyMDE4&doctype=act)).<br><br>The central bank issued a guideline for payment aggregators in July 2026, bringing them under a stated supervisory expectation for the first time ([guideline](https://www.bom.mu/sites/default/files/guideline_for_payment_aggregators_0.pdf)).<br><br>The financial-crime statute book was amended in April 2026 ([Act](https://mauritiusassembly.govmu.org/mauritiusassembly/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/Act0326.pdf)). No enforcement action, licence refusal or sanction under the payments Act is held.</details> | Advanced |
| Morocco | [Law 103-12](https://www.bkam.ma/content/download/757666/8545663/loi%20bancaire%20fr%2022.pdf) defines electronic money, payment accounts and non-bank payment institutions under central bank approval. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [banking law of 2014](https://www.bkam.ma/content/download/757666/8545663/loi%20bancaire%20fr%2022.pdf) is the statutory basis for Moroccan payment institutions, and no amending statute appears in the base.<br><br>What governs conduct is made under it rather than beside it: the [interchange cap cut to 0.50 per cent from 1 October 2026](https://conseil-concurrence.ma/communique-conjoint-du-conseil-de-la-concurrence-et-de-bank-al-maghrib/) by the central bank and the competition authority, and the [consolidated forex rulebook in force from 1 January 2026](https://www.oc.gov.ma/fr/actualites/publication-de-l-instruction-generale-des-operations-de-change-2026).</details> | No change |
| Mozambique | The central bank [made connection to the single national payments network mandatory with 60-minute incident reporting](https://www.oeconomico.com/banco-de-mocambique-da-60-minutos-as-instituicoes-financeiras-para-reportarem-falhas-nos-pagamentos-electronicos/) and [set an annual ceiling on card payments abroad](https://bancomoc.mz/media/5xqnu1b4/aviso-4-gbm-2026-de-28-de-julho.pdf). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-06-02 — a central-bank notice [made connection to the single national network mandatory for electronic payment services and imposed 60-minute incident-reporting, continuity and disaster-recovery duties on financial institutions](https://www.oeconomico.com/banco-de-mocambique-da-60-minutos-as-instituicoes-financeiras-para-reportarem-falhas-nos-pagamentos-electronicos/). The gazette text was not locatable, and the base holds the fullest available account instead.<br><br>2026-07-28 — a further notice [set an annual limit of 3,000,000 meticais on external payments made with bank cards, revoking the December 2025 notice it replaces](https://bancomoc.mz/media/5xqnu1b4/aviso-4-gbm-2026-de-28-de-julho.pdf), this one the primary gazette text.<br><br>Two instruments moved in two months, in opposite directions: one compels participation in a domestic rail, the other caps what can leave through a foreign one.</details> | Advanced |
| Namibia | The [Payment System Management Act](https://www.lac.org.na/laws/2023/8171.pdf) commenced in August 2023, its licensing determination took effect in January 2026, and government-to-person payments went live in June. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Act commenced on 8 August 2023, and the central bank's PSD-1 determination brought licensing and authorisation of payment service providers into operation on 6 January 2026 ([commencement](https://www.lac.org.na/laws/2023/8171.pdf), [determination](https://www.bon.com.na/CMSTemplates/Bon/Files/bon.com.na/31/313b4ab5-b138-45d3-b526-28959686db96.pdf)).<br><br>Under the Act the payments association moved from a management council to a management board during 2025, and a fees and charges determination came into force in December 2025.<br><br>The first live government-to-person grant payments ran over the national instant payment network on 17 June 2026 through one certified bank ([disbursements](https://economist.com.na/107579/retail/government-to-person-disbursements-go-live-under-the-instant-payment-platform/)).<br><br>An operational and cybersecurity standard for the payment system followed in July 2026. The statute is doing what statutes are for: each determination under it lands as a dated rule.</details> | Advanced |
| Niger | The regional instant-payment platform [set a June 2026 connection deadline for all supervised institutions](https://ns2.bceao.int/sites/default/files/2026-04/Communiqu%C3%A9_Connexion_%C3%A0_PI-SPI_2_avril_2026.pdf) after its [September 2025 launch](https://www.bceao.int/fr/evenement/lancement-officiel-de-la-plateforme-interoperable-du-systeme-de-paiement-instantane-pi). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [platform launched on 30 September 2025](https://www.bceao.int/fr/evenement/lancement-officiel-de-la-plateforme-interoperable-du-systeme-de-paiement-instantane-pi) as the union-wide instant payment infrastructure that Nigerien institutions connect to under the central bank's 2024 instruction.<br><br>By [2 April 2026](https://ns2.bceao.int/sites/default/files/2026-04/Communiqu%C3%A9_Connexion_%C3%A0_PI-SPI_2_avril_2026.pdf) 80 participants were live across the union — 59 banks, nine electronic-money institutions, 11 microfinance institutions and one payment institution — with 42 more in production testing and a deadline of 30 June 2026 for all supervised banks, electronic-money, payment and microfinance institutions to complete connection.<br><br>The regime is regional. No Nigerien domestic statute or regulation on digital payments is on file, and no national implementing text transposing the instruction has been located.</details> | Advanced |
| Nigeria | Crypto sits [inside the securities perimeter](https://techcabal.com/2025/05/09/investments-and-securities-act-nigeria-2025/), an [executive order](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/20/nigeria-launches-coordinated-regulatory-framework-for-virtual-assets/) created a coordinating council, [draft virtual-asset rules](https://www.itweb.africa/article/nigeria-sec-toughens-crypto-rules/dgp45vaBlmw7X9l8) set capital thresholds and a [stablecoin licensing regime](https://www.cbn.gov.ng/Out/2026/CCD/Nigeria%20Payments%20System%20Vision%202028.pdf) is drafted. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Investments and Securities Act 2025 put crypto inside the securities perimeter ([Act](https://techcabal.com/2025/05/09/investments-and-securities-act-nigeria-2025/)), and an executive order of July 2026 created a Virtual Asset Council and Office with its secretariat at the central bank, creating no new regulator and transferring no powers ([order](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/20/nigeria-launches-coordinated-regulatory-framework-for-virtual-assets/)).<br><br>Draft rules out for comment set N500m minimum capital for digital-asset platforms, N200m for service providers, a N30m registration fee, local incorporation and a resident chief executive ([rules](https://www.itweb.africa/article/nigeria-sec-toughens-crypto-rules/dgp45vaBlmw7X9l8), [data](https://techcabal.com/2026/08/20/nigerias-sec-wants-crypto-firms-to-share-transaction-data/)).<br><br>A drafted issuer-licensing regime for fiat-collateralised stablecoins requires full reserves with licensed Nigerian banks, daily reconciliation and monthly audited publication; no instrument is issued ([regime](https://www.cbn.gov.ng/Out/2026/CCD/Nigeria%20Payments%20System%20Vision%202028.pdf)). The securities regulator has admitted further firms to an incubation programme with no graduation route published ([incubation](http://www.wearetech.africa/fr/fils/breves/breves-simple/le-nigeria-integre-trois-nouvelles-entreprises-crypto-dans-son-incubateur-virtuel)), and the central bank intends to run observer nodes on approved stablecoin blockchains ([nodes](https://techcabal.com/2026/08/21/nigerias-central-bank-wants-to-monitor-stablecoins/)).<br><br>2026-08-31 — the same draft [also carries custody and incident duties: customer assets segregated from the firm's own with 80% held offline, N2bn minimum capital for exchanges and custodians, a fidelity bond of at least 25% of that minimum, incidents notified within 24 hours and reported within 48, and SEC approval before a foreign-issued stablecoin is listed](https://techcabal.com/2026/08/31/nigeria-sec-wants-digital-asset-firms-to-store-80-of-customer-funds-offline/). No commencement date or transition period is stated.</details> | Advanced |
| Rwanda | The lower house approved the rationale for a [draft law on virtual assets](https://techafricanews.com/2026/04/01/parliament-approves-draft-law-on-virtual-assets-as-rwanda-strengthens-digital-finance-rules/) in April 2026 with nothing reported since, above a [central bank regulatory sandbox](https://anjolaadebowale.substack.com/p/strategy-ahead-of-infrastructure) established in 2022. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The chamber approved the rationale in April 2026 and nothing has been reported on the draft since ([draft](https://techafricanews.com/2026/04/01/parliament-approves-draft-law-on-virtual-assets-as-rwanda-strengthens-digital-finance-rules/)).<br><br>The sandbox has run since 2022 on an analyst's account; its own regulation is not held, so its admission criteria, cohort and exit route are unstated ([sandbox](https://anjolaadebowale.substack.com/p/strategy-ahead-of-infrastructure)).</details> | Advanced |
| Sao Tome and Principe | The [2018 payment system law](https://www.africa-laws.org/Saotome/banking%20and%20finance%20law/Law%20no%2017-2018%20%20Legal%20System%20of%20the%20National%20Payment%20System%20%28in%20Portuguese%29.pdf) is in force, a [new central bank organic law](https://www.bcstp.st/Upload/Documentos/Gabinete_Governador/Lei_organica.pdf) replaced the 1992 statute in 2025, and the [2026 budget law caps cash payments](https://cipstp.st/wp-content/uploads/2026/05/Lei-OGE-2026_23-marco-2026.pdf) at 10,000 dobras. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The payment system law applies to payment systems and providers operating in the country, makes the central bank regulator and overseer, and carries a chapter on electronic funds transfers between individuals, commercial entities and financial institutions ([law](https://www.africa-laws.org/Saotome/banking%20and%20finance%20law/Law%20no%2017-2018%20%20Legal%20System%20of%20the%20National%20Payment%20System%20%28in%20Portuguese%29.pdf)).<br><br>The 2025 organic law sets the bank's attributions to regulate and oversee payment systems and to license and supervise financial institutions ([organic law](https://www.bcstp.st/Upload/Documentos/Gabinete_Governador/Lei_organica.pdf)).<br><br>Article 9 of the 2026 budget law prohibits taxpayers with organised accounting from making or receiving cash payments of 10,000 dobras or more and requires a means identifying the payee, with cash spending above the limit not tax-deductible ([budget law](https://cipstp.st/wp-content/uploads/2026/05/Lei-OGE-2026_23-marco-2026.pdf)). That is the first instrument in this base compelling electronic settlement rather than enabling it.</details> | Advanced |
| Seychelles | The [National Payment System Act 2014](https://seylii.org/akn/sc/act/2014/14/eng@2014-08-18) is the statute, and [December 2025 regulations](https://www.gazette.sc/sites/default/files/2025-12/SI%2099%202025%20No%202%20-%20National%20Payment%20System%20%28Licensing%20and%20Authorisation%29%20%28Amendment%29%20Regulations%202025.pdf) made interoperable IT systems a licence condition. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Act 14 of 2014 commenced on 18 August 2014 and has been amended by the 2022 electronic money regulations and a 2023 amendment Act ([Act](https://seylii.org/akn/sc/act/2014/14/eng@2014-08-18)).<br><br>The amendment to the licensing and authorisation regulations, made on 15 December 2025, requires safe and reliable IT systems and adequate interfaces to ensure straight-through processing as a condition of licence for payment, clearing and settlement operators ([regulations](https://www.gazette.sc/sites/default/files/2025-12/SI%2099%202025%20No%202%20-%20National%20Payment%20System%20%28Licensing%20and%20Authorisation%29%20%28Amendment%29%20Regulations%202025.pdf)).<br><br>Cabinet approved amendments to the Foreign Exchange Act in July 2026 adding credit unions and payment service providers to the class of authorised dealers ([Cabinet](https://www.nation.sc/articles/31629/cabinet-clears-major-reforms-across-economy-education-and-environment)); that approval is not yet an enacted instrument.</details> | Advanced |
| Sierra Leone | The [National Payment Systems Act 2022](https://sierralii.gov.sl/akn/sl/act/2022/8/eng@2022-06-23) is the governing statute, with a [Public Financial Management Act 2026](https://sierraloaded.sl/news/digital-reforms-proposed-new-finance-law/) in consultation above it. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The payment systems Act is Act 8 of 2022, gazetted on 23 June 2022, with subsidiary legislation made under it ([Act](https://sierralii.gov.sl/akn/sl/act/2022/8/eng@2022-06-23)).<br><br>A draft Public Financial Management Act was consulted on in Freetown in August 2026 covering digital payments; no text, parliamentary stage or commencement date is published ([draft](https://sierraloaded.sl/news/digital-reforms-proposed-new-finance-law/)). The communications Act was tested against the mobile-money fraud wave in a published legal analysis in July 2026 ([Act](https://owlpress-sl.com/dont-call-meeting-inside-whatsapp-scam-emptying-orange-money/)).<br><br>Between them and the cybercrime Act the country has statutes that reach the conduct. What it does not have, recorded separately, is a rule allocating the loss.</details> | Advanced |
| Somalia | The [National Payment System Bill](https://sonna.so/en/somalias-upper-house-passes-national-payment-system-bill-receives-briefing-on-worsening-drought/) cleared both chambers by January 2026, replacing a regime that rested on [2020 mobile money regulations](https://centralbank.gov.so/wp-content/uploads/2025/06/Mobile-Money-Regulation-2020_amended_2021.pdf). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The lower house passed the bill 154 to 2 on 8 November 2025 ([vote](https://golekaab.so/en/bill/15)) and the upper house carried its third reading 28 to nil in January 2026 ([vote](https://sonna.so/en/somalias-upper-house-passes-national-payment-system-bill-receives-briefing-on-worsening-drought/)). It empowers the central bank to license and supervise payment systems and digital-money platforms.<br><br>Until it is signed, the operative instrument remains the Mobile Money Regulations amended in 2020, covering licensing, conduct of business, safeguarding of consumer funds, supervision and winding-up ([regulations](https://centralbank.gov.so/wp-content/uploads/2025/06/Mobile-Money-Regulation-2020_amended_2021.pdf)).<br><br>No record of presidential signature was found.</details> | Advanced |
| South Africa | A National Payment System Bill will go out for public comment, moving payment regulation from entity-based to activity-based ([announcement](https://techcentral.co.za/national-payment-system-bill-sarb-fintech/285597/)). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Reserve Bank governor said on 1 September 2026 that the National Payment System Bill will soon go out for public comment, moving payment regulation from an entity-based model to an activity-based one ([announcement](https://techcentral.co.za/national-payment-system-bill-sarb-fintech/285597/)).<br><br>An activity-based model is what would open payment licensing to non-banks, which the base currently carries as a not-held position.<br><br>Nothing is enacted: the base holds a governor's statement of intent, no published bill text, no comment period dates and no commencement provision.</details> | Advanced |
| South Sudan | A [National Payment System Bill](https://www.eyeradio.org/boss-parliament-back-stronger-legal-framework-for-digital-payments/) went to a five-day validation workshop in August 2026, with no text, introduction date or parliamentary timetable held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The workshop opened on 10 August 2026 ([workshop](https://www.eyeradio.org/boss-parliament-back-stronger-legal-framework-for-digital-payments/), [transition](https://itweb.africa/article/south-sudan-pushes-cashless-transition/nWJad7bNDyL7bjO1), [bill](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/17/south-sudan-pushes-digital-payments-as-central-bank-calls-for-faster-payment-bill/)).<br><br>The separately recorded national instant payment system has been planned since early 2025 and has not moved, so the statute and the rail are both at the stage of intention.</details> | Advanced |
