| Sudan | Four central bank instruments cover digital payments: [mobile payment licensing](https://cbos.gov.sd/ar/content/%D9%84%D8%A7%D8%A6%D8%AD%D8%A9-%D8%AA%D8%B1%D8%AE%D9%8A%D8%B5-%D9%88%D8%AA%D9%86%D8%B8%D9%8A%D9%85-%D8%A3%D8%B9%D9%85%D8%A7%D9%84-%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%85%D8%A4%D8%B3%D8%B3%D8%A7%D8%AA-%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%85%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%8A%D8%A9-%D9%84%D9%84%D8%AF%D9%81%D8%B9-%D8%B9%D8%A8%D8%B1-%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%85%D9%88%D8%A8%D8%A7%D9%8A%D9%84-%D9%84%D8%B3%D9%86%D8%A9-2020), [banking agency](https://cbos.gov.sd/ar/node/24549/), [USSD controls](https://cbos.gov.sd/ar/node/24567/) and [anti-money-laundering controls](https://cbos.gov.sd/sites/default/files/%D9%85%D9%86%D8%B4%D9%88%D8%B1%20%D8%B1%D9%82%D9%85%288-2026%29-%20%D8%B6%D9%88%D8%A7%D8%A8%D8%B7%20%D9%85%D9%83%D8%A7%D9%81%D8%AD%D8%A9%20%D8%BA%D8%B3%D9%84%20%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A3%D9%85%D9%88%D8%A7%D9%84.pdf). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The 2020 regulation sets licensing conditions, capital and insurance requirements, e-money and trust-account rules and agent rules for mobile payment institutions ([regulation](https://cbos.gov.sd/ar/content/%D9%84%D8%A7%D8%A6%D8%AD%D8%A9-%D8%AA%D8%B1%D8%AE%D9%8A%D8%B5-%D9%88%D8%AA%D9%86%D8%B8%D9%8A%D9%85-%D8%A3%D8%B9%D9%85%D8%A7%D9%84-%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%85%D8%A4%D8%B3%D8%B3%D8%A7%D8%AA-%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%85%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%8A%D8%A9-%D9%84%D9%84%D8%AF%D9%81%D8%B9-%D8%B9%D8%A8%D8%B1-%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%85%D9%88%D8%A8%D8%A7%D9%8A%D9%84-%D9%84%D8%B3%D9%86%D8%A9-2020)).<br><br>Three instruments were made inside the window. The banking agency regulation of 1 March 2026 lets banks deliver services through licensed agents outside branches ([regulation](https://cbos.gov.sd/ar/node/24549/)). Circular 7/2026 sets binding consumer-protection requirements for USSD services ([circular](https://cbos.gov.sd/ar/node/24567/)). Circular 8/2026 extends anti-money-laundering supervision to mobile and phone payment channels ([circular](https://cbos.gov.sd/sites/default/files/%D9%85%D9%86%D8%B4%D9%88%D8%B1%20%D8%B1%D9%82%D9%85%288-2026%29-%20%D8%B6%D9%88%D8%A7%D8%A8%D8%B7%20%D9%85%D9%83%D8%A7%D9%81%D8%AD%D8%A9%20%D8%BA%D8%B3%D9%84%20%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A3%D9%85%D9%88%D8%A7%D9%84.pdf)).<br><br>All four are central bank instruments made under the Banking Business Organisation Act 2004. No primary payments statute is held.</details> | Advanced |
| Togo | An [anti-money-laundering law](https://www.togofirst.com/en/economic-governance/3012-17892-togo-adopts-new-law-to-strengthen-anti-money-laundering-rules) was adopted in the December 2025 budget session, with no promulgation date, number or gazette reference held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The source gives only the budget session, so the vote date is not established ([law](https://www.togofirst.com/en/economic-governance/3012-17892-togo-adopts-new-law-to-strengthen-anti-money-laundering-rules)).<br><br>It is the only payments-adjacent statute in this ledger, and its text cannot be read. Against six institutions live on the regional instant payment platform and a treasury prepaid card announced, the governing law is present as a headline.</details> | Advanced |
| Uganda | [Digital lending platform regulation](https://www.monitor.co.ug/uganda/news/national/govt-moves-to-tighten-regulation-of-digital-lenders-as-financial-inclusion-rises-5561784) was stated as intended at a finance ministry and parliamentary retreat, with no bill, instrument or timetable held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The intention was stated in August 2026 ([intention](https://www.monitor.co.ug/uganda/news/national/govt-moves-to-tighten-regulation-of-digital-lenders-as-financial-inclusion-rises-5561784)).<br><br>It is the only payments-specific legislative movement in this ledger, against a payments record that includes a stablecoin corridor, a tokenised-asset exchange, a currency-settlement service and a credit-scoring memorandum with nothing published about its consent or legal basis.</details> | Advanced |
| Zambia | The [mobile money levy](https://www.gsma.com/mobilefordevelopment/) rate doubled during 2025, dated only to the year, and the source is an industry body arguing against such levies. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The doubling is dated only to the year, so it cannot be placed inside or outside the window ([levy](https://www.gsma.com/mobilefordevelopment/)).<br><br>Naming the source's interest matters here: an industry body arguing against mobile money levies is the only account of this one, and no government or regulator statement of the rate or its yield is held.</details> | No change |
| Zimbabwe | The [National Payment Systems Act](https://zimlii.org/akn/zw/act/2001/21/eng@2016-12-31) of 2001 is the standing statute, and Statutory Instrument 65 of 2026 opened a licensed fintech sandbox at the Victoria Falls financial centre. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Act of 2001, commenced on 16 November 2001 and consolidated to 31 December 2016, provides for the recognition, operation, regulation and supervision of payment, clearing and settlement systems, electronic payment instructions included ([Act](https://zimlii.org/akn/zw/act/2001/21/eng@2016-12-31)).<br><br>Statutory Instrument 65 of 2026, made under section 81 of the Banking Act, establishes a technology department and a financial technology lab at the Victoria Falls International Financial Services Centre, with application, licensing, variation, suspension, monitoring and interim and final reporting for tested activities ([regulations](https://veritaszim.net/sites/veritas_d/files/SI%202026-065%20Banking%20%28Victoria%20Falls%20International%20Financial%20Services%20Centre%29%20%28Financial%20Technology%29%20Regulations%2C%202026.pdf)).<br><br>The instrument is held as a capture truncated near section 9, so the licensing conditions themselves are not on file.</details> | Advanced |

## Legislation enabling data interoperability

| Country | Developments | Progress |
|---|---|---|
| Algeria | A [national data-governance system was launched](http://news.radioalgerie.dz/fr/node/79376) in February 2026 under a presidential decree of December 2025. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-02-09 - the prime minister [launched the national data-governance system established by a presidential decree of 30 December 2025](http://news.radioalgerie.dz/fr/node/79376).<br><br>The trust-services law of February 2026 is the other enabling instrument: it [establishes the national trust-service and electronic-identification framework across 116 articles and requires trust-service-provider data to be hosted nationally](https://www.mpt.gov.dz/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/Loi-n%C2%B0-26-02.Services-de-Confiance.Identification-electronique.FR_.pdf).<br><br>Neither is accompanied by an operating rule, a participating body, a dataset exchanged or a service built on it, so what is established is the legal frame and not its use.</details> | Advanced |
| Benin | The exchange's founding decree of March 2020 [is in force and now on file](https://api.xroad.bj/media/617ac98a74baa_D%C3%A9cret%20n%C2%B02020-209%20du%2018%20mars%202020%20portant%20mise%20en%20place%20de%20la%20Plateforme%20d%27Interop%C3%A9rabilit%C3%A9%20XRoad%20BJ%20%281%29.pdf), acquired as a signed scan rather than a gazette copy. <details><summary>Full record</summary>[The decree instituting the national exchange was signed on 18 March 2020 and remains in force](https://api.xroad.bj/media/617ac98a74baa_D%C3%A9cret%20n%C2%B02020-209%20du%2018%20mars%202020%20portant%20mise%20en%20place%20de%20la%20Plateforme%20d%27Interop%C3%A9rabilit%C3%A9%20XRoad%20BJ%20%281%29.pdf). What moved in the window is custody rather than law: the base acquired the signed decree in August 2026 from the operator's own media host. It is not a gazette copy, and the file is a page-image scan with no text layer, so its articles cannot be quoted.<br><br>No amendment, implementing decree or successor statute appears. The instruments that have moved are administrative — the access conditions and the architecture referentials reported elsewhere — which is how a six-year-old decree comes to govern an exchange carrying 32.7m requests a month.</details> | No change |
| Botswana | The [Digital Services Act supplies the enabling law for data interoperability](http://www.bocra.org.bw/sites/default/files/sites/default/files/documents/digital_services_ACT.pdf), at sections 12 and 22. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2025-10-17 — [the Act requires a standardised framework for secure exchange of information and an information gateway](http://www.bocra.org.bw/sites/default/files/sites/default/files/documents/digital_services_ACT.pdf), the first statutory basis for cross-government data exchange the base holds.<br><br>No regulations have been made under either section, and the base carries nothing on the gateway being built.</details> | Advanced |
| Burkina Faso | A [Prime Ministerial order of 23 May 2018 adopted the Referentiel general d'interoperabilite](https://burkina24.com/2019/10/15/burkina-un-referentiel-pour-moderniser-ladministration-publique/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[The Referentiel general d'interoperabilite was adopted by Prime Ministerial arrete of 23 May 2018, fixing the rules ensuring compatibility between public administrations' information systems; a training seminar on it was held in October 2019](https://burkina24.com/2019/10/15/burkina-un-referentiel-pour-moderniser-ladministration-publique/).<br><br>This is a secondary account: the framework's own text could not be fetched from the ministry's server. Nothing states which administrations comply with it, or whether any system has been certified against it.</details> | No change, a standing instrument the base holds for the first time |
| Burundi | A 2018 presidential decree [is the closest instrument to legislation enabling data sharing between public bodies](https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/bur205433.pdf); nothing moved on it. <details><summary>Full record</summary>[Decret n 100/085 du 25 juillet 2018 establishes the national framework for the collection, dissemination, access, archiving and security of data and micro-data](https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/bur205433.pdf). It defines administrative data as data collected by public institutions in the exercise of their functions, brings administrative data into the dissemination regime, and provides for making data available by electronic means, with institutional public users and rules on access.<br><br>It is an enabling instrument rather than a mandate: it does not require any two public bodies to connect, and the base holds no implementing text, no data-sharing agreement made under it and no platform operating on its authority.</details> | No change |
| Cape Verde | The [interoperability standards diploma announced for 2024 is unadopted](https://www.governo.cv/ministra-edna-oliveira-ouvida-na-1a-comissao-especializada-no-ambito-da-discussao-na-especialidade-oe2024/) while interoperability is asserted as working. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The diploma was [announced for drafting in 2024 under the state-modernisation strategy, with no adoption on record](https://www.governo.cv/ministra-edna-oliveira-ouvida-na-1a-comissao-especializada-no-ambito-da-discussao-na-especialidade-oe2024/).<br><br>There is no evidence it was adopted two years past its stated year, while [interoperability is asserted as a working property of the government portal](https://expressodasilhas.cv/pais/2026/02/24/governo-lanca-portal-govcv-modernizar-os-servicos-publicos/101584) and of the company registry. The gap between an asserted interoperability layer and an unadopted standards instrument is the finding: what public systems must do to connect, and who decides, is set by nothing published.</details> | Stalled |
| Central African Republic | A national platform [became operationally effective at one ministry](https://primature.cf/2026/02/27/les-activites-du-premier-ministre-chef-du-gouvernement-du-23-fevrier-2026/) in February 2026; no interoperability statute is held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-02 - a national platform [became operationally effective at the planning ministry, with data migrated, staff use made mandatory and a launch ceremony held](https://mepci-rca.org/2026/02/05/digitalisation-du-ministere-de-leconomie-du-plan-et-de-la-cooperation-internationale-devenue-effective-entre-audace-et-realisme/), [launched publicly in late February](https://primature.cf/2026/02/27/les-activites-du-premier-ministre-chef-du-gouvernement-du-23-fevrier-2026/). It is the first working government data platform the base holds.<br><br>The architecture it would sit in is commissioned rather than built: the digital governance project's procurement plan carries [a US$300,000 technical-assistance contract for interoperability architecture](https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/099090225122538742/pdf/P174620-0d2f5052-a9a7-41f7-a2d3-1807ec27df8f.pdf).<br><br>No statute, decree or standard obliging interoperability appears anywhere on this ledger, so what enables exchange here is a platform and a contract, not a law.</details> | Advanced |
| Chad | A [digital code](https://techafricanews.com/2026/04/29/chad-forms-committee-to-overhaul-digital-telecom-and-postal-legal-framework/) has no adopted text: a forty-five-day drafting deadline set in April 2026 expired in June with nothing on record. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The code was described as finalised in December 2025 and a committee was then given forty-five days from 27 April 2026 to draft the framework; that deadline expired about 11 June with no text on record ([code](https://techafricanews.com/2026/04/29/chad-forms-committee-to-overhaul-digital-telecom-and-postal-legal-framework/), [finalisation](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news-digital/1012-51293-chad-finalizes-digital-law-to-align-national-framework-with-global-standards), [agency](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news-digital/1603-53795-chad-approves-reform-strengthening-national-cybersecurity-agency)).<br><br>The committee itself was inaugurated with thirty-four members and mandated to build on prior regulator contributions, and no output is on record ([committee](https://techafricanews.com/2026/04/29/chad-forms-committee-to-overhaul-digital-telecom-and-postal-legal-framework/)).<br><br>A ministry system prohibition and an interoperability programme are both recorded separately as depending on a framework that does not exist.</details> | Stalled |
| Cote d'Ivoire | A [2017 ordinance](https://anssi.gouv.ci/documents/22/Ordonnance_2017-500_02_ao%C3%BBt_2017_sur_les_eservices.pdf) requires an interoperability reference framework, a platform every authority must join, and a bar on asking users for data the state already holds. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The statute is the enabling law and it is unchanged in the window. It requires a general interoperability reference framework fixing the technical rules, data repositories, norms and standards administrative authorities must use; requires the State to put in place an e-services interoperability platform every administrative authority is bound to connect to, giving existing systems two years to conform; and [forbids an authority requiring a user to supply information already produced to any authority whose system respects that framework](https://anssi.gouv.ci/documents/22/Ordonnance_2017-500_02_ao%C3%BBt_2017_sur_les_eservices.pdf).<br><br>2026-01-16 - the identification office and the post office launched an application giving every holder of the national identification number a digital post box, and the office [placed the national register of natural persons at the centre of it, stating that embedding a certified identity is what guarantees transaction reliability, data protection and traceability](https://www.oneci.ci/actualites/1768657500) - the once-only principle working in one service rather than across the administration.<br><br>The two-year conformity deadline the ordinance set fell in 2019. Nothing on the ledger records it being met, enforced or extended.</details> | No change, the 2017 statute stands unamended and its two-year conformity deadline is eight years past |
| Djibouti | A [2022 law gives the digital economy ministry a statutory mandate over the interoperability of state information systems](https://www.journalofficiel.dj/texte-juridique/loi-n164-an-22-8eme-l-portant-missions-organisation-et-fonctionnement-du-ministere-delegue-charge-de-leconomie-numerique-et-de-linnovation/), unchanged in the period. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The law establishing the ministry [charges it with ensuring the coherence and interoperability of information systems by design, breaking down ministerial application silos and enabling data sharing between administrations through a dedicated platform](https://www.journalofficiel.dj/texte-juridique/loi-n164-an-22-8eme-l-portant-missions-organisation-et-fonctionnement-du-ministere-delegue-charge-de-leconomie-numerique-et-de-linnovation/), in force since 27 July 2022.<br><br>It is a mandate on a ministry rather than a duty on data holders: no law compels an administration to expose its data, and no implementing decree under this article is held. The [interoperability framework the state information-systems agency maintains](https://thedocs.worldbank.org/en/doc/e3b093b92e0531dcb5994e53e1b0733d-0460012022/original/Cadre-d-Interop-rabilit-de-Djibouti.pdf) is a technical document with no enacting instrument on file.</details> | No change |
| Egypt | The [data centre and cloud licensing framework](https://www.tra.gov.eg/ar/%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AA%D9%86%D8%B8%D9%8A%D9%85/%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A3%D8%B7%D8%B1-%D9%88%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%82%D9%88%D8%A7%D8%B9%D8%AF-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AA%D9%86%D8%B8%D9%8A%D9%85%D9%8A%D8%A9/%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A3%D8%B7%D8%B1-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AA%D9%86%D8%B8%D9%8A%D9%85%D9%8A%D8%A9-%D9%84%D8%AE%D8%AF%D9%85%D8%A7%D8%AA-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A7%D8%AA%D8%B5%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A7%D8%AA/%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A5%D8%B7%D8%A7%D8%B1-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AA%D9%86%D8%B8%D9%8A%D9%85%D9%8A-%D9%84%D8%A5%D9%86%D8%B4%D8%A7%D8%A1-%D9%88%D8%AA%D8%B4%D8%BA%D9%8A%D9%84-%D9%85%D8%B1%D8%A7%D9%83%D8%B2-%D8%A7/) is the operative rule; no interoperability statute is held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The regulator's [licensing and registration framework for establishing and operating data centres and providing hosting and cloud computing services](https://www.tra.gov.eg/ar/%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AA%D9%86%D8%B8%D9%8A%D9%85/%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A3%D8%B7%D8%B1-%D9%88%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%82%D9%88%D8%A7%D8%B9%D8%AF-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AA%D9%86%D8%B8%D9%8A%D9%85%D9%8A%D8%A9/%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A3%D8%B7%D8%B1-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AA%D9%86%D8%B8%D9%8A%D9%85%D9%8A%D8%A9-%D9%84%D8%AE%D8%AF%D9%85%D8%A7%D8%AA-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A7%D8%AA%D8%B5%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A7%D8%AA/%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A5%D8%B7%D8%A7%D8%B1-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AA%D9%86%D8%B8%D9%8A%D9%85%D9%8A-%D9%84%D8%A5%D9%86%D8%B4%D8%A7%D8%A1-%D9%88%D8%AA%D8%B4%D8%BA%D9%8A%D9%84-%D9%85%D8%B1%D8%A7%D9%83%D8%B2-%D8%A7/) is the operative rule beneath the estate, issued under the telecommunications law.<br><br>The one law that obliges an exchange is sectoral: [the 2025 real estate identifier law establishes a unified non-repeating national identifier database built on a unified base map](https://projects.sharkia.gov.eg/legislations/file/183), which is a shared key by statute.<br><br>No general statute requiring public bodies to exchange data, and no once-only rule, appears anywhere on this ledger.</details> | No change, a first stated position with no earlier account behind it |
| Equatorial Guinea | A bill creating an [electronic public procurement information system](https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/noticias/inicio_del_estudio_en_lectura_unica_del_proyecto_de_ley_de_contratos_del_sector_publico) went to single-reading study in November 2025. <details><summary>Full record</summary>No statute enables data exchange across Equatoguinean agencies. The [electronic laws of 2015-2017](https://palaciodelpueblo.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/02/LEYES-ELECTRONICAS.pdf) address protection, retention and signature, not exchange.<br><br>The nearest thing to an enabling instrument is sectoral: the [public sector contracts bill creating an electronic procurement information system](https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/noticias/inicio_del_estudio_en_lectura_unica_del_proyecto_de_ley_de_contratos_del_sector_publico), opened for single-reading study in November 2025. The [business single window](https://xn--embajadadeguineaecuatorialenespaa-yfd.com/decreto-de-creacion-de-la-ventanilla-unica-empresarial/) remains the only decree that folds several registers into one process.</details> | Advanced |
| Eritrea | No law enables data exchange between government systems; a 2022 assessment records no service bus and no cross-system exchange ([dataset](https://digitalidentityindex.com/countries/eritrea/govtech/)). <details><summary>Full record</summary>A structured government-technology dataset for 2022 records no government interoperability framework and no system that exchanges data across financial management, human resources, payroll, customs, tax or social-insurance systems ([dataset](https://digitalidentityindex.com/countries/eritrea/govtech/)).<br><br>A regional ministerial assessment places Eritrea's data-protection policy area at a nascent stage without legislation or regulatory oversight ([policy matrix](https://hoainitiative.org/sites/default/files/2025-09/25th%20HoAI%20Ministerial%20-%20Background%20Paper%20on%20Digital%20Integration%20Update.pdf)), so there is also no legal basis on which systems could lawfully share personal data.<br><br>The one system that did move is closed rather than connected: the customs platform runs on an intranet architecture independent of continuous internet access ([account](https://www.undp.org/eritrea/news/eritrea-launches-landmark-digital-customs-system-modernizing-trade)).</details> | No change |
| Eswatini | No Act enables data interoperability. The [Electronic Communications and Transactions Act](https://www.gov.sz/images/ICT/ELECTRONIC-COMMUNICATIOACT.pdf) is the nearest instrument, and it does not create a duty to share. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Act of 2022 is the operative electronic-transactions statute and the closest legal basis for exchange between public bodies; nothing in the base creates an interoperability obligation ([Act](https://www.gov.sz/images/ICT/ELECTRONIC-COMMUNICATIOACT.pdf)).<br><br>The obligations that do exist run the other way. The data protection authority requires every controller to document its data flows, storage and sharing, which constrains exchange rather than mandating it ([directive](https://edpa.org.sz/uploads/publications/Data_Mapping_Guideline_3.pdf)).<br><br>Where systems are being joined it is by project rather than statute: the 2026 budget speech reports the first release of an integrated financial management information system ([budget speech](https://parliament.gov.sz/media/speeches/budget/2026_27%20FY%20Budget_Speech_2026%20FINAL.pdf)).<br><br>More than half of ministries still lack meaningful system interoperability.</details> | No change, no interoperability statute and the duties that exist are protective |
| Ethiopia | No instrument compels agencies to integrate with the unified service platform, [on its own regional executives' account](https://birrmetrics.com/mesob-meets-the-monster-it-was-built-to-kill/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>Regional executives of the unified public-service platform say no legal framework obliges agencies to join it, so integration rests on agreement between them ([platform review](https://birrmetrics.com/mesob-meets-the-monster-it-was-built-to-kill/)).<br><br>The base holds no interoperability proclamation, no data-sharing regulation and no mandate under the e-government interoperability framework, which has itself been in development since 2024.</details> | No change |
| Gabon | An [ordinance of September 2025](https://www.gabonreview.com/e-gouvernement-le-gabon-se-dote-dun-cadre-juridique-pour-accelerer-la-digitalisation-de-son-administration/) makes interconnection between administrations compulsory and mandates digitisation of administrative services. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The ordinance on digitalisation and electronic government was signed by the President and published in the gazette of 7 September 2025, carrying compulsory interconnection between administrations and a national-preference clause in digital public procurement ([the ordinance](https://www.gabonreview.com/e-gouvernement-le-gabon-se-dote-dun-cadre-juridique-pour-accelerer-la-digitalisation-de-son-administration/)).<br><br>Its number comes from the minister's own commentary rather than from the instrument ([ministerial commentary](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/public-management/gabon-s-2026-digital-budget-soars-by-156-to-fund-tech-overhaul)), and the base holds no article text or gazette reference.<br><br>It names no platform and no standard, so the obligation to interconnect exists without anything to interconnect through.</details> | Advanced |
| Gambia | The [2019 Act](https://gicta.gov.gm/functions-of-gicta/) gives the ICT agency the powers an exchange would need; none has been exercised in a published instrument. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The agency's [own statement of its functions](https://gicta.gov.gm/functions-of-gicta/) under the 2019 Act covers a government data bank, technical standards, electronic signatures and cloud services.<br><br>What is missing is any instrument made under them. The [Data Protection and Privacy Bill 2024 reached assented stage](https://assembly.gm/bills) on the Assembly's own tracker in July 2026, which supplies the safeguard side; the exchange side remains a proposal in the [e-government strategy](https://ovp.gov.gm/wp-content/uploads/2023/10/E-GOVERNMENT-STRATEGY.pdf).</details> | No change |
| Ghana | A [data harmonisation bill](https://www.graphic.com.gh/news/general-news/ghanas-data-harmonisation-bill-2025-to-be-opened-for-public-consultation-from-nov-19-to-27-2025.html) was consulted on in November 2025 and a separate data exchange Act was named in July 2026; neither has been laid. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The harmonisation bill went to public consultation from 19 to 27 November 2025 and would make the information technology agency both regulator and technical enforcer of the standards; it was renamed an Act in the July 2026 artificial-intelligence plan and has not been laid before Parliament ([bill](https://www.graphic.com.gh/news/general-news/ghanas-data-harmonisation-bill-2025-to-be-opened-for-public-consultation-from-nov-19-to-27-2025.html)).<br><br>The ministry names a data exchange Act as a statute distinct from harmonisation, carrying interoperability and inter-agency sharing, with no draft or timetable ([exchange Act](https://thechronicle.com.gh/government-rolls-out-10-year-ai-implementation-roadmap-to-drive-jobs-economic-growth/)).<br><br>Two instruments have been named for the same problem and neither exists as a text the base can read.</details> | Advanced |
| Guinea-Bissau | A national interoperability regulation for public and private digital systems [was approved by the government in August 2026](https://pt.linkedin.com/posts/wardip-guin%C3%A9-bissau_governo-aprova-regulamento-nacional-de-interoperabilidade-activity-7491768929355194368-Vk5Q), with no decree number or in-force date named. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-08-08 — the WARDIP programme [announced government approval of a national interoperability framework regulating how public and private digital systems and platforms connect](https://pt.linkedin.com/posts/wardip-guin%C3%A9-bissau_governo-aprova-regulamento-nacional-de-interoperabilidade-activity-7491768929355194368-Vk5Q), presented as Guinea-Bissau's contribution to the regional programme.<br><br>It is the one instrument of the three [draft decrees the Council of Ministers approved on 10 July 2025](https://ang.gw/governo-aprova-projeto-de-regulamento-sobre-a-interoperabilidade-de-sistemas-e-plataformas-digitais/) that has moved since. What the record does not carry is the instrument itself: no decree number, no gazettal and no entry-into-force date, and the approval is reported by the World Bank-backed programme that supports the work rather than by an official journal.<br><br>No data exchange platform appears on this ledger for the regulation to govern.</details> | Advanced |
| Kenya | Interoperability duties are arriving sector by sector: the [Digital Health Act](https://new.kenyalaw.org/akn/ke/act/2023/15) in 2023, an agricultural information bill in 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Digital Health Act of November 2023 is the clearest statutory basis for data exchange in a sector, carrying its own data exchange regulations ([act](https://new.kenyalaw.org/akn/ke/act/2023/15)).<br><br>A Digital Agricultural Information Bill published in March 2026 would establish a centre for digital agricultural information with statutory functions ([bill](https://kilimo.go.ke/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/THE-DRAFT-DIGITAL-AGRICULTURAL-INFORMATION-BILL-04.03.2026-1.pdf)).<br><br>A national data governance policy remains in validation ([validation](https://www.ict.go.ke/government-moves-strengthen-kenyas-data-governance-framework)). What the base does not hold is any general statute establishing a duty to share data across government; each instrument enables one sector.</details> | Advanced |
| Lesotho | No dedicated interoperability statute exists; the [VAT e-invoicing regulations are the binding data-exchange rule](https://www.rsl.org.ls/sites/default/files/2026-04/VAT%20-%20E-Invoicing%20Regulations%20No.25%20of%202026%20%283%29_0.pdf) the base holds. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2012-02-22 — [the Data Protection Act is the closest enacted instrument bearing on lawful sharing of personal government data](https://lesotholii.org/akn/ls/act/2012/5/eng@2012-02-22).<br><br>2026-03-27 — [Legal Notice 25 of 2026 requires system-to-system electronic transmission of VAT transaction data](https://www.rsl.org.ls/sites/default/files/2026-04/VAT%20-%20E-Invoicing%20Regulations%20No.25%20of%202026%20%283%29_0.pdf), a binding interoperability rule confined to one revenue stream.<br><br>2025-08-26 — [a workshop opened to develop a digital public infrastructure framework](https://www.lena.gov.ls/infrastructure-central-to-digital-journey-ps/).<br><br>No statute enabling data exchange across government generally is held.</details> | Advanced, on a tax regulation rather than a general statute |
| Liberia | [Executive Order 147](https://emansion.gov.lr/sites/default/files/documents/Executive%20Order%20147%20%20-%20Mandatory%20Enrollment%20of%20all%20Citizens%20and%20the%20use%20of%20NBIC_0.pdf) compels inter-agency verification against the identity register; no statute enables data exchange generally. <details><summary>Full record</summary>[Executive Order 147](https://emansion.gov.lr/sites/default/files/documents/Executive%20Order%20147%20%20-%20Mandatory%20Enrollment%20of%20all%20Citizens%20and%20the%20use%20of%20NBIC_0.pdf) requires all public and private institutions to use the registry database, the Identity Query Portal and the e-Verification platform, which is the only operative instrument compelling data sharing across Liberian agencies.<br><br>It is an executive order resting on the [2011 Act](https://citizenshiprightsafrica.org/wp-content/uploads/2021/11/Liberia-NIR-Act-2011.pdf), not a data-exchange statute. [Executive Order 163](https://www.emansion.gov.lr/media/press-release/president-boakai-issues-landmark-executive-order-no-163-launch-national) of April 2026 mandates a Government Interoperability Framework but is likewise executive.</details> | No change |
| Libya | A [2018 decision](https://lawsociety.ly/legislation/%d9%82%d8%b1%d8%a7%d8%b1-%d8%b1%d9%82%d9%85-130-%d9%84%d8%b3%d9%86%d8%a9-2018-%d9%85-%d8%a8%d8%b4%d8%a3%d9%86-%d8%a5%d8%b9%d8%a7%d8%af%d8%a9-%d8%aa%d9%88%d8%b2%d9%8a%d8%b9-%d8%a7%d8%ae%d8%aa%d8%b5/) hands the information authority the national database and the mandate to interconnect state bodies. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [decision](https://lawsociety.ly/legislation/%d9%82%d8%b1%d8%a7%d8%b1-%d8%b1%d9%82%d9%85-130-%d9%84%d8%b3%d9%86%d8%a9-2018-%d9%85-%d8%a8%d8%b4%d8%a3%d9%86-%d8%a5%d8%b9%d8%a7%d8%af%d8%a9-%d8%aa%d9%88%d8%b2%d9%8a%d8%b9-%d8%a7%d8%ae%d8%aa%d8%b5/) is the operative instrument: it redistributes the national number project's competences and gives the authority both the database and the linkage mandate.<br><br>The service it produced is running — [electronic linkage connecting national bodies to the central database](https://www.gia.gov.ly/interoperability/), with no civil registry link named — while the framework that would standardise it is [still only proposed](https://www.gia.gov.ly/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/Libya-GIA-Interoperability-Plan2.pdf).</details> | No change |
| Madagascar | A [draft electronic-signature and interoperability decree](https://newsmada.com/2025/09/13/signature-electronique-et-interoperabilite-madagascar-renforce-le-cadre-juridique/) was validated at a ministry workshop in September 2025 and has not been adopted. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The 2015 electronic-signature law has had no implementing decree; the draft validated in September 2025 would supply one and provides for a national interoperability committee ([draft](https://newsmada.com/2025/09/13/signature-electronique-et-interoperabilite-madagascar-renforce-le-cadre-juridique/)).<br><br>The minister named the recasting as a contribution to a future digital code, which is not held. A year on, no adoption, gazette reference or committee appointment is recorded.</details> | Advanced |
| Malawi | A proposed [government data sharing regulation](https://itweb.africa/article/malawi-devises-operational-rules-for-national-data-exchange-platform/xnklOvz1R2bq4Ymz) would route all data requests through the national data centre and require binding sharing agreements, and it is not made. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Ministries and agencies had been sharing citizen data by email, USB drive and paper, and the platform operated without binding rules ([regulation](https://itweb.africa/article/malawi-devises-operational-rules-for-national-data-exchange-platform/xnklOvz1R2bq4Ymz)).<br><br>The proposal would require encryption in transit and at rest, set a common digital standard for system-to-system exchange, and require binding data sharing agreements with usage limits and audit trails. No draft text is held, and the minister described a binding regulation as the missing piece in an account given during a vendor meeting abroad.</details> | Advanced |
| Mauritania | [Decree 2022-009](https://mtnima.gov.mr/sites/default/files/2024-02/D%C3%A9cret%20n%C2%B02022-009%20Relatif%20aux%20%C3%A9changes%20%C3%A9lectroniques%20entre%20les%20usagers%20et%20les%20autorit%C3%A9s%20administratives%20et%20entre%20les%20autorit%C3%A9s%20administratives_%5BJ.O%20n%C2%B01505%5D.pdf) governs electronic exchanges between users and administrations and between administrations, under the electronic transactions law of 2018. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The decree was made on the digital ministry's report under law n. 2018-022 of 12 June 2018 on electronic transactions, and is the legal footing the service portals rest on ([decree](https://mtnima.gov.mr/sites/default/files/2024-02/D%C3%A9cret%20n%C2%B02022-009%20Relatif%20aux%20%C3%A9changes%20%C3%A9lectroniques%20entre%20les%20usagers%20et%20les%20autorit%C3%A9s%20administratives%20et%20entre%20les%20autorit%C3%A9s%20administratives_%5BJ.O%20n%C2%B01505%5D.pdf)).<br><br>No implementing arrangement, register of compliant administrations or evaluation is held,; the national data management system it would serve is still in development, and the base holds no register of administrations exchanging under it.</details> | No change |
| Mauritius | An [omnibus bill](https://maurice-info.mu/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/The-Economic-and-Financial-Measures-Miscellaneous-Provisions-Bill.pdf) of 119 pages amending fifty-eight Acts carries the fintech committee, the artificial-intelligence city scheme, a threat-sharing platform and a digital travel authorisation. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The bill was introduced on 24 July 2026 with staged commencement from 1 September and several provisions deemed retrospective ([bill](https://maurice-info.mu/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/The-Economic-and-Financial-Measures-Miscellaneous-Provisions-Bill.pdf)).<br><br>A secondary account put the figure at some sixty laws; the enacting text gives fifty-eight amended and one repealed ([account](https://lexpress.mu/node/560855)). It is the single largest legislative vehicle in the country's digital record, and it was introduced and not enacted.</details> | Advanced |
| Morocco | A [Digital X.0 framework bill](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/tech/morocco-drafts-landmark-law-to-govern-ai-data-and-digital-identity) sits with the government's general secretariat on three pillars, one of them interoperability on traceable consent. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The bill was under review by the general secretariat in November 2025, on pillars of data governance, sectoral digital identity, and interoperability resting on traceable consent ([bill](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/tech/morocco-drafts-landmark-law-to-govern-ai-data-and-digital-identity), [account](https://www.biometricupdate.com/202511/morocco-prepares-new-legislation-to-accelerate-digital-transformation)).<br><br>The ministry frames it as sitting under the 2009 data-protection law rather than replacing it. No text, article count or tabling date is on record, and nothing has been published about it since.</details> | Advanced |
| Namibia | The [Electronic Transactions Act](http://www.lac.org.na/laws/2019/7068.pdf) of 2019 is the enabling statute, and its electronic-signature and accreditation provisions were brought into force in June 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Act of 2019 is the standing statute for electronic transactions and remains the interim instrument law enforcement relies on for cybercrime ([Act](http://www.lac.org.na/laws/2019/7068.pdf)).<br><br>Electronic signature regulations were gazetted in December 2025 and the regulator separately gazetted regulations for accrediting security products, services and their providers; the Act's section 20 and its accreditation chapter were brought into force in June 2026 ([signatures](https://www.lac.org.na/laws/2025/8814.pdf), [accreditation](https://www.lac.org.na/laws/2025/8808.pdf), [commencement](https://www.lac.org.na/laws/2026/8949.pdf)).<br><br>The regulator states it is the national root certification authority, and legally recognised electronic signatures still had no launch on record in July 2026.<br><br>Trust infrastructure is now legislated. What is not legislated is a duty on public bodies to make their systems talk to each other.</details> | Advanced |
| Nigeria | A [National Digital Economy and E-Governance Bill](https://techcabal.com/2025/11/11/nigerias-digital-economy-bill-hands-sweeping-powers-to-nitda/) before the assembly would elevate the technology agency to a super regulator, with overlap flagged by stakeholders. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The bill offers a unified legal framework with broad supervisory and enforcement powers, and also carries the risk-based artificial-intelligence regime ([bill](https://techcabal.com/2025/11/11/nigerias-digital-economy-bill-hands-sweeping-powers-to-nitda/)).<br><br>Its electronic-signature provisions overlap with the root certification authority the identity statute already created, and which instrument governs digital signatures is unresolved on the record.</details> | Advanced |
| Rwanda | The [2023 single digital identity law](https://www.nida.gov.rw/fileadmin/user_upload/NIDA/Publications/OG_n___Special_of_15.06.2023_Iyandikwa_ry_abaturage_muri_sisitemu_imwe_y_Igihugu_indangamuntu_koranabuhanga.pdf) requires relying-party integration and synchronisation with civil registration; the [strategy above it](https://www.risa.gov.rw/news-detail/rwanda-advances-digital-future-with-launch-of-national-dpi-strategy) followed in March 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The law enacted in June 2023 governs population registration in the single digital identity system, defines authentication, obliges relying parties to integrate, and requires synchronisation with civil registration ([law](https://www.nida.gov.rw/fileadmin/user_upload/NIDA/Publications/OG_n___Special_of_15.06.2023_Iyandikwa_ry_abaturage_muri_sisitemu_imwe_y_Igihugu_indangamuntu_koranabuhanga.pdf)).<br><br>That is the clearest statutory obligation to interoperate the base holds for any Rwandan system.<br><br>The digital public infrastructure strategy launched in March 2026 sets the architecture around it ([launch](https://www.risa.gov.rw/news-detail/rwanda-advances-digital-future-with-launch-of-national-dpi-strategy)), and the shared data hub that would carry general exchange is still at pre-procurement ([minutes](https://www.risa.gov.rw/index.php?eID=dumpFile&f=151133&t=f&token=ea2f00488a3d4ebb4cde95d0c365c403869d9fb7)).</details> | Advanced |
| Sao Tome and Principe | A [National Framework for Interoperability](https://www.ibanet.org/document?id=Digital-Regulations-in-the-Metaverse-Era-Sao-Tome-Principe) was enacted as Law 1/2024 for public-sector data sharing, and only a secondary legal survey describes it. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The law is held through the same 2024 legal-survey chapter as the data-protection law, not through its own text ([law](https://www.ibanet.org/document?id=Digital-Regulations-in-the-Metaverse-Era-Sao-Tome-Principe)).<br><br>It is the stated legal basis for the civil-registry to electoral-database linkage recorded separately, and the base now also holds a civil-registry exchange interface ([interface](https://leginon.dgrn.gov.st/)), a health platform ([health](https://minsaude.st/sis/)) and a social protection system integrated with the social registry ([social protection](https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/099091725161547918/txt/BOSIB-92986af7-9307-44ac-b3b9-5f75cb4949c8.txt)).<br><br>Each was built to its own design. The framework the law would be applied through is a draft from 2021 ([framework](https://inic.gov.st/docs/QNI_STP_v0.1.pdf)), so the statute has evidenced uses and no published specification behind them.</details> | No change |
| Senegal | The interoperability decree [was still being presented as a draft in May 2026](https://fr.linkedin.com/posts/mctnsn_dpi-newdealtechnologique-activity-7457180609757528065-UqRT); the nearest enacted duty is a [five-day request transfer](https://www.archives.sn/api/fichiers/e05027c9-f6f2-4f70-a313-b0ee81cf2c08). <details><summary>Full record</summary>On 4 May 2026 the ministry [convened public-sector actors, stating that the state still runs information systems built in isolation with different architectures and fragmented data, and announced that the following day would cover presentation of the draft decree framing the interoperability platform, the national interoperability framework, data-exchange architectures, technical norms and security requirements](https://fr.linkedin.com/posts/mctnsn_dpi-newdealtechnologique-activity-7457180609757528065-UqRT). As at May 2026 the framework decree was a draft, not an adopted and published act.<br><br>The operative published instrument is narrower: a [2024 decree charging the digital minister with establishing transversal governance of state information systems and putting in place a data-hosting status](https://primature.sn/publications/lois-et-reglements/decret-ndeg-2024-953-relatif-aux-attributions-du-ministre-de-la). It vests a competence; it does not oblige administrations to share data.<br><br>The nearest enacted transfer duty sits in the access law: article 15 obliges a body that does not hold the requested information to [transfer the request to the body likely to hold it within five days](https://www.archives.sn/api/fichiers/e05027c9-f6f2-4f70-a313-b0ee81cf2c08).</details> | No change, the framework decree remained a draft through the window |
| Seychelles | The [payment licensing regulations](https://www.gazette.sc/sites/default/files/2025-12/SI%2099%202025%20No%202%20-%20National%20Payment%20System%20%28Licensing%20and%20Authorisation%29%20%28Amendment%29%20Regulations%202025.pdf) make straight-through processing a licence condition; a National Addressing System Bill was Cabinet-approved in April 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The December 2025 regulations require payment, clearing and settlement operators to hold safe and reliable IT systems and adequate interfaces for straight-through processing, the clearest statutory interoperability duty the base holds ([regulations](https://www.gazette.sc/sites/default/files/2025-12/SI%2099%202025%20No%202%20-%20National%20Payment%20System%20%28Licensing%20and%20Authorisation%29%20%28Amendment%29%20Regulations%202025.pdf)).<br><br>Cabinet approved the National Addressing System Bill in April 2026, creating a National Addressing Department and Database Unit as a shared reference-data layer described as improving cross-agency access to data ([Bill](https://statehouse.gov.sc/news/6905/vice-president-pillay-outlines-cabinet-decisions-on-public-safety-system-reform-and-service-delivery)).<br><br>Both are sectoral. No general statute obliges public bodies to share or expose data to one another, and the addressing Bill is approved rather than enacted.</details> | Advanced |
| Somalia | [Law 46 of 2026](https://www.osagsomalia.com/media/documents/bulletins-contents/legislation/2026/sharciga_Dad-dhigista_iyo_Tirakoobka_Xaaladaha_Madaniga.pdf) puts civil registration on a statutory footing beside the identity system; nothing yet compels one public body to share data with another. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Population Registration and Civil Status Statistics Law, gazetted 5 April 2026, establishes the civil-registration and vital-statistics layer alongside the national identity infrastructure ([law](https://www.osagsomalia.com/media/documents/bulletins-contents/legislation/2026/sharciga_Dad-dhigista_iyo_Tirakoobka_Xaaladaha_Madaniga.pdf)).<br><br>The Data Protection Act supplies the conditions under which personal data may move, including outside the country ([Act](https://moct.gov.so/en/wp-content/uploads/2023/07/Somalia-Data-Protection-Act-2.pdf)) — permission, not obligation.<br><br>What carries interoperability in practice is administrative: a cross-institutional working group launched in June 2026 to integrate the national identity and its verification platform across public and private services ([working group](https://www.nira.gov.so/news/nira-launches-national-id-and-public-service-integration-working-group)).</details> | Advanced |
| South Africa | Draft data-exchange legislation [is a stated deliverable of the exchange pilot](https://mzansixchange.treasury.gov.za/pilot/), over [a 2014 statute that already provides for administration-wide norms and standards](https://www.gov.za/sites/default/files/gcis_document/201501/38374gon1054act11of2014.pdf). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The standing instrument is not a data law. [The Public Administration Management Act has been in force since 2014 as the frame for administration-wide arrangements across national, provincial and municipal spheres, including the transfer and secondment of employees and the establishment of common norms and standards](https://www.gov.za/sites/default/files/gcis_document/201501/38374gon1054act11of2014.pdf) — which is the authority under which service standards are set, and it says nothing about data.<br><br>[Draft data-exchange legislation is one of three named deliverables of the one-year exchange pilot](https://mzansixchange.treasury.gov.za/pilot/), alongside four task teams and interoperability standards.<br><br>So the country is running a federated data exchange in pilot with no statute behind it, and the statute is scheduled to be drafted by the pilot itself. Nothing on this ledger states who would introduce it or when.</details> | Advanced |
| South Sudan | An [ICT Authority](https://www.eyeradio.org/govt-set-to-establish-ict-authority-to-regulate-internet-service-providers-ateny/) is being established while awaiting legislation, and a May 2026 account states the President already signed its legal framework; the two are unreconciled. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Both accounts trace to one speaker. In May 2026 the minister said the law is out already and signed by the President; in August 2026 he was reported standing the body up while parliamentary legislation is awaited ([establishment](https://www.eyeradio.org/govt-set-to-establish-ict-authority-to-regulate-internet-service-providers-ateny/), [framework](https://szn.ss/south-sudan-to-establish-ict-authority-as-part-of-media-tech-reforms/)).<br><br>No bill, act, presidential order or gazette entry can be found. The justice ministry's law collection stops at 2017 and omits the cybercrime Act too, the Office of the President publishes no decrees, and the Assembly's bills register stops in 2023 — so the registers are not current enough for their silence to settle anything.<br><br>The division of competence with the existing communications regulator is unstated. The qualified status is the resting state.</details> | Mixed, an ICT Authority reported both as legally established and as awaiting legislation, from the same speaker |
| Sudan | A [draft Digital Transformation Law of 2026](https://sudantribune.net/article/317108) is in redrafting with the ministry, the third of a three-instrument package. <details><summary>Full record</summary>It went to the Council of Ministers alongside the cybersecurity and data drafts and was reported in redrafting in August 2026 ([draft](https://sudantribune.net/article/317108), [ministry](https://www.sudanindependent.com/news/politics/2026/08/11/%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B9%D8%AF%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A9-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B3%D9%88%D8%AF%D8%A7%D9%86%D9%8A%D8%A9-%D8%B9%D9%84%D9%89-%D8%A3%D8%B9%D8%AA%D8%A7%D8%A8-%D9%85%D8%B1)).<br><br>None of the three texts is published. The platforms the law would presumably govern — the national services platform, the identity credential, the civil-service records system — are already live, so the statute follows the systems rather than authorising them.</details> | Advanced |
| Tunisia | The [Development Plan 2026-2030 was promulgated as a law](https://www.9anoun.tn/fr/kb/jorts/jort-2026-073-5e980/loi-ndeg-2026-16-du-20-juillet-2026-portant-approbation-du-plan-de-developpement-2026-2030-4) in July 2026, and every volume of the annex held is marked draft. <details><summary>Full record</summary>It was promulgated on 20 July 2026 after adoption by both chambers ([law](https://www.9anoun.tn/fr/kb/jorts/jort-2026-073-5e980/loi-ndeg-2026-16-du-20-juillet-2026-portant-approbation-du-plan-de-developpement-2026-2030-4), [annex](https://www.arp.tn/loi/project/4315)).<br><br>Whether the promulgated annex differs from the version tabled and voted is not established, and no as-enacted printing has been published. That is a live problem rather than a formality: the annex is where the digital programme's project lines sit, so the legally binding content of the country's digital plan cannot be read.</details> | Advanced |
| Zambia | A [Cabinet directive on open-source technology](https://itweb.africa/article/zambias-govt-leans-towards-open-source/KjlyrvwBpprqk6am) was issued to ministries, reported only in an excerpt of a trade-press article with no government publication held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>It was issued alongside the spatial data infrastructure launch in June 2026 ([directive](https://itweb.africa/article/zambias-govt-leans-towards-open-source/KjlyrvwBpprqk6am)).<br><br>No government publication of it is held. Officials elsewhere in this ledger name fragmented, vendor-locked systems as a governance and legal problem rather than a technical one, which is the case the directive would answer — and the directive itself cannot be read.</details> | Advanced |
| Zimbabwe | No Act enabling data interoperability is held. The [Cyber and Data Protection Act](https://www.veritaszim.net/sites/veritas_d/files/Cyber%20%26%20Data%20Protection%20Act%20Cap1207%20No%205%20of%202021%20gaz%202022-03-11.pdf) governs lawful sharing, and the standards work is technical rather than statutory. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Act's transborder-flow and processing provisions are the strongest enacted instrument bearing on lawful data linking and sharing, and they constrain rather than mandate exchange ([Act](https://www.veritaszim.net/sites/veritas_d/files/Cyber%20%26%20Data%20Protection%20Act%20Cap1207%20No%205%20of%202021%20gaz%202022-03-11.pdf)).<br><br>Where interoperability is being enabled, it is by technical standard rather than legislation: a national health information exchange is in development to connect laboratory, logistics and facility systems ([exchange](https://build.fhir.org/ig/mohcc/fhir-zw-core-ig/)).<br><br>Nothing on the base creates a duty on public bodies to make their systems interoperable, or a legal basis for once-only data sharing across ministries.</details> | No change, the sharing rules sit in data-protection law and no interoperability statute exists |

## AI legislation / regulations

| Country | Developments | Progress |
|---|---|---|
| Algeria | An [ethical-artificial-intelligence law is reported in preparation](https://www.echoroukonline.com/%D8%AA%D8%B4%D8%B1%D9%8A%D8%B9%D8%A7%D8%AA-%D8%AC%D8%AF%D9%8A%D8%AF%D8%A9-%D9%84%D8%B6%D8%A8%D8%B7-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A7%D8%B3%D8%AA%D8%AE%D8%AF%D8%A7%D9%85-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A3%D8%AE%D9%84%D8%A7%D9%82), with no draft text, sponsor or timetable held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Nothing existed at the window's opening. The law is [described as in preparation, with no draft text, sponsor or timetable held](https://www.echoroukonline.com/%D8%AA%D8%B4%D8%B1%D9%8A%D8%B9%D8%A7%D8%AA-%D8%AC%D8%AF%D9%8A%D8%AF%D8%A9-%D9%84%D8%B6%D8%A8%D8%B7-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A7%D8%B3%D8%AA%D8%AE%D8%AF%D8%A7%D9%85-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A3%D8%AE%D9%84%D8%A7%D9%82) as at 2026-08-03.<br><br>That is nine months of a stated preparation with nothing published, against a national strategy whose deployment roadmap was agreed in the same period and a university assistant announced to steer module allocation and mark student answers from the 2026-2027 academic year. No rule, appeal route or disclosure duty governs any of it.</details> | Advanced |
| Angola | An 86-article artificial-intelligence bill [went to public consultation in September 2025](https://consultapublica.minttics.gov.ao/storage/docs/Proposta%20de%20Lei%20Sobre%20a%20Intelig%C3%AAncia%20Artificial.pdf), with no tabling, committee stage or vote since. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The draft [runs to 86 articles in nine chapters](https://consultapublica.minttics.gov.ao/storage/docs/Proposta%20de%20Lei%20Sobre%20a%20Intelig%C3%AAncia%20Artificial.pdf) and departs from the tiered risk pyramid used elsewhere: [a single critical-AI category carries registration, security-risk assessment and emergency-response duties, with extraterritorial reach over activity abroad affecting Angolan interests, a right to explanation and to refuse decisions made by artificial intelligence, and both criminal and strict civil liability](https://www.techhiveadvisory.africa/insights/review-of-angolas-proposed-law-on-artificial-intelligence).<br><br>It repeals no existing legislation, defers military applications to a separate instrument and excludes scientific research and purely personal use. Its financial assessment is not costed.<br><br>No tabling, committee stage or vote is on record in the eleven months since consultation opened, and the policy the law would implement does not exist: the readiness assessment reported under strategy names its absence as the central gap.</details> | Advanced |
| Benin | A four-country comparative study [found no artificial-intelligence-specific legislation in force or in draft](https://grain-africa.org/en/intelligence-artificielle-en-afrique-de-louest-grain-presente-les-resultats-dune-etude-comparative-pour-une-gouvernance-plus-inclusive-et-responsable-de-lia/); the 2023 to 2027 strategy carries the field. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-08-10 — [a four-country comparative study of West African artificial-intelligence governance found no artificial-intelligence-specific instrument in force or in draft in Benin](https://grain-africa.org/en/intelligence-artificielle-en-afrique-de-louest-grain-presente-les-resultats-dune-etude-comparative-pour-une-gouvernance-plus-inclusive-et-responsable-de-lia/), and rates a neighbour's strategy the most advanced of the four on inclusion and data governance. That is a searched absence rather than an unexamined one.<br><br>What carries the field instead is soft law: the national strategy, the data protection authority's artificial-intelligence policy and the generative-tool regime for public agents, all reported elsewhere in this report. Each binds a part of the state and none binds a private deployer, which is the gap a statute would close.</details> | No change |
| Botswana | [No AI-specific Act or regulation exists](https://www.unesco.org/ethics-ai/en/botswana); the Data Protection Act 2024 is the closest adjacent enacted instrument. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2025-12-22 — [the country profile records no AI legislation and the national policy at an advanced stage](https://www.unesco.org/ethics-ai/en/botswana).<br><br>2025-08-13 — [a minister said the policy was in final drafting](https://dailynews.gov.bw/news-detail/88106), the earliest in-window movement toward one.<br><br>The absence was searched on 31 August 2026 and nothing was found: no Bill, no regulations under an existing statute, and no announced timetable for either.</details> | No change |
| Burundi | No binding artificial-intelligence instrument was located in any national legal source; [a non-binding strategy was validated in April 2026](https://abpinfo.bi/2026/04/22/validation-de-la-strategie-nationale-de-lia/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[No Burundian loi, decret, ordonnance ministerielle or regulator reglement governing artificial intelligence was located across the official legal database, the official gazette, the presidency, both chambers, the communications regulator and the state technology secretariat](https://www.undp.org/fr/burundi/actualites/pnud-le-burundi-trace-sa-feuille-de-route-strategique-pour-lintelligence-artificielle), on a search dated to the national readiness assessment held in Bujumbura on 25-27 November 2025.<br><br>2026-04-21 — [a national validation session for the artificial-intelligence strategy was opened by the ministry responsible for the digital economy with a development agency](https://abpinfo.bi/2026/04/22/validation-de-la-strategie-nationale-de-lia/). What moved is the validation of a non-binding strategy, not any instrument: the absence the indicator asks about is unchanged and is now dated.</details> | No change |
| Chad | [No liability regime for artificial intelligence exists](https://www.unesco.org/ethics-ai/fr/chad), and the only statutory mention is the [reformed security agency's](https://www.alwihdainfo.com/Tchad-promulgation-de-la-reforme-de-l-ANSICE_a148767.html) widened remit. <details><summary>Full record</summary>UNESCO's country profile, drawn from Chad's own readiness assessment, finds that Chad regulates cyberspace, that some provisions are obsolete, and that at this stage no liability regime for artificial intelligence exists ([profile](https://www.unesco.org/ethics-ai/fr/chad)).<br><br>The March 2026 reform of the computer-security and electronic-certification agency names artificial-intelligence systems within its widened remit ([reform](https://www.alwihdainfo.com/Tchad-promulgation-de-la-reforme-de-l-ANSICE_a148767.html)) — the only statutory mention held.<br><br>Chad's declaration to the Geneva dialogue records adherence to the 2021 international recommendation and a readiness diagnostic under way, with no adopted national instrument ([declaration](https://atpe.td/gouvernance-de-lia-le-tchad-fait-entendre-sa-voix-a-geneve/)).</details> | No change |
| Comoros | An [obligation to label artificial-intelligence content entered law at article 231 of the new communication code](https://www.cnpa-comores.org/un-nouveau-souffle-pour-le-cnpa/); no dedicated statute exists. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-02 - the communication code was [modified and completed by loi n 25-021/AU, promulgated on 2 February 2026; its article 231 requires explicit labelling of content generated or heavily modified by artificial intelligence, backed by graduated administrative sanctions to one million Comorian francs and a public campaign](https://www.cnpa-comores.org/un-nouveau-souffle-pour-le-cnpa/). The [full text is now on file, running to two hundred and seventy-four articles](https://munganyo.km/public/storage/Décret N°26-023PR du 02 fév 2026 portant promul Loi N°25-021AU modifiant et completant le Code Information et de la Com.pdf).<br><br>2026-03 - the Council of Ministers [took a six-month evaluation of institutional, technical and regulatory readiness, adding the interior ministry to the committee on national-security and data-governance grounds](https://beit-salam.km/actualit%C3%A9s/conseil-des-ministres-du-11-mars-2026-des-initiatives-pour-l%E2%80%99agriculture,-la-s%C3%A9curit%C3%A9,-l%E2%80%99innovation-et-l%E2%80%99environnement.html). Its stated June conclusion has passed with nothing published.<br><br>The country now regulates one use of the technology inside a media statute, and nothing else.</details> | Advanced |
| Cote d'Ivoire | [No Ivorian artificial-intelligence statute is published](https://cipit.strathmore.edu/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/AIIPin-Africa-Final.pdf); governance runs entirely through non-binding strategy instruments. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The position at both ends of the window is that [no bill, act or implementing decree on artificial intelligence has been published](https://cipit.strathmore.edu/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/AIIPin-Africa-Final.pdf), and that is a cited absence rather than an unexamined one.<br><br>What moved instead is policy. The [draft 2026-2030 cybersecurity strategy extends state scope to artificial intelligence, emerging technologies and cloud](https://www.aip.ci/cote-divoire-aip-cybersecurite-lanssi-prepare-une-nouvelle-strategie-nationale-face-a-la-montee-des-menaces-numeriques/), and is the only binding-track vehicle doing so. A published bill, act or implementing decree would settle the indicator; nothing on file suggests one is drafted.</details> | No change |
| Djibouti | [No artificial-intelligence law, decree or procurement rule is held](https://www.wearetech.africa/fr/fils/actualites/tech/djibouti-prepare-sa-strategie-nationale-d-intelligence-artificielle); the strategy in preparation is not an instrument. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The position is unchanged at both ends of the window: [no law, decree or procurement rule governing artificial-intelligence systems in public use is held](https://www.wearetech.africa/fr/fils/actualites/tech/djibouti-prepare-sa-strategie-nationale-d-intelligence-artificielle), and the strategy under preparation is not an instrument.<br><br>It matters more here than the absence alone suggests, because systems are already in public use: a weather early-warning system in service and a memorandum for a compute zone signed. Nothing published states what a public body must do before deploying such a system, who may audit it, or what a person subject to one may contest.</details> | No change |
| Egypt | The minister told Cabinet a [regulator unit already detects and acts on artificial-intelligence-manipulated content](https://www.youm7.com/story/2026/7/29/وزير-الاتصالات-وحدة-خاصة-بالجهاز-القومي-لرصد-المخالفات-السلبية-لمواقع/7497055), and legislative amendment is needed. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-07-29 - the minister [told Cabinet that a unit had been formed inside the telecommunications regulator which already detects and acts on manipulated video and imagery, but that legislative amendment is needed to address the practices at root](https://www.youm7.com/story/2026/7/29/وزير-الاتصالات-وحدة-خاصة-بالجهاز-القومي-لرصد-المخالفات-السلبية-لمواقع/7497055). The unit's formation date, legal basis, staffing, method, appeal route and contest procedure are not stated: the detection capability operates ahead of the instrument that would authorise it.<br><br>At the same meeting the [Cabinet agreed a review of the governing social-media statutes, heavier financial penalties and public awareness campaigns, with a comparative study of other states' approaches being prepared and no bill, text or timetable stated](https://www.youm7.com/story/2026/7/29/وزير-الاتصالات-وحدة-خاصة-بالجهاز-القومي-لرصد-المخالفات-السلبية-لمواقع/7497055); the Prime Minister credited a press and media campaign with prompting the response.</details> | Advanced |
| Equatorial Guinea | A [draft artificial intelligence law](https://realequatorialguinea.com/tecnologia/guinea-ecuatorial-da-los-primeros-pasos-para-disponer-de-una-ley-de-inteligencia-artificial/) is being written, setting six guiding principles and proposing an autonomous national authority for the technology and for data. <details><summary>Full record</summary>A drafting session in August 2025 addressed splitting the proposed authority's functions, and two national institutions are named as prospective training and certification centres under the draft; no enactment, text or adoption date is published ([draft law](https://realequatorialguinea.com/tecnologia/guinea-ecuatorial-da-los-primeros-pasos-para-disponer-de-una-ley-de-inteligencia-artificial/)).<br><br>The proposed authority would supervise both artificial intelligence and data, in a country whose existing data-protection statute has had no supervisory body for a decade.</details> | Advanced |
| Eritrea | A review dated June 2026 records no dedicated artificial-intelligence law and no national strategy ([review](https://www.levellers.ai/what-is/ai-regulation-eritrea)). <details><summary>Full record</summary>A legal-status review dated 8 June 2026 states that Eritrea has no dedicated artificial-intelligence law, no national artificial-intelligence strategy, no data-protection statute and no data-protection authority, and that the nearest legal anchor is the privacy article of the 1997 constitution, which has never entered into force ([review](https://www.levellers.ai/what-is/ai-regulation-eritrea)).<br><br>The 2025 government artificial-intelligence readiness index, in its January 2026 edition, scores Eritrea zero on policy capacity and ranks it 193rd overall on a score of 13.69 ([index](https://oxfordinsights.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/01/2025-Government-AI-Readiness-Index-Report_01_26.pdf)).<br><br>The absence is total rather than partial: no instrument, no draft and no institution with a mandate.</details> | No change |
| Eswatini | No AI Act or regulation exists. The regulator's [spectrum guidelines](https://www.esccom.org.sz/publications/notices/docs/Notice%2010%20Guidelines%20on%20The%20Use%20of%20Licence%20Exempt%20Spectrum%20Bands%20in%20Eswatini.pdf) are the only binding instrument touching AI devices, and a national AI strategy is not held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The 2024 licence-exempt spectrum guidelines are the base's only binding instrument expressly touching artificial-intelligence and internet-of-things devices ([guidelines](https://www.esccom.org.sz/publications/notices/docs/Notice%2010%20Guidelines%20on%20The%20Use%20of%20Licence%20Exempt%20Spectrum%20Bands%20in%20Eswatini.pdf)).<br><br>A reviewed science, technology and innovation policy and a combined fourth and fifth industrial revolution strategy went to stakeholder validation in February 2026, framing artificial intelligence, automation and data governance inside a human-centred approach; neither document is held ([validation](https://www.linkedin.com/posts/ministry-of-ict-eswatini_rsti-stipolicyreview-digitaltransformation-activity-7432119915588812800-2Nv-)).<br><br>The Prime Minister told a global governance dialogue in July 2026, speaking for the King, that artificial-intelligence governance frameworks should protect children and uphold human dignity ([Geneva](https://times.co.sz/34851/news/pm-urges-ai-rules-to-protect-children-uphold-human-dignity/)).<br><br>The regulator has issued guidance on artificial intelligence and deepfakes in broadcasting, which is guidance and not an instrument with force. A national AI strategy was probed and not found.</details> | No change, no artificial-intelligence instrument and a strategy that is Not held |
| Ethiopia | An implementing directive gave the security agency power to designate new and emerging technology products for clearance ([directive](https://justice.gov.et/wp-content/uploads/2026/05/1135_Information_Technology_Products_Security_Clearance_and_Control-1.pdf)). <details><summary>Full record</summary>Directive 1135/2026, made under the Information Technology Products Security Clearance and Control Proclamation, creates security clearance, permit and monitoring procedures for restricted and prohibited information technology products and provides for the designation of new or emerging products — a power wide enough to reach advanced artificial-intelligence systems ([directive](https://justice.gov.et/wp-content/uploads/2026/05/1135_Information_Technology_Products_Security_Clearance_and_Control-1.pdf)).<br><br>The only instrument addressed to artificial intelligence as such remains the National Artificial Intelligence Policy adopted by the Council of Ministers in June 2024, a policy rather than a binding statute ([policy](https://www.lawethiopia.com/images/Policy_documents/Ethiopian%20ai%20policy.pdf)).<br><br>The base holds no AI act, no draft bill and no regulator with an artificial-intelligence mandate, so what governs AI here is import control and general policy.</details> | Advanced, on a security-clearance directive rather than on any statute addressed to artificial intelligence |
| Gambia | No artificial-intelligence instrument exists and the [policy is still in drafting](https://unctad.org/system/files/non-official-document/ecn162026_s08_ai_gambia_en.pdf) as at April 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The ministry's [statement to the United Nations science and technology commission](https://unctad.org/system/files/non-official-document/ecn162026_s08_ai_gambia_en.pdf) puts the national policy in development. No bill, regulation or regulator guideline addressing artificial intelligence has been laid.<br><br>Where the state has acted on synthetic content it has done so institutionally rather than legally: a [misinformation response centre](https://factguard.gov.gm/) runs a fact-check hotline with a public audit trail, staffed by human analysts.</details> | No change |
| Ghana | An [emerging technologies bill](https://digitalpolicyalert.org/blog/data-protection-in-africa-roundup) is the chosen vehicle instead of a standalone artificial-intelligence Act, and the oversight body it needs does not exist. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The draft would create an emerging-technologies agency for cross-functional compliance across artificial intelligence, blockchain, connected devices, cloud and quantum, with voluntary startup registration, and was to be laid before Parliament in 2026; no text is held ([bill](https://digitalpolicyalert.org/blog/data-protection-in-africa-roundup)).<br><br>A responsible artificial-intelligence body was proposed at a national consultation in April 2025 and provided for in the launched strategy within its first year, and sixteen months after first proposal it has no establishing instrument, budget or staff ([oversight body](https://www.graphic.com.gh/news/general-news/ghana-news-president-unveils-270m-national-ai-strategy-250m-computing-centre-20m-implementation-fund-earmarked-to-create-africas-ai-hub.html)).<br><br>Sources differ on whether it is one body or an authority plus a ministry office ([consultation](https://www.myjoyonline.com/the-road-to-approval-inside-ghanas-ai-strategy-and-knusts-leadership/), [approval](https://www.africaainews.com/p/ghana-approves-250m-ai-hub-and-national)).<br><br>A third draft would overhaul the 2008 law governing the information technology agency, licensing every technology business, certifying professionals, regulating artificial intelligence and blockchain, levying 1 per cent on services and splitting the agency's regulatory and commercial arms ([agency bill](https://www.myjoyonline.com/government-to-split-nitas-regulatory-and-commercial-functions-under-proposed-reforms/)); the minister's answer to critics is that detail comes in a later legislative instrument ([agency bill](https://www.myjoyonline.com/government-to-split-nitas-regulatory-and-commercial-functions-under-proposed-reforms/), [critics](https://www.myjoyonline.com/what-are-they-hiding-tech-consultant-questions-rush-for-15-digital-bills/), [response](https://www.myjoyonline.com/nita-bill-i-hear-the-tech-industry-but-we-wont-rewrite-rules-sam-george-to-critics-on-legislative-process/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Guinea-Bissau | No artificial intelligence instrument exists; the nearest is the [law against false online information](https://ang.gw/cnt-aprova-com-alteracoes-lei-contra-divulgacao-de-falsas-informacoes-nas-redes-sociais/) of May 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [law passed with amendments in May 2026](https://ang.gw/cnt-aprova-com-alteracoes-lei-contra-divulgacao-de-falsas-informacoes-nas-redes-sociais/) addresses false information on social networks and names no technology.<br><br>It was passed by a transitional council seated after a takeover whose leaders had [ordered radio shutdowns and blocked four major platforms nationwide](https://mfwa.org/country-highlights/guinea-bissau-military-takeover-triggers-media-suspension-online-restrictions/) in November 2025, which is the context any reading of it has to carry.</details> | No change |
| Kenya | Two tracks run in parallel: a [governance act under debate](https://www.amnesty.org/en/latest/news/2026/08/amnesty-internation/) and an [artificial intelligence bill before the Senate](https://alicemunyua.substack.com/p/gatekeepers-xiv) transplanting the European statute. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Senate bill is a self-declared transplant with a presidentially appointed commissioner and fines capped at KES 5m, on a separate legislative track from the ministry's draft policy; the bill text is not held ([Senate bill](https://alicemunyua.substack.com/p/gatekeepers-xiv), [copyright and AI](https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/kenyas-new-copyright-bill-good-start-ready-ai-age-zaccheaus-amenya-0zeuf/)).<br><br>A human-rights organisation argued on 24 August 2026 that the governance act carries no child-specific protections and no consultation mechanism, setting it against the country's membership of a children's protection coalition and against a global drive to age assurance that the analysis argues adds data trails and surveillance risk rather than closing the harms it names; the act's text, sponsor and date remain unestablished ([critique](https://www.amnesty.org/en/latest/news/2026/08/amnesty-internation/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Lesotho | The government names the [AI Policy of 2025 as its governance foundation](https://www.gov.ls/development/country-statement-of-the-kingdom-of-lesotho-at-the-united-nations-global-dialogue-on-artificial-intelligence-governance-delivered-by-the-honourable-deputy-prime-minister-in-palexpo-international-exhib/); no AI Act or regulation exists. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-02-20 — [the ministry’s ICT director reported the national AI policy successfully approved](https://www.gov.ls/ict/infrastructure-development-remains-key-masiu/).<br><br>2026-07-07 — [the Deputy Prime Minister told the UN global dialogue that the policy is the country’s AI governance foundation](https://www.gov.ls/development/country-statement-of-the-kingdom-of-lesotho-at-the-united-nations-global-dialogue-on-artificial-intelligence-governance-delivered-by-the-honourable-deputy-prime-minister-in-palexpo-international-exhib/).<br><br>2026-07-20 — [Lesotho signed the founding agreement of a world AI cooperation organisation in Shanghai](https://www.gov.ls/ict/lesotho-joins-the-founding-members-of-waico/), an international commitment ahead of any domestic law.<br><br>The absence was searched on 31 August 2026. No Bill, no regulations, and no announced timetable for either.</details> | No change, a policy with no statute behind it |
| Liberia | No artificial-intelligence instrument: a court reached [alleged AI-generated audio](https://www.thenewdawnliberia.com/ai-generated-voice-recording-sparks-debate-at-criminal-court-a/) through ordinary disclosure rules. <details><summary>Full record</summary>At Criminal Court A on 19 December 2025 the defence [played an alleged AI-generated recording](https://www.thenewdawnliberia.com/ai-generated-voice-recording-sparks-debate-at-criminal-court-a/) to argue that social-media audio is inadmissible without forensic corroboration; the judge sustained the objection on disclosure grounds, applying no AI-specific rule because none exists in the reported reasoning.<br><br>The executive's own response to synthetic media is an appeal rather than a rule: in July 2026 the presidential press secretary [warned of fabricated and AI-generated documents](https://www.emansion.gov.lr/media/press-release/executive-mansion-rejects-frontpage-africas-claims-purported-confidential) attributed to government and asked citizens to authenticate through official channels.</details> | No change |
| Libya | The [adopted AI strategy](https://www.gia.gov.ly/wp-content/uploads/2026/06/Libya_AI_Strategy_2025_2030.pdf) names the legislative vacuum and sets a four-stage law roadmap to 2030. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [strategy adopted in June 2026](https://www.gia.gov.ly/wp-content/uploads/2026/06/Libya_AI_Strategy_2025_2030.pdf) is unusually direct about its own starting point: it names a legislative vacuum and sets a four-stage roadmap to legislation by 2030. No artificial intelligence statute is in force.<br><br>The instrument that arrived first is soft: the regulator [reopened the draft national artificial intelligence charter for public comment in April 2026, five weeks before adoption](https://lana.gov.ly/post.php?id=356121&lang=ar), and the information authority [convened a meeting in April to move the strategy from drafting to implementation](https://www.gia.gov.ly/en/2026/05/03/the-general-authority-for-information-hosts-an-extensive-meeting-to-launch-the-executive-initiatives-of-the-national-artificial-intelligence-strategy/).</details> | Advanced |
