| Madagascar | No artificial-intelligence law, bill or regulator exists; the [data-protection commission](https://digital.gov.mg/2026/03/27/rapport-annuel-sur-la-confidentialiteet-la-protection-des-donnees-a-madagascar/) is the nearest body with oversight bearing on it. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The only binding provision located is in the 2014 data-protection law, whose article 3 prohibits decisions taken solely on automated profiling ([law](https://digital.gov.mg/en/2022/07/05/loi-n-2014-038-sur-la-protection-des-donnees-a-caractere-personnel/)).<br><br>The commission that enforces it published its first annual report in 2026, confirming its effective establishment ([report](https://digital.gov.mg/2026/03/27/rapport-annuel-sur-la-confidentialiteet-la-protection-des-donnees-a-madagascar/)).<br><br>A courtesy visit to the National Assembly in August 2025 drew a statement of support for laws adapted to technological questions, the closest dated statement of legislative intent on file ([visit](https://assemblee-nationale.mg/article/la-cmil-en-visite-de-courtoisie-au-president-de-lassemblee-nationale/)). No bill has been tabled.</details> | No change |
| Malawi | An [AI Bill](https://times.mw/government-eyes-ai-laws-amid-push-for-innovation/) is targeted to reach parliament before December 2026, and the [copyright body will not register wholly AI-generated works](https://malawi24.com/2026/07/23/malawi-draws-the-line-on-ai-music/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The bill is announced as the statutory backing for the strategy, to establish a framework promoting innovation while safeguarding citizen privacy and ensuring operational accountability; the minister put the target at before December 2026, no draft text is stated, and the strategy it would ground is itself pre-adoption ([bill](https://times.mw/government-eyes-ai-laws-amid-push-for-innovation/), [drafting](https://www.telecompaper.com/news/malawi-drafts-ai-bill--1579445), [scope](https://itweb.africa/article/malawi-drafts-ai-bill-to-govern-public-sector-tech/PmxVE7KEOnpqQY85)).<br><br>The statutory copyright body will not register works created entirely by artificial intelligence or distribute royalties on them; the rule is known only as reported, with no test stated for how much assistance is admissible, and the body's own statement is not held ([rule](https://malawi24.com/2026/07/23/malawi-draws-the-line-on-ai-music/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Mali | [Francophone West African artificial-intelligence governance guidelines](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/tech/six-francophone-west-african-countries-unite-on-ai-governance-framework) were adopted with five other states in July 2026, led by an advocacy organisation. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The guidelines were adopted on 9 July 2026 and announced at a global dialogue on artificial-intelligence governance ([guidelines](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/tech/six-francophone-west-african-countries-unite-on-ai-governance-framework)).<br><br>They are the country's only artificial-intelligence governance instrument in force, they are regional rather than national, and they were led by an advocacy organisation rather than by a state. No domestic statute, decree or regulator on the subject is held.</details> | Advanced |
| Mauritius | The [higher education AI regulations of April 2026](https://supremecourt.govmu.org/downloadPDF/legislation/18106230) are the country's only binding artificial-intelligence instrument. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Government Notice 39 of 2026 makes regulations on the use of artificial intelligence in higher education ([regulations](https://supremecourt.govmu.org/downloadPDF/legislation/18106230)).<br><br>The national strategy and its fairness, accountability, inclusiveness and responsibility guidelines are non-binding ([strategy](https://mitci.govmu.org/mitci/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/aistrategy.pdf), [guidelines](https://mitci.govmu.org/mitci/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/FINAL-GUIDELINES-15AVRIL.pdf)).<br><br>The higher education regulator issued amended guidelines of its own in May 2026 ([guidelines](https://hec.mu/pdf_downloads/recent_posts/AI%20guidelines_amended13052026.pdf)). What the base does not hold is any general artificial-intelligence statute, bill or regulator: the one binding rule reaches universities only.</details> | Advanced |
| Morocco | The [electoral organic law](https://www.hespress.com/%d8%a8%d9%86-%d8%ad%d9%85%d8%b2%d8%a9-%d8%aa%d8%ac%d8%b1%d9%8a%d9%85-%d8%a7%d9%84%d8%aa%d8%b6%d9%84%d9%8a%d9%84-%d8%a8%d8%a7%d9%84%d8%b0%d9%83%d8%a7%d8%a1-%d8%a7%d9%84%d8%a7%d8%b5%d8%b7%d9%86%d8%a7-1784231.html) carries the only enacted artificial-intelligence offences, while a [private members bill](https://www.hespress.com/%D9%85%D8%A8%D8%A7%D8%AF%D8%B1%D8%A9-%D8%AA%D8%B4%D8%B1%D9%8A%D8%B9%D9%8A%D8%A9-%D8%AA%D9%87%D8%AF%D9%81-%D8%A5%D9%84%D9%89-%D8%AA%D9%86%D8%B8%D9%8A%D9%85-%D8%A7%D8%B3%D8%AA%D8%B9%D9%85%D8%A7%D9%84-1494587.html) on regulating the technology has not moved. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Organic law 53.25 enumerates artificial-intelligence tools as a means of commission on four electoral offences and creates a new article punishing the fabrication of election disinformation with two to five years and a fine of MAD 50,000 to 100,000, upheld against a press-freedom challenge ([offences](https://www.hespress.com/%d8%a8%d9%86-%d8%ad%d9%85%d8%b2%d8%a9-%d8%aa%d8%ac%d8%b1%d9%8a%d9%85-%d8%a7%d9%84%d8%aa%d8%b6%d9%84%d9%8a%d9%84-%d8%a8%d8%a7%d9%84%d8%b0%d9%83%d8%a7%d8%a1-%d8%a7%d9%84%d8%a7%d8%b5%d8%b7%d9%86%d8%a7-1784231.html)). The constitutional court found nothing unconstitutional in December 2025 ([decision](https://www.cour-constitutionnelle.ma/Decision?Page=Decision&id=2123), [tabling](https://www.chambredesrepresentants.ma/sites/default/files/loi/Projet_loi_53.25_0.pdf)).<br><br>A seventeen-article private member's bill setting fines of MAD 50,000 to 500,000 has been before the chamber's bureau since January 2025 with no committee report ([bill](https://www.hespress.com/%D9%85%D8%A8%D8%A7%D8%AF%D8%B1%D8%A9-%D8%AA%D8%B4%D8%B1%D9%8A%D8%B9%D9%8A%D8%A9-%D8%AA%D9%87%D8%AF%D9%81-%D8%A5%D9%84%D9%89-%D8%AA%D9%86%D8%B8%D9%8A%D9%85-%D8%A7%D8%B3%D8%AA%D8%B9%D9%85%D8%A7%D9%84-1494587.html), [roundup](https://digitalpolicyalert.org/blog/data-protection-in-africa-roundup)).</details> | Advanced |
| Mozambique | There is [no dedicated artificial-intelligence legislation](https://360mozambique.com/innovation/tech/intic-drafts-copyright-and-ai-legislation-to-protect-digital-creators/); an [online copyright act](https://360mozambique.com/innovation/tech/intic-drafts-copyright-and-ai-legislation-to-protect-digital-creators/) is in drafting and an [intellectual property strategy](https://360mozambique.com/development/mozambique-approves-ip-strategy-to-study-ai-patentability-and-digital-rights/) mandates a patentability study. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The ICT institute's board chairman stated in August 2026 that the country still lacks dedicated artificial-intelligence legislation ([absence](https://360mozambique.com/innovation/tech/intic-drafts-copyright-and-ai-legislation-to-protect-digital-creators/)).<br><br>An Online Copyright Protection Act is in drafting for introduction next year, its chairman naming authorship of artificial-intelligence output and liability for artificial-intelligence training as open questions the country has no law on; no draft text or introduction date is held ([drafting](https://360mozambique.com/innovation/tech/intic-drafts-copyright-and-ai-legislation-to-protect-digital-creators/)).<br><br>An intellectual property policy and strategy to 2035, approved by resolution in June 2026, mandates a study of the patentability of inventions made by artificial intelligence and of the inventive-step and disclosure standards that should apply ([strategy](https://360mozambique.com/development/mozambique-approves-ip-strategy-to-study-ai-patentability-and-digital-rights/), [account](https://patentlawyermagazine.com/mozambique-to-examine-ai-patentability-under-new-national-intellectual-property-strategy/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Namibia | [Artificial-intelligence legislation](https://itweb.africa/article/namibia-taps-deloitte-for-ai/KzQenMjyxWY7Zd2r) is announced as following the strategy, and the research council's own authors recorded no governance framework in place. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The minister sequenced legislation behind the strategy, and nothing has been drafted or tabled ([sequencing](https://itweb.africa/article/namibia-taps-deloitte-for-ai/KzQenMjyxWY7Zd2r), [framework](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/05/namibia-and-deloitte-discuss-ai-strategy-and-digital-infrastructure-development/)).<br><br>The research council's own authors recorded no artificial-intelligence governance framework in place as at February 2026. A strategy that is itself unpublished cannot yet ground a statute, so both halves of the governance question are pending on the same document.</details> | No change, announced as following the strategy with nothing drafted or tabled |
| Niger | The [data protection authority was abolished in June 2026](https://www.africadataprotection.org/actualites/Niger-Suppression-de-la-Haute-Autorite-de-Protection-des-Donnees-a-caractere-Personnel-et-transfert-de-ses-competences.html), removing the body that held the declaration duty for artificial-intelligence operators. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The government [abolished the data protection authority by decree](https://www.africadataprotection.org/actualites/Niger-Suppression-de-la-Haute-Autorite-de-Protection-des-Donnees-a-caractere-Personnel-et-transfert-de-ses-competences.html) and transferred its prerogatives to the ministries of justice and of the interior as part of a wider administrative reorganisation; no primary decree text was located.<br><br>The provisions themselves stand. The [consolidated text of the 2022 data protection law](https://www.hapdp.ne/uploads/files/Loi-2022-59-consolidee.pdf) defines artificial intelligence at article 57, requires developers, manufacturers and service providers operating from Niger to declare to the authority, and restricts fully automated decisions and profiling at article 52.<br><br>What has gone is the body those duties were owed to. No transitional arrangement naming which ministry now receives a declaration is on file, and the law names neither deepfakes nor algorithmic disinformation.</details> | Regressed |
| Nigeria | A [risk-based regime](https://itweb.africa/article/nigeria-plans-to-introduce-ai-regulations/DZQ587V8lQDqzXy2) sits in an unpassed bill, competition law was [turned on AI training over Nigerian journalism](https://fccpc.gov.ng/president-tinubu-directs-fccpc-to-investigate-big-techs/), and [judicial officers](https://www.vanguardngr.com/2026/08/digicivic-trains-judicial-officers-on-digital-rights/) called for authentication standards. <details><summary>Full record</summary>High-risk systems in finance, public administration, surveillance and automated decision-making would face annual impact assessments with fines up to ₦10m or 2 per cent of Nigerian revenue ([bill](https://itweb.africa/article/nigeria-plans-to-introduce-ai-regulations/DZQ587V8lQDqzXy2)); an external reading finds the bill envisages risk assessment without categorising risk into levels, and a domestic critique warns it concentrates broad powers in agencies that lack proven capacity ([bill](https://itweb.africa/article/nigeria-plans-to-introduce-ai-regulations/DZQ587V8lQDqzXy2), [control](https://techcabal.com/2025/11/20/nigerias-ai-bill-puts-control-first-but-at-what-cost-to-innovation/), [governance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.17191)).<br><br>A presidential directive of July 2026 covers alleged anti-competitive practices, unlawful exploitation of news content and other unfair conduct by major global technology and generative artificial-intelligence firms; it followed a press-organisation petition and no terms of reference or interim finding is published ([directive](https://fccpc.gov.ng/president-tinubu-directs-fccpc-to-investigate-big-techs/), [scope](https://www.mondaq.com/nigeria/antitrust-eu-competition/1833602/president-tinubu-directs-fccpc-to-investigate-global-digital-platforms-over-competition-and-copyright-concerns)).<br><br>Fifty-four judicial officers called for stricter authentication standards for AI-generated evidence and a review of the digital-evidence, cybercrime and privacy statutes; a communique carries no legal force and no legislative response is on record ([communique](https://www.vanguardngr.com/2026/08/digicivic-trains-judicial-officers-on-digital-rights/)). A university-system artificial-intelligence framework has been written and three tertiary regulators committed to harmonise ([tertiary](https://www.vanguardngr.com/2026/08/naaip-symposium-urges-unified-ai-guardrails-for-nigerian-tertiary-institutions/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Rwanda | No [review framework for commercial artificial-intelligence partnerships](https://www.techpolicy.press/anthropic-is-becoming-the-backbone-of-rwandas-government-but-who-is-accountable/) is held, and a three-year vendor memorandum was signed in February 2026 without one. <details><summary>Full record</summary>External commentary asks who is accountable when a frontier vendor becomes infrastructure for government health and education work, and records that the memorandum was signed with no review framework in place; the criticism is attributed, not adopted ([commentary](https://www.techpolicy.press/anthropic-is-becoming-the-backbone-of-rwandas-government-but-who-is-accountable/), [memorandum](https://www.anthropic.com/news/anthropic-rwanda-mou)).<br><br>The country holds an artificial-intelligence policy and is standing up an agency, so the gap is specifically in the instrument that would govern a commercial partnership rather than in artificial-intelligence policy generally.</details> | No change |
| Sao Tome and Principe | There are [no AI-specific laws or regulations](https://www.unesco.org/ethics-ai/en/saotomeeprincipe) and no national AI ethics commission; the data protection law and its authority are the only governance anchors. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The readiness assessment finds no specific legal provision on artificial intelligence and names the 2016 personal data protection law and the 2017 cybercrime law as the nearest anchors, the cybercrime law needing updating to cover artificial intelligence; there is consequently no national AI ethics commission or dedicated governance structure ([assessment](https://unesdoc.unesco.org/ark:/48223/pf0000394953)).<br><br>The same assessment records that the data protection law makes it an administrative offence, punishable by fine, for a body notified by the authority to keep open network access available to controllers not complying with the law ([assessment](https://unesdoc.unesco.org/ark:/48223/pf0000394953)); the innovation institute can issue strategies and guidelines but these do not carry the force of law ([profile](https://www.unesco.org/ethics-ai/en/saotomeeprincipe)).<br><br>An AI justice platform is meanwhile being built with a chatbot trained on national legislation ([platform](https://www.unodc.org/westandcentralafrica/en/westandcentralafrica/stories/2026/when-walking-into-a-police-station-is-the-barrier--build-a-door-that-opens-from-the-other-side.html)): the first public-facing AI system arrives ahead of any rule governing one.</details> | No change |
| Senegal | A four-country comparative study found [no artificial-intelligence-specific legislation in force or in draft](https://grain-africa.org/en/intelligence-artificielle-en-afrique-de-louest-grain-presente-les-resultats-dune-etude-comparative-pour-une-gouvernance-plus-inclusive-et-responsable-de-lia/), and the strategy stack and a planned ethics committee carry the field instead. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The study searched in August 2026 and found no artificial-intelligence-specific instrument on record; it rates a neighbour's strategy the most advanced of the four on inclusion and data governance ([study](https://grain-africa.org/en/intelligence-artificielle-en-afrique-de-louest-grain-presente-les-resultats-dune-etude-comparative-pour-une-gouvernance-plus-inclusive-et-responsable-de-lia/)).<br><br>What the country holds instead is a 2023 strategy, an instruction for a development plan, a planned ethics committee and a non-binding regional guideline set — four policy artefacts and no binding rule.</details> | No change |
| Seychelles | The [Broadcasting Corporation Act 2026](https://www.gazette.sc/sites/default/files/2026-07/Act%2012%202026%20-%20Seychelles%20Broadcasting%20Corporation%20Act%202026.pdf) requires an AI-use policy for content and broadcast operations — the only statutory AI duty held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Act, assented on 9 July and gazetted on 13 July 2026, repeals the 2011 statute and requires the Corporation to develop and periodically review a policy on the use of artificial intelligence in content creation, curation and broadcast operations ([Act](https://www.gazette.sc/sites/default/files/2026-07/Act%2012%202026%20-%20Seychelles%20Broadcasting%20Corporation%20Act%202026.pdf)).<br><br>It is sector-specific and applies to one state broadcaster. No general artificial-intelligence statute, regulation or code applies across the economy, and the policy the Act requires is not itself held.</details> | Advanced |
| Somalia | No artificial-intelligence instrument is in force or in draft. A [readiness assessment](https://www.unesco.org/en/articles/call-expression-interest-lead-national-expert-somalia-somalia-unesco-regional-office-eastern-africa) was still recruiting its lead expert in October 2025. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Recruitment opened in October 2025 for a national expert to lead a readiness assessment and produce a policy-oriented report to inform a national governance framework, working under a government-side steering committee ([call](https://www.unesco.org/en/articles/call-expression-interest-lead-national-expert-somalia-somalia-unesco-regional-office-eastern-africa)). Somalia was listed at inception stage.<br><br>The national artificial intelligence centre names advising on ethical policy formulation among its objectives ([centre](https://snaic.gov.so/about/)), which places the drafting capacity somewhere but produces no instrument.<br><br>The Data Protection Act is the only statute reaching automated processing at all, and it is not AI-specific ([Act](https://moct.gov.so/en/wp-content/uploads/2023/07/Somalia-Data-Protection-Act-2.pdf)).</details> | No change |
| South Africa | [No rules govern state automated decision-making](https://www.itweb.co.za/article/ai-hallucinations-strike-again-at-sa-courts/Pero3qZ31oGvQb6m). What moved instead was [litigation over an algorithmic grant eligibility screen](https://www.globalcenter.ai/news/human-rights-implications-of-using-algorithms-for-social-grants) and a copyright Bill silent on AI. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The position is unchanged and stated as such: the base holds [no instrument governing automated decision-making by the state](https://www.itweb.co.za/article/ai-hallucinations-strike-again-at-sa-courts/Pero3qZ31oGvQb6m).<br><br>What the window produced is a court case rather than a rule. The state is [appealing a January 2025 judgment that struck down regulations governing the Social Relief of Distress grant](https://www.globalcenter.ai/news/human-rights-implications-of-using-algorithms-for-social-grants), a scheme administered online only and screened by an algorithmic verification system that excluded applicants it read as earning above R624.00; the court found the automation unfairly excluded otherwise eligible applicants, an AI governance organisation was admitted as a friend of the court, and the appeal was set down for 25 August 2026.<br><br>The one Bill in the window that touches machine learning does so silently. The [Copyright Amendment Bill would replace fair dealing with a broader fair use exception](https://www.itweb.co.za/article/paperback-writer-meet-the-shredder/kYbe97XbZ35qAWpG) and returned to Parliament in June 2026 after the Constitutional Court upheld most of it; it says nothing explicit about generative AI or text and data mining, which imports another country's judicial answer to those questions along with the doctrine.</details> | No change |
| South Sudan | No artificial intelligence statute: the [Cybercrimes and Computer Misuse Act 2026](https://www.scribd.com/document/1032350790/Cybercrimes-and-Computer-Misuse-Act-2026) is the closest instrument, enacted citing AI-enabled abuse. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The legislature passed the bill on 25 November 2025, with the ICT minister giving artificial intelligence being used to discredit and abuse people as a reason for it ([passage](https://mictps.gov.ss/revitalized-natl-legislative-assembly-passed-cybersecurity-and-computer-misuse-bill/)), and the President signed it into law on 18 February 2026 ([Act](https://www.scribd.com/document/1032350790/Cybercrimes-and-Computer-Misuse-Act-2026)).<br><br>The Act is technology-neutral - hacking, identity theft, cyberstalking, cyberbullying, false or misleading information - with no artificial-intelligence or deepfake provision of its own ([explainer](https://www.radiotamazuj.org/en/news/article/explainer-cybercrime-and-computer-misuse-act-2026-chapter-i)).<br><br>A general cybercrime law invoked against a specific harm is not regulation of that harm.</details> | Advanced |
| Sudan | No artificial-intelligence statute is in force. The [cybercrime amendment of 2020](https://redress.org/storage/2020/07/2-Sudan-Amendments-July-2020-English-REDRESS-translation.pdf) is the instrument reached for, and it is not AI-specific. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Cyber Crime Prevention (Amendment) Act, 2020 was signed on 22 April 2020 and gazetted on 13 July, sharply increasing penalties across articles 5 to 39 of the 2018 Cyber Crime Law ([gazette](https://redress.org/storage/2020/07/2-Sudan-Amendments-July-2020-English-REDRESS-translation.pdf)).<br><br>It carries no deepfake or generative-AI provision, and no dedicated instrument was found. A draft data and artificial intelligence law was reported in redrafting in August 2026.<br><br>When a manipulated image of the prime minister circulated in June 2026, the information ministry reserved the right to act without naming a provision ([statement](https://sudanhorizon.com/ministry-of-information-legal-action-against-individuals-behind-disinformation-campaign/)).</details> | No change |
| Zambia | No binding instrument; a [readiness assessment methodology was piloted ahead of the national strategy's review before its end-2026 expiry](https://zambia.un.org/en/318469-unesco-and-zambia-strengthen-partnership-advance-youth-development-and-ethical-ai). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-06-22 to 23 — [a Lusaka consultation piloted an international readiness assessment methodology, ahead of the National AI Strategy's review before its end-2026 expiry](https://zambia.un.org/en/318469-unesco-and-zambia-strengthen-partnership-advance-youth-development-and-ethical-ai).<br><br>That is the whole of the movement, and the expiry is the part worth recording: the country's only artificial-intelligence instrument is a strategy that runs out at the end of this year, with no statute, statutory instrument or regulator rule behind it and no successor drafted. A readiness assessment is a diagnostic, not a rule.</details> | No change |
| Zimbabwe | A [national artificial-intelligence act](https://technomag.co.zw/new-ai-strategy-mandates-local-storage-for-national-data/) is proposed by the strategy with no draft on record, and an [ethics framework](https://spikedmedia.co.zw/zimbabwe-bets-on-digital-gold-inside-the-launch-of-ndarama-and-the-push-for-ai-sovereignty/) is committed for tabling by the first quarter of 2027. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The act is a strategy proposal with no draft on record ([act](https://technomag.co.zw/new-ai-strategy-mandates-local-storage-for-national-data/), [privacy](https://www.techhiveadvisory.africa/insights/bimonthly-update-on-privacy-in-africa-january-february-2026)).<br><br>The minister's own caveat on the ethics framework was that policy without implementation is mere poetry ([framework](https://spikedmedia.co.zw/zimbabwe-bets-on-digital-gold-inside-the-launch-of-ndarama-and-the-push-for-ai-sovereignty/)).<br><br>A regulatory sandbox is named in the strategy and a supervising council was announced in August 2026 ([sandbox](https://technomag.co.zw/zimbabwe-bets-on-ai-innovation-as-government-launches-national-grand-challenge/)), and technical standards are likewise a strategy proposal ([standards](https://www.techhiveadvisory.africa/insights/bimonthly-update-on-privacy-in-africa-january-february-2026)). Four artificial-intelligence instruments, all proposed by one strategy that has no legal force.</details> | Advanced |

## E-commerce legislation

| Country | Developments | Progress |
|---|---|---|
| Algeria | [Law 18-05 is in force while most online selling runs through social-media pages outside its obligations](https://www.echoroukonline.com/%d8%a7%d8%b2%d8%af%d9%87%d8%a7%d8%b1-%d8%a7%d9%84%d8%aa%d8%ac%d8%a7%d8%b1%d8%a9-%d8%a7%d9%84%d8%a5%d9%84%d9%83%d8%aa%d8%b1%d9%88%d9%86%d9%8a%d8%a9-%d9%81%d9%8a-%d8%a7%d9%84%d8%ac%d8%b2%d8%a7%d8%a6), cash on delivery dominant. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The law is [in force, and the great majority of online selling runs through social-media pages outside its obligations, with cash on delivery dominant](https://www.echoroukonline.com/%d8%a7%d8%b2%d8%af%d9%87%d8%a7%d8%b1-%d8%a7%d9%84%d8%aa%d8%ac%d8%a7%d8%b1%d8%a9-%d8%a7%d9%84%d8%a5%d9%84%d9%83%d8%aa%d8%b1%d9%88%d9%86%d9%8a%d8%a9-%d9%81%d9%8a-%d8%a7%d9%84%d8%ac%d8%b2%d8%a7%d8%a6).<br><br>The assessment is named-analyst opinion from a consumer organisation rather than a state position; the law's own date and text are not held, and no enforcement action or compliance figure is on file. A statute whose scope most of the activity it governs sits outside, with no published enforcement, is the position - and the payment side matches it, with fewer than a tenth of merchants operating a card terminal.</details> | No change |
| Benin | [Loi 2017-20, the Code du numerique, has been in force since 20 April 2018](https://sgg.gouv.bj/doc/loi-2017-20/download) as the sector's framework statute. <details><summary>Full record</summary>[The Code du numerique was adopted by the National Assembly on 13 June 2017, brought into conformity with the Constitution after two Constitutional Court decisions, and promulgated on 20 April 2018; it has since been amended by Loi 2020-35](https://sgg.gouv.bj/doc/loi-2017-20/download). [The earlier Code de l'information et de la communication, Loi 2015-07 of 20 March 2015, remains alongside it](https://sgg.gouv.bj/doc/loi-2015-07/download).<br><br>The code is the authority every subsequent instrument on this ledger cites: the state information systems security policy, the non-terrestrial networks regime and the interoperability framework are all made under it. What the base does not hold is any account of its enforcement.</details> | No change, a standing statute the base holds for the first time |
| Botswana | The [Electronic Communications and Transactions Act 2014 is the principal e-commerce statute](https://www.bocra.org.bw/sites/default/files/documents/Electronic-Communications-and-Transactions-Act-2014.pdf); the movement this year was in taxing digital supply. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2014-05-14 — [the Act gives legal recognition to electronic communications and transactions](https://www.bocra.org.bw/sites/default/files/documents/Electronic-Communications-and-Transactions-Act-2014.pdf) and is unamended on the record.<br><br>2026-05-29 — [regulations set the registration and compliance machinery for foreign suppliers of remote services](https://www.burs.org.bw/images/VAT%20Added%20Tax%20Regulations%202026.pdf).<br><br>2026-07-01 — [the Value Added Tax Act 2026 was gazetted, taxing remote services and electronic marketplaces supplied into Botswana](https://www.burs.org.bw/index.php/tax/tax-laws-2026?download=958%3Avalue-added-tax-act-2026).<br><br>The capture of the 2014 Act is a truncated scan and does not carry its commencement date.</details> | Advanced, on the tax side |
| Burkina Faso | [Loi 045-2009, adopted 10 November 2009, is the principal electronic-transactions statute](https://www.ictpolicyafrica.org/api/documents/download?_id=5eb968597c7814001bc4bcbe). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[Loi 045-2009 was adopted on 10 November 2009 and promulgated on 4 December by Decret 2009-824, covering information-society services, electronic signatures, electronic commerce and distance-contract consumer protection](https://www.ictpolicyafrica.org/api/documents/download?_id=5eb968597c7814001bc4bcbe).<br><br>No implementing decree, enforcement record or case under it is held.</details> | No change, a standing statute the base holds for the first time |
| Burundi | There is no free-standing e-commerce statute; coverage sits in the [2024 communications code](https://arct.gov.bi/wp-content/uploads/2024/09/LOI_COMMUNICATIONS-ELECTRONIQUES.pdf), under which the regulator [made a conformity declaration compulsory in June 2026](https://arct.gov.bi/2026/06/26/appel-a-la-declaratio-n-de-conformite-des-plateformes-et-sites-marchands-de-commerce-electronique-au-burundi/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[There is no Burundian law on electronic commerce, electronic transactions or electronic signature; coverage is carried inside the 2024 code des communications electroniques et postales, whose article 1 applies to all transactions by electronic means](https://arct.gov.bi/wp-content/uploads/2024/09/LOI_COMMUNICATIONS-ELECTRONIQUES.pdf), and the regulator treats that code as the legal basis for electronic commerce.<br><br>2026-05-29 — [the regulator published guidelines for the national electronic-commerce sector setting technical, operational and organisational standards for interoperability, transparency, transaction security and online consumer protection](https://arct.gov.bi/2026/05/29/lignes-directrices-de-lecosysteme-national-de-commerce-electronique-du-burundi/).<br><br>2026-06-26 — [it then made a prior conformity declaration compulsory for platforms and merchant sites operating in Burundi](https://arct.gov.bi/2026/06/26/appel-a-la-declaratio-n-de-conformite-des-plateformes-et-sites-marchands-de-commerce-electronique-au-burundi/), which is the operative regulatory step. The guidelines themselves are an image-only scan, so what they require cannot be read from the base.</details> | Advanced |
| Cameroon | [Loi 2010/021 on electronic commerce has been in force since December 2010](https://www.mincommerce.gov.cm/sites/default/files/documents/loi-n-2010-021-du-21-decembre-2010-regissant-le-commerce-electronique-au-cameroun.pdf), with its [implementing decree from 2011](https://www.mincommerce.gov.cm/sites/default/files/documents/decret-n-2011-1521-pm-du-15-juin-2011-fixant-les-modalites-d-application-de-la-loi-regissant-le-commerce-electronique-au-cameroun.pdf). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[Loi 2010/021 on electronic commerce was enacted on 21 December 2010](https://www.mincommerce.gov.cm/sites/default/files/documents/loi-n-2010-021-du-21-decembre-2010-regissant-le-commerce-electronique-au-cameroun.pdf), [Decret 2011-1521 fixed its modalities in June 2011](https://www.mincommerce.gov.cm/sites/default/files/documents/decret-n-2011-1521-pm-du-15-juin-2011-fixant-les-modalites-d-application-de-la-loi-regissant-le-commerce-electronique-au-cameroun.pdf), and [Decret 2012-1318 constituted the electronic certification authority under it](https://camgovca.cm/images/downloads/articles/decret-autorite-certification.pdf).<br><br>The statutory chain is complete and nothing on the file reports enforcement, a certified provider count or a transaction volume under it.</details> | No change, a standing statute the base holds for the first time |
| Cape Verde | Legal frameworks for digital services, e-commerce and digital banking are [named among the 5G strategy's declared reach](https://techafricanews.com/2026/02/16/cape-verde-launches-national-5g-strategy-to-accelerate-digital-nation-vision/); no bill is held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The frameworks were [declared as a component of the 5G strategy presented in September 2025](https://www.governo.cv/cabo-verde-apresenta-estrategia-nacional-para-o-5g-e-ambiciona-ser-hub-digital-regional-ate-2030/) and are [named among the declared reach of the strategy approved on 20 February 2026, alongside a regulatory and business framework to support investment](https://techafricanews.com/2026/02/16/cape-verde-launches-national-5g-strategy-to-accelerate-digital-nation-vision/). No bill, draft or diploma is held: it is a stated legislative programme covering three fields, not a named instrument.<br><br>Against that, a [dedicated startup law with tax benefits, a special technology economic zone, modern data-protection and digital-transactions laws, simplified business registration and a digital-nomad visa are all stated to be in place](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/public-management/cape-verde-ramps-up-digital-start-up-ecosystem-to-boost-economy), with no instrument number, adoption date, text or take-up figure for the startup law or the visa. That position rests entirely on a minister's summary in trade press.</details> | Advanced |
| Central African Republic | [Loi 22.002 of January 2022](https://www.arcep.cf/fr/index.php/reglementation/lois-et-ordonnances) is the operative electronic transactions law and the only instrument on the subject the base holds. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The law adopted on 11 January 2022 governs electronic transactions and is the single instrument on the subject the base carries; [the regulator's own law index carries the citation](https://www.arcep.cf/fr/index.php/reglementation/lois-et-ordonnances).<br><br>The law's own held record has no resolvable address - the regulator's hosted file did not answer from two clients - so what a reader can follow is the index entry rather than the text. No implementing decree, no dispute under it and no electronic-commerce measure is held, and nothing in the window touched it.</details> | No change, a first stated position with no earlier account behind it |
| Chad | [Loi 008/PR/2015 on electronic transactions](https://arcep.td/lois.html) is the statute; the [digital code that would replace it](https://www.toumaiwebmedias.com/tic-le-code-numerique-de-la-republique-du-tchad-autour-des-debats-avec-les-experts/) was still a draft in 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The regulator's register names Loi n. 008/PR/2015 on electronic transactions among the operative statutes ([register](https://arcep.td/lois.html)).<br><br>A second workshop on the digital code drafting mission opened on 17 February 2026 discussing the avant-projet written after a December 2025 workshop, the drafting funded through the state fibre project ([workshop](https://www.toumaiwebmedias.com/tic-le-code-numerique-de-la-republique-du-tchad-autour-des-debats-avec-les-experts/)).<br><br>The electronic-communications framework moved separately: a decret of 16 January 2026 promulgated the law ratifying an August 2025 ordinance amending it ([promulgation](https://www.alwihdainfo.com/Tchad-Le-chef-de-l-Etat-promulgue-10-lois_a147575.html)), and the March 2026 agency reform brought electronic transactions expressly into its remit ([reform](https://www.alwihdainfo.com/Tchad-promulgation-de-la-reforme-de-l-ANSICE_a148767.html)).</details> | Advanced |
| Comoros | The [e-commerce law is still to be drafted](https://anaden.org/actualites/anaden-information-les-avancees-sur-le-code-numerique), and no [electronic-signature instrument exists](https://anaden.org/uploads/media/5e3969272d9f8/strat-comores-numerique-v2-3-compresse.pdf). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The e-commerce law was [recorded as still to be drafted in March 2021 and is unchanged](https://anaden.org/actualites/anaden-information-les-avancees-sur-le-code-numerique), as is a [revised public procurement code for procurement conducted electronically, drafted and not enacted, with no procurement platform on record](https://anaden.org/actualites/anaden-information-les-avancees-sur-le-code-numerique).<br><br>An electronic-signature instrument was [named as a legal vacuum by the state's own 2018 strategy](https://anaden.org/uploads/media/5e3969272d9f8/strat-comores-numerique-v2-3-compresse.pdf) and none is on file at 2026-08-04 - the last of six gaps the state itself named in 2018 that the base cannot show closed. Without a signature instrument, none of the paperless procedures being procured elsewhere in this report has a legal basis for the act it would replace.</details> | No change |
| Congo | Certified electronic invoicing became [compulsory for every economic agent from 1 July 2026](https://sgg.cg/JO/2026/congo-jo-2026-28.pdf), with four implementing orders gazetted. <details><summary>Full record</summary>A voluntary pilot group ran at the window's opening. A decree [signed 31 March 2026 and gazetted 9 July made invoicing mandatory from 1 July for every economic agent except the state, local authorities on public-service missions and diplomatic bodies, with terminals, software and enterprise modules homologated jointly by the tax directorate and the security agency](https://sgg.cg/JO/2026/congo-jo-2026-28.pdf), after a [sensitisation campaign in June](https://www.finances.gouv.cg/fr/articles/lancement-officiel-de-la-campagne-nationale-de-sensibilisation-au-syst%C3%A8me-de-facturation).<br><br>Four orders gazetted 16 July set a [four-stage homologation with a 30-day decision deadline, mandatory cryptographic certification, a penetration test under twelve months old, a sworn no-backdoor attestation, ten-year invoice retention and real-time tax-directorate data access, at FCFA 5,000,000 per terminal model and per software version](https://sgg.cg/JO/2026/congo-jo-2026-29-4.pdf). No phase-in by firm size and no assisted or offline route is stated.</details> | Advanced |
| Cote d'Ivoire | [Loi n 2013-546 on electronic transactions remains in force with article 50 repealed](https://fr.apanews.net/technologies/rci-lanssi-lance-la-pki-pour-une-souverainete-numerique/), and its implementing decrees still stand. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The law stands [in force with article 50 repealed by ordinance and ratified in 2025, and its 2014 and 2016 implementing decrees on cryptology, electronic signature and digital archiving are the stated legal basis of the national public key infrastructure](https://fr.apanews.net/technologies/rci-lanssi-lance-la-pki-pour-une-souverainete-numerique/).<br><br>Nothing in the window amended it. It is the only Ivorian digital statute the base can name implementing decrees for, which is why it carries the weight it does here: electronic signature across the administration, certificate issuance and digital archiving all rest on a 2013 text and its two decrees.</details> | No change |
| Djibouti | The [Digital Code, eight volumes and about 800 articles adopted on 30 June 2025, is in force with no implementing decree held](https://techafricanews.com/2025/07/02/a-first-for-east-africa-djibouti-enacts-comprehensive-digital-code/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The code was [adopted by the National Assembly on 30 June 2025](https://www.jonesday.com/en/insights/2025/07/national-assembly-of-the-republic-of-djibouti-approves-digital-code), three weeks before the window opened, and is [in force across eight volumes and about 800 articles](https://techafricanews.com/2025/07/02/a-first-for-east-africa-djibouti-enacts-comprehensive-digital-code/), [explained at length in the state daily](https://www.lanation.dj/le-code-du-numerique-un-pas-decisif-pour-la-transformation-digitale/).<br><br>No implementing decree is held for any of its volumes. It is the instrument on which the data-protection regime, the prior-authorisation regime and the supervisory commission all rest, and none of the three is evidenced in operation - so a code of eight hundred articles has produced no secondary instrument the base can show in the thirteen months since.</details> | No change |
| Egypt | A [54-article party draft on the digital space would oblige platforms to appoint a legal representative and register influencers](https://www.alnaharegypt.com/1191209); it is not tabled. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Nothing of the kind was on record at the window's opening. The draft was [described by its sponsor on 10 August 2026 as fifty-four articles across seven chapters, requiring digital platforms to appoint a legal representative in the country and register influencers on a national platform, and regulating artificial intelligence, deepfakes, online extortion, gambling and child protection online](https://www.alnaharegypt.com/1191209).<br><br>It is not tabled and no text is published; the article count and the preparation time are the sponsoring member's own account, and it is separate from the cabinet's own social-media legislative review. So the most comprehensive platform instrument on this ledger exists only as a member's description of it.</details> | Advanced |
| Equatorial Guinea | The [2015-2017 laws](https://palaciodelpueblo.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/02/LEYES-ELECTRONICAS.pdf) supply electronic signature and access rules; the state is instead building [platforms](https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/noticias/el_gobierno_impulsa_un_plan_para_profesionalizar_y_monetizar_la_actividad_de_los_influencers). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The legal base for electronic dealing is the [signature and electronic access laws](https://palaciodelpueblo.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/02/LEYES-ELECTRONICAS.pdf) inside the 2015-2017 package. No electronic commerce act has followed.<br><br>Activity has run ahead of the statute: national marketplace and e-commerce platforms sit on the ledger, and in [April 2026 a foreign platform pitched an e-commerce hub while the Vice-President formed a task force on paying digital creators](https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/noticias/el_gobierno_impulsa_un_plan_para_profesionalizar_y_monetizar_la_actividad_de_los_influencers). Neither has a consumer-protection rule behind it.</details> | Advanced |
| Eritrea | No electronic transactions framework is in force; the only relevant texts are unofficial consultation drafts ([drafts](https://theabcjourney.org/legislature/draftlaws)). <details><summary>Full record</summary>A diaspora legal-reform initiative published around eighty consultation-draft acts, codes and regulations intended to reconstruct Eritrea's legal order, including a proposed commercial code and a payment systems and digital finance act addressing electronic transactions ([drafts](https://theabcjourney.org/legislature/draftlaws)). These are unofficial and carry no legislative status.<br><br>The standing assessment is that Eritrea lacks a comprehensive legal framework in the digital-trade area, with the only body holding sector-supervision power having little to no decision-making authority ([profile](https://dtri.uneca.org/v1/uploads/country-profile/eri-country-profile-en.pdf)).<br><br>Nothing on the base records a government bill, a consultation or a stated intention to legislate on electronic transactions.</details> | No change |
| Eswatini | [Online Media Publishing Guidelines 2026](https://times.co.sz/40740/news/new-regulations-to-crack-whip-on-online-media/) are stated by the ministry as complete in development, and [restrictions on children reaching harmful content](https://times.co.sz/41773/news/eswatini-eyes-curbing-childrens-social-media-access/) were raised in the Senate with no instrument behind them. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The ministry stated development of the guidelines is complete, and used the same account to state that implementation, enforcement and oversight of data protection are vested in the regulator rather than itself ([guidelines](https://times.co.sz/40740/news/new-regulations-to-crack-whip-on-online-media/)).<br><br>A senator asked during a Senate debate what prevents minors reaching harmful content; the answer was that two offices are working together on online-content regulation. No instrument, timetable, age-verification method or definition of harmful content is stated, and no draft is held ([question](https://times.co.sz/41773/news/eswatini-eyes-curbing-childrens-social-media-access/)).<br><br>Guidelines complete but unpublished, and content regulation that exists only as an answer in debate, are the two forms this unit's regulation of platforms takes.</details> | Advanced |
| Ethiopia | A draft amendment would add [service provider duties, site blocking and take-down notices](https://www.thereporterethiopia.com/52113/) to the 2004 copyright proclamation, the first platform-liability instrument held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The intellectual property authority reported in August 2026 that the amendment is being drafted after several consultation rounds, and would put duties on internet service providers, set procedures for blocking infringing sites and create a rights-holder take-down notice ([the authority's account](https://www.thereporterethiopia.com/52113/)).<br><br>Enforcement would stay rights-holder-initiated and by web address, because channel operators cannot be identified, against existing remedies of one-to-ten-year sentences and fines to 25,000 Birr ([the same report](https://www.thereporterethiopia.com/52113/)).<br><br>A draft proclamation ratifying accession to the Berne Convention is before the Council of Ministers, with no parliamentary date published ([the report again](https://www.thereporterethiopia.com/52113/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Gabon | [Loi 025/2021 governs electronic transactions](https://journal-officiel.ga/18190-025-2021-/) and [Loi 026/2018 the electronic communications sector](https://journal-officiel.ga/5055-026-2018-/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[Loi 025/2021 on electronic transactions was gazetted on 28 December 2021](https://journal-officiel.ga/18190-025-2021-/), and [Loi 026/2018 on electronic communications on 17 October 2019](https://journal-officiel.ga/5055-026-2018-/).<br><br>Together with the 2023 cybersecurity and data-protection laws they are the statutory base the later ordinances build on. No implementing texts, enforcement record or case under either is held.</details> | No change, standing statutes the base holds for the first time |
| Gambia | The [Communications Bill 2025](https://standard.gm/lawmakers-back-bill-to-protect-personal-data-in-digital-age/) passed second reading in March 2026 and covers electronic commerce, signatures and cybercrime in one instrument. <details><summary>Full record</summary>It also covers electronic communications and broadcasting, and was referred to National Assembly committees after the second reading on 2 March 2026 ([second reading](https://standard.gm/lawmakers-back-bill-to-protect-personal-data-in-digital-age/)).<br><br>The press union is pressing for repeal of the restrictive media laws around it ([committee stage](https://thepoint.gm/africa/gambia/national-news/lawmakers-engaged-over-communication-bill)).<br><br>No enactment date is held, and until it passes the country has no electronic transactions or electronic signature statute at all.</details> | Advanced |
| Ghana | An [electronic transactions bill](https://digitalpolicyalert.org/blog/data-protection-in-africa-roundup) dated October 2025 would mandate age verification, ban targeted advertising to children and impose duties on very large platforms. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The draft is published on the ministry's site and would replace the 2008 Act, requiring risk assessments and semi-annual transparency reports from very large platforms ([draft bill](https://digitalpolicyalert.org/blog/data-protection-in-africa-roundup)).<br><br>The 2008 Act remains the standing baseline until it is enacted, and no parliamentary date is held.</details> | Advanced |
| Guinea | An [electronic signature decree](https://www.guinee360.com/19/08/2026/guinee-mamadi-doumbouya-officialise-la-signature-electronique/) signed in August 2026 completes a frame that has rested since 2016 on a statute the base can only read in summary. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The 2016 statute frames electronic signature, digital archiving, certification of information systems, consumer information duties in online commerce and the related fraud and forgery offences; the agency publishes it as a 14-page image-only scan with no text layer, so the provisions are its summary rather than the statute's words ([the statute](https://anssi.gov.gn/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/loi_l-2016-035-an_relative_aux_transactions_electroniques.pdf)).<br><br>The 2021 implementing decree gives the telecommunications regulator supervision of electronic transactions including mobile money, with security oversight, data collection, administrative police powers and the power to levy charges on payment traffic; providers must hold a domestic or generic domain, a dedicated site and a secured transaction facility, and price in local currency inclusive of taxes and delivery ([decree](https://anssi.gov.gn/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/DECRET-D-2021-092-PRG-SGG-Copie-2.pdf)).<br><br>The August 2026 decree gives an electronic signature the same probative force as a paper writing where it is authentic, unfalsifiable, non-reusable, unalterable and irrevocable, extends it to administrative, private-sector and person-to-person acts, and imposes duties on certification services; no certification authority is yet accredited on the record ([signature decree](https://www.guinee360.com/19/08/2026/guinee-mamadi-doumbouya-officialise-la-signature-electronique/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Guinea-Bissau | The electronic transactions bill remains in development; the regional layer moved instead, with a [paperless customs directive presented at Bissau](https://ang.gw/uemoa-bissau-acolhe-workshop-de-apresentacao-do-projecto-de-plataforma-eletronica-de-trocas-de-documentos-comerciais-no-espaco-comunitario/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The electronic transactions bill sits on the ledger as in development and no enacted commerce statute is held.<br><br>The movement is regional and sectoral: a [Bissau workshop in April 2026 presented the union's paperless customs directive and a planned trade-document exchange platform](https://ang.gw/uemoa-bissau-acolhe-workshop-de-apresentacao-do-projecto-de-plataforma-eletronica-de-trocas-de-documentos-comerciais-no-espaco-comunitario/). Domestic electronic dealing is happening under tax administration rules rather than commerce law, through the [platform-only value added tax declaration for customs clearance](https://kontaktu.mef.gw/api/public_files/LX4zIpsBZ7DXb_HFUy3A.pdf).</details> | No change |
| Kenya | A [copyright bill](https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/kenyas-new-copyright-bill-good-start-ready-ai-age-zaccheaus-amenya-0zeuf/) and an [intellectual property bill](https://www.pulse.co.ke/story/the-intellectual-property-bill-2026-what-kenyan-bloggers-and-tiktokers-need-to-know-2026081618295613444) are both out for comment, alongside an adopted inter-agency piracy enforcement framework. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The copyright bill would create a copyright authority and a disputes tribunal, and carries the 2001 computer-generated-work authorship rule forward unchanged; its comment window's closing date is not on file ([copyright bill](https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/kenyas-new-copyright-bill-good-start-ready-ai-age-zaccheaus-amenya-0zeuf/)).<br><br>The intellectual property bill would merge the industrial property institute, the copyright board and the anti-counterfeit authority into one regulator with a tribunal, replace the 2001 and 2008 statutes, and set rules distinguishing artificial-intelligence-assisted from artificial-intelligence-generated inventions ([property bill](https://www.pulse.co.ke/story/the-intellectual-property-bill-2026-what-kenyan-bloggers-and-tiktokers-need-to-know-2026081618295613444)).<br><br>An inter-agency digital piracy enforcement framework was adopted on a steering committee's hundred-day report, with the ministry coordinating the copyright board, the regulator, the media council and the tribunal; a national policy on digital piracy is announced as the next step with no draft, drafter or date ([framework](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/31/kenya-unveils-national-framework-to-combat-kes-92-billion-digital-piracy-losses/), [losses](https://itweb.africa/article/piracy-costs-kenya-712m-annually/j5alrvQArJyvpYQk)).</details> | Advanced |
| Lesotho | The [Electronic Transactions and Electronic Communications Bill remains pending promulgation](https://www.gov.ls/download/national-digital-policy-2024-2035/); the movement was in adjacent law. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2024-12-30 — [the National Digital Policy records the 2022 Bill as still pending promulgation](https://www.gov.ls/download/national-digital-policy-2024-2035/).<br><br>2026-03-27 — [electronic invoicing was made mandatory for VAT transactions](https://www.rsl.org.ls/sites/default/files/2026-04/VAT%20-%20E-Invoicing%20Regulations%20No.25%20of%202026%20%283%29_0.pdf), a direct digital-commerce compliance rule made while the general statute waits.<br><br>2026-05-05 — [the Payment System Bill reached second reading](https://www.lena.gov.ls/finance-minister-presents-payment-system-bill/).<br><br>Four years after it was drafted, Lesotho still has no statute recognising electronic transactions generally.</details> | No change, the Bill still pending |
| Liberia | The [2002 Electronic Transactions Law](https://www.wto.org/english/thewto_e/acc_e/lbr_e/WTACCLBR15_LEG_18.pdf) is the only enacted e-commerce statute and predates platforms entirely. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Chapter 13 of Title 14, [approved 8 January 2002 and published 19 June 2002](https://www.wto.org/english/thewto_e/acc_e/lbr_e/WTACCLBR15_LEG_18.pdf), facilitates the use of electronic transactions for commercial purposes.<br><br>Nothing covering online consumer protection, platform obligations or intermediary liability has been enacted in the twenty-four years since. The base holds no e-commerce bill in drafting.</details> | No change |
| Libya | [Law 6 of 2022](https://lawsociety.ly/legislation/%D9%82%D8%A7%D9%86%D9%88%D9%86-%D8%B1%D9%82%D9%85-6-%D9%84%D8%B3%D9%86%D8%A9-2022-%D9%85-%D8%A8%D8%B4%D8%A3%D9%86-%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%85%D8%B9%D8%A7%D9%85%D9%84%D8%A7%D8%AA-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A5%D9%84%D9%83/) covers electronic signatures, contracts and fifteen disclosures to online buyers; no e-commerce act follows it. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [law](https://lawsociety.ly/legislation/%D9%82%D8%A7%D9%86%D9%88%D9%86-%D8%B1%D9%82%D9%85-6-%D9%84%D8%B3%D9%86%D8%A9-2022-%D9%85-%D8%A8%D8%B4%D8%A3%D9%86-%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%85%D8%B9%D8%A7%D9%85%D9%84%D8%A7%D8%AA-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A5%D9%84%D9%83/) enacted in October 2022 is the operative instrument for electronic dealing: signature and contract rules, and fifteen matters a seller must disclose to an online buyer.<br><br>It is not a full electronic commerce regime, and the [national digital agenda](https://www.gia.gov.ly/wp-content/uploads/2026/01/N-A-F-D-09-10-2025.pdf) of October 2025 records the electronic commerce law as still unenacted alongside the data protection law.</details> | No change |
| Madagascar | The [2014 electronic transactions law](https://www.africa-laws.org/Madagascar/Comercial%20law/Loi-n%C2%B02014-024-du-10-12-2014_sur-Les-Transactions-Electroniques.pdf) stands unamended; a separate cybercrime bill was adopted in Council of Ministers in July 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The law of December 2014 governs electronic transactions, contracts and payment across 35 articles ([law](https://www.africa-laws.org/Madagascar/Comercial%20law/Loi-n%C2%B02014-024-du-10-12-2014_sur-Les-Transactions-Electroniques.pdf)); the base holds it from a mirror of the official legal database and only to its first part.<br><br>A regional workshop in September 2025 put data governance and electronic commerce to delegates from the southern African bloc ([workshop](https://newsmada.com/2025/09/23/developpement-du-numerique-focus-sur-la-gouvernance-des-donnees-et-le-commerce/)).<br><br>A draft bill reforming the decade-old cybercrime statute was adopted in Council of Ministers in July 2026 ([bill](https://www.2424.mg/cadre-juridique-le-gouvernement-propose-une-mise-a-jour-de-la-loi-sur-la-lutte-contre-la-cybercriminalite/)). It is a criminal-law instrument and does not amend the transactions law.</details> | No change |
| Malawi | [Consumer-protection regulations were enacted in March 2026](https://malawilii.org/akn/mw/act/gn/2026/27/eng@2026-04-10) above a [2016 e-commerce Part](https://malawilii.org/akn/mw/act/2016/33/eng@2017-12-31) still unamended. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The standing framework is Part V of the [Electronic Transactions and Cyber Security Act, in force since 1 June 2017](https://malawilii.org/akn/mw/act/2016/33/eng@2017-12-31); the capture does not reach that Part, so what it obliges is not readable from the base.<br><br>2025-12 - the government's statutory fiscal policy statement [committed to developing e-trade legislation, regulations, a policy and a strategy](https://www.finance.gov.mw/documents/uploads/2025-12/2026%20EFPS%20FINAL-PRINT%20VERSION.pdf), with no draft, sponsor or date attached.<br><br>2026-04 - the [Competition and Fair Trading Regulations 2026 were enacted as Government Notice 27 of 2026, assented 5 March](https://malawilii.org/akn/mw/act/gn/2026/27/eng@2026-04-10), strengthening the consumer-protection framework that governs online trade. The regulation's own text failed to render at capture.</details> | Advanced |
| Mali | [Law 2016-012 on electronic transactions, exchanges and services](https://sgg-mali.ml/JO/2016/mali-jo-2016-22.pdf) is the instrument, alongside a [consumer protection law of 2015](https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/mli153143.pdf). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The transactions law of 6 May 2016 is held from the official journal and is the footing an electronic signature service would rest on; no such service is in operation on the record ([law](https://sgg-mali.ml/JO/2016/mali-jo-2016-22.pdf)).<br><br>The consumer protection law of 16 July 2015 sits behind the regulator's decisions on telephone credit validity and internet bundle practices, and neither instrument cites the other on the record ([consumer law](https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/mli153143.pdf), [decision](https://sgg-mali.ml/JO/2026/mali-jo-2026-09-2.pdf)).</details> | No change |
| Mauritania | The transport regulator gave ride-hailing applications [fifteen days to bring their authorisations into line](https://lequotidien.mr/?p=40128&lang=fr) in August 2026, warning of suspension. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The communique warned that failure within the deadline could bring legal proceedings including suspension of the activity, with no further notice or warning required, and set the warning against growing use of digital platforms for transport services ([communique](https://lequotidien.mr/?p=40128&lang=fr)).<br><br>No operator is named and no count of non-compliant authorisations is given. It is the base's only record of platform activity being regulated in the country, and it reaches the platforms through transport law rather than through any digital statute.</details> | Advanced |
| Mauritius | A [virtual-asset solicitation rule](https://maurice-info.mu/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/The-Economic-and-Financial-Measures-Miscellaneous-Provisions-Bill.pdf) in the omnibus bill would presume any publicly accessible communication targets Mauritius, and [electronic transactions amendments](https://lexpress.mu/s/face-aux-cyberattaques-le-secteur-postal-prepare-sa-riposte-560993) were referred to in July 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Unlicensed solicitation of investors would be prohibited by any medium, defined to reach targeted or algorithmic advertising, platforms, applications, social media, influencers, affiliates, referral codes, seminars and webinars, with any publicly accessible communication presumed to target the country; the presumption shifts the burden onto an offshore promoter to show it was not soliciting locally ([rule](https://maurice-info.mu/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/The-Economic-and-Financial-Measures-Miscellaneous-Provisions-Bill.pdf)).<br><br>Amendments modernising the digital-commerce framework and consumer protection were referred to by the minister at a postal-sector workshop, with no text, timetable or introduction date published ([amendments](https://lexpress.mu/s/face-aux-cyberattaques-le-secteur-postal-prepare-sa-riposte-560993)).</details> | Advanced |
| Morocco | The [2026 forex rulebook](https://www.oc.gov.ma/sites/default/files/2025-12/Release%20IGOC%202026.pdf) raises the electronic commerce allowance for accredited start-ups to MAD 2m. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Electronic dealing rests on the [trust services law of 2020](https://www.dgssi.gov.ma/fr/loi-ndeg43-20-relative-aux-services-de-confiance-pour-les-transactions), which governs signatures and certification providers and replaced parts of the earlier electronic exchange law.<br><br>The constraint that actually binds cross-border sellers is exchange control, and it loosened: the [2026 general instruction raises the electronic commerce allowance for accredited start-ups to MAD 2m and the individual ceiling to MAD 20,000](https://www.oc.gov.ma/sites/default/files/2025-12/Release%20IGOC%202026.pdf), inside a [256-article consolidated rulebook in force from 1 January](https://www.oc.gov.ma/sites/default/files/2026-01/IGOC%202026_1.pdf).</details> | Advanced |
| Mozambique | The [electronic transactions licensing regime](https://techafricanews.com/2026/06/08/mozambique-issues-first-licenses-to-digital-operators-in-major-regulatory-milestone/) issued its [first 19 licences](https://techafricanews.com/2026/06/08/mozambique-issues-first-licenses-to-digital-operators-in-major-regulatory-milestone/) in June 2026, against about 170 companies under evaluation. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Nineteen licences were issued to digital operators in June 2026 of about 170 registered companies under evaluation ([licences](https://techafricanews.com/2026/06/08/mozambique-issues-first-licenses-to-digital-operators-in-major-regulatory-milestone/)).<br><br>The fee is one national minimum wage plus 1 per cent of revenue annually from the next fiscal year ([regime](https://techafricanews.com/2026/06/08/mozambique-issues-first-licenses-to-digital-operators-in-major-regulatory-milestone/), [diagnostic](https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/099817512092543722/pdf/IDU-7812f059-6c08-413f-af64-c7a8611c88d2.pdf)). The regime had been in force with no licences issued a year earlier, so the movement here is the door opening rather than the rule changing.</details> | Advanced |
| Namibia | The [Electronic Transactions Act 2019](https://www.uneca.org/sites/default/files/ATPC/cp/Digital%20trade%20regulatory%20integration%20-%20Country%20profile%20-%20Namibia-%20ENG.pdf) has been in force since March 2021 and covers signatures, consumer protection and contracts but not personal data or cross-border flow. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Act is the interim instrument law enforcement relies on for cybercrime, with the communications statute and the penal code ([Act](https://itweb.africa/article/namibia-pushes-cyber-security-reforms/mQwkoq6YJWlM3r9A)).<br><br>A regulatory profile records that it does not reach cross-border data flow, data localisation or personal data protection ([profile](https://www.uneca.org/sites/default/files/ATPC/cp/Digital%20trade%20regulatory%20integration%20-%20Country%20profile%20-%20Namibia-%20ENG.pdf)). Company process under the corporate laws reform is routed through it, so it is doing more work than it was written for.</details> | No change |
| Niger | An [arrêté placing commercial Wi-Fi zones under a declaration regime](https://mcnti.gouv.ne/ova_doc/portant-repression-de-la-cybercriminalite-au-niger/) was published in May 2026, on an electronic transactions law from 2019. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [arrêté](https://mcnti.gouv.ne/ova_doc/portant-repression-de-la-cybercriminalite-au-niger/) places commercial Wi-Fi zone provision under a declaration regime with the regulator, caps each provider at five points of sale, bans signal amplifiers and masts, sets an annual fee schedule by geographic zone and gives three months to comply.<br><br>The framing statute is the [2019 law on electronic transactions](https://mcnti.gouv.ne/ova_doc/loi-2019-03-du-30-avril-2019-portant-sur-les-transactions-electroniques-au-niger/), which defines the rights and obligations governing electronic transactions in Niger; its full text is held only as an official landing page, the attachment having resisted capture.<br><br>No consumer-protection rules specific to online sale, and no volume figures for electronic commerce, are on file.</details> | Advanced |
| Nigeria | A [US$222m consumer-protection penalty](https://www.zawya.com/en/economy/africa/nigeria-tests-data-sovereignty-tfwnm5he) is under appeal, [enforcement of new platform rules was paused](https://www.nigeriacommunicationsweek.com.ng/fg-halts-enforcement-of-new-regulations-on-internet-platforms/) in July 2026, and a [local-presence bill](https://www.digitalbusiness.africa/nigeria-souverainete-numerique-le-senateur-ned-nwoko-veut-obliger-les-reseaux-sociaux-a-ouvrir-des-bureaux-dans-le-pays/) went to public consultation. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The penalty was imposed in 2025 and is under appeal, distinct from the data-protection track ([penalty](https://www.zawya.com/en/economy/africa/nigeria-tests-data-sovereignty-tfwnm5he)).<br><br>The minister paused enforcement of new internet-platform rules pending a unified national digital-economy policy framework, convening a joint technical committee of three regulators to resolve overlapping mandates ([pause](https://www.nigeriacommunicationsweek.com.ng/fg-halts-enforcement-of-new-regulations-on-internet-platforms/)).<br><br>An amendment requiring large platforms to maintain physical offices went to a Senate committee hearing and then to public consultation; a coalition backed passage on an investment case while a rights organisation calls for withdrawal over a power it reads as letting the commission prohibit a non-compliant platform's activities after thirty days without adequate judicial safeguards, and thresholds, deadlines, sanctions and appeal routes are still undefined ([bill](https://www.digitalbusiness.africa/nigeria-souverainete-numerique-le-senateur-ned-nwoko-veut-obliger-les-reseaux-sociaux-a-ouvrir-des-bureaux-dans-le-pays/), [hearing](https://www.vanguardngr.com/2026/07/project-sprint-rallies-senate-support-for-data-protection-bill/), [legislation](https://itweb.africa/article/senate-progresses-social-media-legislation/JBwErvn3ogJ76Db2)).</details> | Mixed, a US$222m platform penalty under appeal and a local-presence bill in consultation while enforcement of new platform rules was paused |
| Rwanda | A [competition and consumer protection law](https://www.rica.gov.rw/fileadmin/user_upload/RICA/Publications/Laws/OG_n___Special_of_04.03.2026__Ihiganwa_mu_bucuruzi_no_kurengera_umuguzi__1___1___1_.pdf) enacted in February 2026 carries the first dedicated statutory e-commerce framework in its sixth chapter. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The law enacted on 26 February 2026 repeals the 2012 competition law, and its articles 41 to 48 create enterprise and intermediary obligations, licensing requirements and online-consumer rights ([law](https://www.rica.gov.rw/fileadmin/user_upload/RICA/Publications/Laws/OG_n___Special_of_04.03.2026__Ihiganwa_mu_bucuruzi_no_kurengera_umuguzi__1___1___1_.pdf)).<br><br>Until this, e-commerce was governed only by general contract and consumer provisions; the base holds no earlier dedicated instrument.<br><br>No implementing regulation or licensing register has been published since enactment.</details> | Advanced |
| Senegal | A [bill on a media and social-network regulator](https://techreviewafrica.com/news/1958/senegal-introduces-draft-law-to-establish-a-new-media-regulatory-authority) was presented in January 2025 covering digital platforms and online media, and no source has moved it since. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The draft was presented with the broadcasting regulator and its scope covers digital platforms, social networks and traditional and online media ([bill](https://techreviewafrica.com/news/1958/senegal-introduces-draft-law-to-establish-a-new-media-regulatory-authority)).<br><br>Nothing in the base has moved it since January 2025. A ministry legal adviser separately states on the record that the state has worked on social-media regulation since 2024, which is the first dating of the workstream the base holds and does not name the bill ([workstream](https://lesoleil.sn/actualites/technologie/reseaux-sociaux-et-intelligence-artificielle-luniversite-cheikh-ahmadoul-khadim-ucak-au-chevet-des-defis-communautaires-lies-au-numerique/)).</details> | No change |
| Seychelles | The [Electronic Transactions Act 2001](https://seylii.org/akn/sc/act/2001/8/eng@2018-03-29/source) gives legal effect to electronic records and digital signatures; customs payment rules were digitised in 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The founding statute recognises electronic records, digital signatures and electronic dealings with public authorities; the consolidated text held is current to 29 March 2018 ([Act](https://seylii.org/akn/sc/act/2001/8/eng@2018-03-29/source)), and a 2024 statutory instrument amending its exclusions schedule is not applied to it.<br><br>In April 2026 Cabinet approved customs regulations requiring electronic or bank-transfer payment of dues of R50,000 and above and operationalising the ATA Carnet system ([Cabinet](https://statehouse.gov.sc/news/6938/cabinet-business-wednesday-22nd-april-2026)).<br><br>The statute is twenty-five years old and has not been revisited for platforms, marketplaces or consumer contracting online.</details> | Advanced |
| Sierra Leone | A public-private dialogue was convened to [operationalise the Electronic Transactions Act 2019](https://sierraloaded.sl/news/government-activate-e-transactions-law/), seven years after enactment, and the Act as enacted and gazetted is not held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The dialogue ran over two days in July 2026 with donor support ([dialogue](https://sierraloaded.sl/news/government-activate-e-transactions-law/), [framework](https://libertyonlinetv.com/2026/08/03/koica-itc-partner-with-government-of-sierra-leone-to-strengthen-e-transactions-legal-framework/)).<br><br>The Act as enacted and gazetted is not held; every automated route to the text failed as at August 2026, and what the base has is a record of the instrument rather than its provisions ([record](https://old.sierralii.org/sl/legislation/act/2019/89-0)).<br><br>An Act that needs a dialogue to operationalise it seven years after passage is the clearest instance in this unit of the gap between enactment and effect.</details> | Advanced |
| Somalia | A [national e-commerce strategy](https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/099063026190082191/txt/P176181-84d9770b-853c-4bb8-8e5a-996eac681382.txt) stands at its November 2022 baseline against an April 2028 target, with the lender recording that no strategy exists. <details><summary>Full record</summary>It is a results indicator of the regional digital integration project, and the lender records that no strategy exists in this country, in a neighbour, or at the regional body ([strategy](https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/099063026190082191/txt/P176181-84d9770b-853c-4bb8-8e5a-996eac681382.txt)).<br><br>Three parties to the same project have none between them. That is a regional gap rather than a national one, and it is the only place this base can say so.</details> | No change |
| South Africa | The 2002 electronic transactions statute [is the standing law](https://www.gov.za/sites/default/files/gcis_document/201409/a25-02.pdf), [platform competition guidance was gazetted in February 2026](https://www.gov.za/sites/default/files/gcis_document/202602/54086gon7095.pdf), and [a market inquiry imposed binding remedies on six platform companies](https://www.compcom.co.za/wp-content/uploads/2025/11/CC_MDPMI-Final-Report_Non-Confidential-1.pdf). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[The Electronic Communications and Transactions Act has been in force since 2002 as the legal basis for electronic contracting and data messages](https://www.gov.za/sites/default/files/gcis_document/201409/a25-02.pdf), held as an excerpt, with no amendment or successor bill on file.<br><br>2025-11-13 — [the competition authority's media and digital platforms market inquiry closed with binding remedial actions under section 43D(1) for Google, Meta, Microsoft, OpenAI, TikTok and X, on a finding that 77% of the population take social media as their main online source of news](https://www.compcom.co.za/wp-content/uploads/2025/11/CC_MDPMI-Final-Report_Non-Confidential-1.pdf). [Google's remedies commit up to ZAR 687,500,000 over their five-year term](https://www.compcom.co.za/wp-content/uploads/2025/11/CC_MDPMI-Final-Report_Non-Confidential_Annexure8_Remedial-Actions.pdf).<br><br>2026-02-06 — [final guidance for leading online intermediation platforms was gazetted, naming price-parity clauses, interoperability restrictions, self-preferencing and misuse of competitively sensitive data as the competition risks the commission will act on](https://www.gov.za/sites/default/files/gcis_document/202602/54086gon7095.pdf). It is guidance rather than regulation and no enforcement action under it is on file.<br><br>Beyond the inquiry's remedies, the enforcement on file is a municipal one: three online grocery platforms had premises closed in a health and licensing operation, reported under local government.</details> | Advanced |
| South Sudan | A [national e-commerce strategy](https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/099063026190082191/txt/P176181-84d9770b-853c-4bb8-8e5a-996eac681382.txt) is recorded as non-existent in the regional project's seventh implementation report. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The lender records it as non-existent ([strategy](https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/099063026190082191/txt/P176181-84d9770b-853c-4bb8-8e5a-996eac681382.txt)).<br><br>The same report records the same absence for two other parties to the project, so three parties have none between them.</details> | No change |
| Sudan | A ministerial meeting resolved to [review state-level border-trade protocols](https://sudantribune.net/article/317080) and replace them with a single national legal framework, with revenue protection the stated driver. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The meeting was chaired by the finance minister and resolved to refer the state-to-neighbouring-country protocols to the Council of Ministers, to build one national framework in their place ([meeting](https://sudantribune.net/article/317080)).<br><br>It was paired with the adoption of the national platform as the sole channel for foreign-trade transactions, and the stated driver is revenue protection rather than service delivery.<br><br>No draft, timetable or list of the protocols under review is published.</details> | Advanced |
| Tanzania | The [Electronic Transactions Act was amended by the Finance Act 2026](https://oagmis.oag.go.tz/portal/acts/337/download), and a [review of more than 1,000 laws](https://dailynews.co.tz/govt-to-review-over-1000-laws-to-attract-tech/) was announced with no list, sequencing, responsible body or timetable. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Section 13 of the Act was amended on 30 June 2026; the land, building and motor-vehicle requirement is a substantive obligation in its own right and is not part of the scheduled transaction classes the separate order lists ([amendment](https://oagmis.oag.go.tz/portal/acts/337/download)).<br><br>The thousand-law review was announced in August 2026 with nothing stated about scope, sequencing, responsible body, timetable or consultation route ([review](https://dailynews.co.tz/govt-to-review-over-1000-laws-to-attract-tech/)).<br><br>Distinguishing the Act amendment from the order made under it matters: secondary accounts treat the mandatory-payment regime as regulations, and it is neither.</details> | Advanced |
| Togo | [Rules on mobile commercial offers](https://www.iam.ma/documents/66341/0/Maroc+Telecom+-+Rapport+financier+S1+2026+%282%29.pdf/261de29d-cee8-407f-1e28-dbb3a53f985f) entered into force in April 2026, recorded in one line of an operator parent's half-year financial report. <details><summary>Full record</summary>No instrument number, title or text is held, and the sole account is a single line in a foreign-listed operator's financial filing ([rules](https://www.iam.ma/documents/66341/0/Maroc+Telecom+-+Rapport+financier+S1+2026+%282%29.pdf/261de29d-cee8-407f-1e28-dbb3a53f985f)).<br><br>A rule that changes what operators may offer consumers, evidenced only in the accounts of the company it binds, is worth recording as exactly that. The consumer-rights tour recorded separately is running in the same period with no reference to it on file.</details> | Advanced |
| Uganda | A [Protection of Sovereignty Bill](https://www.newvision.co.ug/category/news/bou-governor-atingi-ego-say-sovereignty-bill-NV_232814_052026) caps foreign funding at about UGX 400m a year above which ministerial approval is required, and the regulator [opened nationwide enforcement against unlicensed public wireless access](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/08/uganda-begins-nationwide-enforcement-against-illegal-public-wi-fi-services/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The bill was assessed technically by the central bank to joint committees in April 2026; enactment is reported but unconfirmed ([bill](https://www.newvision.co.ug/category/news/bou-governor-atingi-ego-say-sovereignty-bill-NV_232814_052026), [editors](https://nilepost.co.ug/news/360765/editors-raise-alarm-over-ai-misuse-journalist-identity-data-protection-gaps)).<br><br>A cap on foreign funding with ministerial approval above it reaches every civil-society body in this ledger — the digital-rights submissions, the awareness campaigns and the climate-information project are all externally funded.<br><br>Nationwide enforcement against unlicensed public wireless access opened on a public notice in July 2026 ([enforcement](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/08/uganda-begins-nationwide-enforcement-against-illegal-public-wi-fi-services/)).</details> | Advanced |
