| Mauritania | A [bill on access to and use of public information](https://rsf.org/fr/mauritanie-rsf-salue-le-projet-de-loi-sur-l-acces-et-l-utilisation-de-l-information-publique) is on the record from June 2026, presented as making good a presidential commitment. <details><summary>Full record</summary>A press-freedom organisation welcomed the bill and asked for strengthened guarantees ([bill](https://rsf.org/fr/mauritanie-rsf-salue-le-projet-de-loi-sur-l-acces-et-l-utilisation-de-l-information-publique)).<br><br>Nothing from the government or parliament on it is on file: no text, tabling date or parliamentary step. An advocacy organisation's welcome is the whole of what the base holds, which is the position this row records.</details> | Advanced |
| Mauritius | A [Freedom of Information Bill](https://lexpress.mu/node/553938) was announced as in preparation in February 2026, and no draft, text or timetable has been published since. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The bill was announced by the data-protection commissioner at the launch of the national data strategy ([announcement](https://lexpress.mu/node/553938)).<br><br>Six months on, nothing has followed. The country has a data-protection statute aligned to the European regulation and no statutory right of access to public information to sit beside it.</details> | Advanced |
| Morocco | [Law 31-13](https://www.oc.gov.ma/sites/default/files/loi%2031-13/1.%20BO%20Loi%2031.13%20en%20francais.pdf) has been in force since 2018 and the commission it created publishes no annual reports, though its own article 22 requires them. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The law was promulgated by dahir in February 2018 and created the access-to-information commission, with article 30 deferring entry into force by a year and proactive-publication duties by a further year ([law](https://www.oc.gov.ma/sites/default/files/loi%2031-13/1.%20BO%20Loi%2031.13%20en%20francais.pdf)).<br><br>The commission publishes no annual reports despite the duty in article 22, so eight years on the base holds the statute, the institution and no measure of requests made, granted or refused under it.</details> | No change |
| Mozambique | [Law 34/2014 remains the operative access-to-information statute](https://cscs.gov.mz/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Lei-de-Direito-a-Informacao.pdf), and the Constitutional Council [struck down 18 provisions letting the regulator monitor traffic and suspend services](https://opais.co.mz/conselho-constitucional-declara-inconstitucionais-normas-do-regulamento-sobre-controlo-do-trafego-de-telecomunicacoes/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-07-29 — the Constitutional Council [declared unconstitutional 18 provisions of Decree 48/2025, which had let the telecommunications regulator monitor traffic, collect user data and suspend services without parliamentary authorisation](https://opais.co.mz/conselho-constitucional-declara-inconstitucionais-normas-do-regulamento-sobre-controlo-do-trafego-de-telecomunicacoes/). It removes the main legal basis for communications restrictions.<br><br>2026-05-27 — the President [promulgated three laws, on social communication, broadcasting and a higher council for social communication, replacing the 1991 press framework and extending to internet-based publications](https://opais.co.mz/pr-promulga-pacote-legislativo-da-comunicacao-social/).<br><br>The statute itself is unmoved: [Law 34/2014 on the right to information, implemented under Decree 35/2015, is still operative](https://cscs.gov.mz/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Lei-de-Direito-a-Informacao.pdf). No count of requests, refusals or appeals is published, so what the right delivers in practice cannot be stated.</details> | Mixed, the founding statute is untouched while a court struck down surveillance powers and a new media package replaced the 1991 framework |
| Namibia | The [Access to Information Act 8 of 2022](http://www.lac.org.na/laws/2022/7986.pdf) is gazetted and not commenced, and no Information Commissioner has been appointed. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Section 86 leaves commencement to a date determined by the minister, no commencement notice is held, and the Act runs to ten Parts and eighty-six sections whose compliance deadlines run from commencement ([Act](http://www.lac.org.na/laws/2022/7986.pdf)).<br><br>Appointment machinery for the Commissioner and Deputy was itself gazetted in December 2024, setting five-year selection committee terms and a five-member quorum, made ahead of the Act's own commencement, so the annual report the Act requires cannot yet exist ([regulations](https://www.lac.org.na/laws/2024/8530.pdf)).</details> | Advanced, still not commenced |
| Niger | The media regulator created in 2025 [suspended nine foreign outlets in May 2026](https://anp.ne/suspension-de-neuf-medias-francais-au-niger-lonc-entend-preserver-la-paix-la-stabilite-et-haut-le-moral-des-fds/), while the 2011 access charter still has no implementing decree. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The president [signed the ordinance creating the national communication observatory on 1 September 2025](https://www.gouv.ne/index.php/90-autres-actualites/861-regulation-des-medias-au-niger-le-chef-de-l-etat-signe-une-ordonnance-creant-l-observatoire-national-de-la-communication), the transition-era media regulator provided for by article 71 of the founding charter and tasked with guaranteeing freedom of information and equitable access to public media.<br><br>That body [suspended nine French outlets on 8 May 2026](https://anp.ne/suspension-de-neuf-medias-francais-au-niger-lonc-entend-preserver-la-paix-la-stabilite-et-haut-le-moral-des-fds/) — including two international broadcasters, a news agency and two magazines — on grounds of public order, social cohesion, institutional stability and the morale of the defence and security forces.<br><br>The statutory right is older and unimplemented: the [2011 charter of access to public information](https://cyrilla.org/api/files/1566209433658g62junbhzjn.pdf) establishes a general right of access and an appeal route, but article 35 leaves its implementing decree to a later act that is not on record.</details> | Regressed |
| Nigeria | Two Federal High Court judgments in the window held the [Act binds an incorporated public company](http://saharareporters.com/sites/default/files/2026-01/12.%20Judgment%20%28HEDA%20v.%20NNPCL%29-min.pdf) and [overrides other disclosure laws](https://mediarightsagenda.org/federal-high-court-rules-that-foi-act-overrides-other-laws-in-journalists-suit-against-nigeria-shippers-council/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>On 13 October 2025 the Federal High Court [held the national oil company remains a public institution subject to the Act despite being a company limited by shares, and ordered disclosure of the terms of a $3 billion crude-for-cash loan](http://saharareporters.com/sites/default/files/2026-01/12.%20Judgment%20%28HEDA%20v.%20NNPCL%29-min.pdf).<br><br>On 24 February 2026 the same court [held the Act overrides other disclosure laws and regulations, ordering a public council to release data-protection policy records](https://mediarightsagenda.org/federal-high-court-rules-that-foi-act-overrides-other-laws-in-journalists-suit-against-nigeria-shippers-council/).<br><br>The statute itself is untouched: the [Act was assented on 28 May 2011](https://nimasa.gov.ng/wp-content/uploads/2026/05/freedom-of-information-act.pdf) and remains in force. What moved is its reach, and by judgment rather than by amendment; no compliance report, request volume or refusal statistic is published.</details> | Advanced |
| Rwanda | The [2013 access law](https://rwandalii.org/akn/rw/act/law/2013/4/eng@2013-03-11/source) is in force and [under revision](https://www.rwandainswitzerland.gov.rw/actualites/info-details/rwanda-participates-in-upr-pre-session-ahead-of-its-4th-review), on the government's own statement to an international review in March 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The founding statute establishes the access right, information-officer duties and monitoring by the ombudsman ([law](https://rwandalii.org/akn/rw/act/law/2013/4/eng@2013-03-11/source)).<br><br>Rwanda confirmed at a pre-session ahead of its fourth periodic review that revision of the access law, alongside the media law, is ongoing ([statement](https://www.rwandainswitzerland.gov.rw/actualites/info-details/rwanda-participates-in-upr-pre-session-ahead-of-its-4th-review)).<br><br>No draft text, timetable or direction of revision is published, so what the revision would change cannot be stated from the base.</details> | Advanced |
| Sao Tome and Principe | There is no access-to-information statute; the [constitution's three relevant articles](https://presidencia.st/constituicao.htm) are the whole of the national guarantee. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Artigo 29 gives everyone the right freely to express and disseminate thought by word, image or any other means; Artigo 74 places on holders of political office the duty to keep citizens informed about public affairs; Artigo 76 provides that absence of publication renders laws legally ineffective ([constitution](https://presidencia.st/constituicao.htm)).<br><br>None of the three creates a request procedure, a deadline, an exemption regime, an appeal or an oversight body ([constitution](https://presidencia.st/constituicao.htm)). Without those, a right to information is a principle rather than a route a citizen can take, and no route is held.</details> | No change |
| Senegal | Senegal's [first access-to-information statute was promulgated in September 2025](https://www.archives.sn/api/fichiers/e05027c9-f6f2-4f70-a313-b0ee81cf2c08), its implementing decree adopted in Council in October. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The law [runs to five chapters and 32 articles: it vests the right in any resident natural person and any legal person established in Senegal, requires a registered written request with acknowledgement, creates a national access commission, sets criminal sanctions, leaves implementing modalities to a decree and abrogates three articles of the 2006 archives law](https://www.archives.sn/api/fichiers/e05027c9-f6f2-4f70-a313-b0ee81cf2c08). Article 17 requires an immediate answer and article 18 allows eight clear days where prior processing is needed.<br><br>The Council of Ministers [adopted the draft decree fixing the commission's organisation and functioning on 29 October 2025](https://primature.sn/publications/conseil-des-ministres/conseil-des-ministres-du-29-octobre-2025), alongside implementing decrees for three companion laws.<br><br>That is adoption of a draft, not publication of the final decree nor installation of the commission, and the base holds no request volume or refusal statistic under the law.</details> | Advanced |
| Seychelles | An [access-to-information reform whitepaper](https://www.infocom.sc/wp-content/uploads/ATIA-Updated-Whitepaper-10.11.2025.pdf) recommends a centralised access unit, alignment with the data-protection Act and open-reuse licensing, with drafting targeted for end-2025. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The whitepaper was published in November 2025 with drafting by the attorney-general targeted for the end of that year ([whitepaper](https://www.infocom.sc/wp-content/uploads/ATIA-Updated-Whitepaper-10.11.2025.pdf)).<br><br>Nothing has entered the base since, so the drafting target has passed with no draft on record. The same body holds both the access and the protection mandates, which is why the whitepaper proposes aligning the two statutes rather than treating them separately.</details> | Advanced |
| Sierra Leone | The [Right to Access Information Act 2013](https://raic.gov.sl/images/downloads/Right-access-information-act-2013.pdf) is in force, and a Bill that would carry the access regime forward [completed national validation](https://moice.gov.sl/moice-concludes-final-national-validation-of-the-data-protection-and-right-to-access-information-bill-2025/) in November 2025. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The 2013 Act creates a right of access, a proactive publication duty with approved publication schemes, a schedule of exemptions and an oversight commission ([Act](https://raic.gov.sl/images/downloads/Right-access-information-act-2013.pdf)).<br><br>The Data Protection and Right to Access Information Bill went through nationwide consultations from September 2025 ([consultations](https://moice.gov.sl/nationwide-consultations-begin-on-sierra-leones-first-data-protection-law/)), a pre-legislative session in October ([session](https://moice.gov.sl/ministry-of-information-holds-pre-legislative-session-on-draft-data-protection-and-right-to-access-information-bill/)) and final national validation in November ([validation](https://moice.gov.sl/moice-concludes-final-national-validation-of-the-data-protection-and-right-to-access-information-bill-2025/)).<br><br>It is not enacted and no parliamentary date is set, so the 2013 Act is the operative instrument throughout.</details> | Advanced |
| Somalia | The constitution has required an access-to-information law [since 2012](https://www.parliament.gov.so/images/Downloads/Dastuurka_ku_meelgaarka_SOM_03092012-1_2.pdf); the bill drafted in March 2024 was [still before the lower house](https://defenddefenders.org/federal-republic-of-somalia-joint-submission-to-the-un-universal-periodic-review-52nd-session-of-the-upr-working-group/) in October 2025. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Article 32 of the Provisional Constitution guarantees access to state-held information and directs Parliament to enact an implementing law ([constitution](https://www.parliament.gov.so/images/Downloads/Dastuurka_ku_meelgaarka_SOM_03092012-1_2.pdf)). Fourteen years on, none is enacted.<br><br>A ministry official told a September 2025 event that Cabinet had passed the law and it awaited presentation to the lower house ([event](https://soma.org.so/soma-commemorates-international-day-for-universal-access-to-information/)).<br><br>A civil-society submission to an international review a month later recorded the March 2024 bill still before the house, criticising its broad national-security and confidentiality exemptions and its lack of independent oversight ([submission](https://defenddefenders.org/federal-republic-of-somalia-joint-submission-to-the-un-universal-periodic-review-52nd-session-of-the-upr-working-group/)).</details> | Stalled |
| South Africa | The regulator issued a [precedent-setting access-to-information enforcement notice against a private body in June 2026](https://www.mondaq.com/southafrica/contracts-and-commercial-law/1797238/landmark-ruling-for-private-bodies-under-paia-information-regulators-enforcement-notice-against-mining-house), the first of its kind on the record. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-06 — the Information Regulator [issued an enforcement notice against a mining company under the access-to-information Act](https://www.mondaq.com/southafrica/contracts-and-commercial-law/1797238/landmark-ruling-for-private-bodies-under-paia-information-regulators-enforcement-notice-against-mining-house), which the account records as precedent-setting for private bodies. Before this window the base held no enforcement notice of any kind.<br><br>It is one notice, not a series. The regulator publishes no enforcement register, and the base holds no figure for requests made, refused or appealed under the Act, so what can be said is that the power was used and not how often it is needed.</details> | Advanced |
| South Sudan | A [technical committee was given fourteen days](https://www.radiotamazuj.org/en/news/article/kiir-forms-technical-committee-to-launch-south-sudan-official-gazette) in January 2026 to launch an Official Gazette and clear a publication backlog of every law since 2011, and no edition is on file. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The committee was formed by the President on 12 January 2026 ([committee](https://www.radiotamazuj.org/en/news/article/kiir-forms-technical-committee-to-launch-south-sudan-official-gazette)).<br><br>A protocol issued the week before classifies all documents bearing the President's signature as privileged executive communication that cannot be photographed, scanned or shared online.<br><br>Those two facts together are the single most consequential entry in this ledger. A country with no operating gazette and a rule against reproducing signed instruments cannot have its laws read, which is why the ICT Authority's status is unresolvable and why the cybercrime Act's text is withheld.</details> | Stalled |
| Sudan | The [Right to Access Information Law of 2015](https://cyrilla.org/api/files/1566991640451x872tchicz.pdf) is in force, and the commission it requires was never effectively established. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The law was ratified by the National Assembly on 27 January 2015 and signed on 22 February. It establishes a citizen right to obtain information from public institutions, and a commission with powers to compel publication ([law](https://cyrilla.org/api/files/1566991640451x872tchicz.pdf)).<br><br>Available reporting indicates the commission was never made operational, so the law's practical reach is unverified.<br><br>Nothing later amends or replaces it, and no implementing regulation was located.</details> | No change |
| Zambia | The 2023 Act gained [its first implementing regulations](https://zambialii.org/akn/zm/act/si/2025/56/eng@2025-08-29/source) and then [commission guidelines translating its duties into operational guidance](https://hrc.org.zm/wp-content/uploads/2025/12/Access-To-Information-Guidelines_2025.pdf). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[Act No. 24 of 2023 came into force on 25 June 2024, granting a citizen or residence-permit holder the right to access information held by an information holder](https://www.parliament.gov.zm/sites/default/files/documents/acts/Act%20No.%2024%20of%202023%2C%20The%20Access%20to%20Information%20Act%2C%202023.pdf).<br><br>2025-08-29 — [Statutory Instrument No. 56 of 2025 supplied the request forms, fees and procedures needed to make the right operative](https://zambialii.org/akn/zm/act/si/2025/56/eng@2025-08-29/source) — the step without which an access statute is unusable.<br><br>2025-12-05 — [the rights commission issued guidelines as Government Notice No. 1624 of 2025, translating the Act's duties into operational guidance](https://hrc.org.zm/wp-content/uploads/2025/12/Access-To-Information-Guidelines_2025.pdf).<br><br>Two years from enactment to a usable procedure is the shape of it. No count of requests made, granted or refused under the Act is published.</details> | Advanced |
| Zimbabwe | The [Freedom of Information Act](https://zimlii.org/akn/zw/act/2020/1/eng@2020-07-01) is in force, and two 2026 High Court rulings pull in opposite directions on what the section 62 right reaches. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Act of 2020, commenced on 1 July 2020, implements the section 62 constitutional right of access to information and repealed the Access to Information and Protection of Privacy Act ([Act](https://zimlii.org/akn/zw/act/2020/1/eng@2020-07-01)).<br><br>On 24 December 2025 the High Court held that withholding the report of the commission of inquiry into urban state land sales violates section 62 and ordered its publication within 90 days ([judgment](https://www.veritaszim.net/sites/veritas_d/files/Markham%20v%20President%20%26%20Anor%20HH-854-2025.pdf)).<br><br>On 17 August 2026 the same court dismissed an application to compel a bank to disclose foreign-currency allocation records, holding that banking secrecy under section 76 of the Banking Act barred disclosure without the central bank's authorisation ([ruling](https://www.newsday.co.zw/amp/local-news/article/200059400/court-backs-fbc-bank-in-forex-disclosure-dispute)).<br><br>Whether the commission report was published inside the 90 days is not held.</details> | Mixed, a court ordering publication under the constitutional right while another read a statutory secrecy provision as limiting it |
